The rest of this task's normalization work remains structural and verifies no invariant on its own: totality over the known kind set is still Plan-006's T1.8 test, and the P0-1 / P2-1 / P2-6-producer / P1-6-producer legs are shape and provenance duties rather than load-bearing properties.
  - **P0-1 — default-branch diagnostic, never silent-drop.** Both normalizers consume the Plan-006 registry `EVENT_DISPOSITION_BY_KIND` (Plan-006 Phase 1's disposition map in `packages/contracts/src/event.ts`, campaign B11 — the code leg lands with Plan-006's Phase 1, and the `### Phase 3` precondition block above machine-gates this consume on that merge) as the single disposition source and mirror its `adopt` / `rename` / `correlate` / `discard` verdicts. Any wire kind that resolves to no registered `SessionEventType` — an unknown kind outside the census **or** an interim `typePending` kind whose literal has not landed — routes to a **typed daemon-diagnostic default branch**: a `DriverDiagnosticRecord` (`{ provider, rawWireType, dispositionReason }`) emitted through the T3.11 `driver-diagnostics.ts` surface onto the daemon diagnostic channel (structured daemon logs + OTel — operator-visible; deliberately NOT named `runtime_node.*`, that prefix is the Spec-006 event namespace and this record is off-timeline by design, Codex round 3), **never a `session_events` envelope and never a silent drop**; the kind moves to a real `eventType` when B18's W3 Spec-006 census amendment lands its literal (the Plan-006 T1.8 interim-disposition seam). The corpus checklist's `rate_limits` → `usage.context_window_update` gloss is **corrected here**: the Codex `rate_limits` account-quota kind maps to `usage.rate_limit_update` (account-quota utilization is not context-window utilization, Design §3.2) — Codex `account/rateLimits/updated` push + `account/rateLimits/read` pull both feed it (C-9). Unknown kinds fail open to this diagnostic; malformed frames fail closed per the boundary rule above; every correlation id copied verbatim.
  - **P2-1 — bounded reorder buffer with a first-class overflow diagnostic.** No global causal-order guarantee across aggregates; per-run order is preserved and tool events pair by `tool_use_id` (normalized into the canonical `payload.toolCallId`, Spec-006 §Required Behavior). The normalize boundary carries a bounded reorder buffer parameterized `{ maxBufferedEvents, pairingTimeoutMs }`; Spec-006 §Required Behavior fixes overflow as **flush-in-arrival-order + a diagnostic** and delegates the buffer parameters to the consuming plan, so Plan-005 names the overflow surface here: on **overflow** it flushes in arrival order **and** emits an **operator-visible daemon-diagnostic-channel record** — a `DriverDiagnosticRecord` through the same `driver-diagnostics.ts` off-timeline surface P0-1 routes to, **never a `session_events` timeline row** — **plus** an OpenTelemetry counter (`driver.reorder_buffer.overflow`, emitted by the same module); the overflow is queryable via those diagnostics/metrics surfaces, not via the event timeline. The overflow is never a silent flush: a silent flush degrades to invisible reordering, so the paired diagnostic + metric is load-bearing, not advisory. A pairing timeout (an unpaired `tool_use_id` past `pairingTimeoutMs`) surfaces the same diagnostic + metric pair.
  - **P2-6-producer — window telemetry.** The driver stamps `windowSource ∈ { provider_reported, model_default, estimated }` and computes `exceeded` at the normalize boundary; the Codex leg subtracts the ~12k-token baseline before deriving `windowUsedTokens`. Counts travel both-or-neither (`windowUsedTokens` / `windowMaxTokens` present together, or a counts-absent update reads as headroom unknown) per the Spec-006 pair rule; the B15 consumer applies the trust map.
  - **P1-6-producer — 3-tier cost resolution + native-cap unpriced provenance.** The driver sets `costStatus ∈ { priced, unpriced }` and `costSource ∈ { provider_reported, derived_exact, derived_family_prefix, unpriced_native_cap }` on `usage.cost_update` (the full four-value Spec-006 §Usage Telemetry (usage_telemetry) enum): (a) provider-emitted cost when the wire supplies it, **sanity-bounded** (non-negative, finite, below the configured absurdity ceiling; gross divergence from a derivable estimate emits a diagnostic) → `provider_reported`; (b) else daemon-derived from token counts × the local per-model-family pricing table with family-prefix fallback → `derived_exact` / `derived_family_prefix`; (c) an owner-admitted **native-cap** run (the C-12 leg, T3.15) is **unpriced by provenance** — no per-update value is derivable, so it emits `costStatus: 'unpriced'`, `costSource: 'unpriced_native_cap'`, `costCents` absent (the USD bound lives on the `run.queued` `admittedUnpricedCapCents`, not on per-update rows); (d) else **fail-closed unpriced** for a genuinely unpriceable model (`usage.budget_warning { reason: 'unpriced-model' }`). A conformance test asserts the native-cap path emits exactly `{ costStatus: 'unpriced', costSource: 'unpriced_native_cap' }` with `costCents` absent. The producer never halts and never branches on `costSource` — the B15 accountant owns the single ceiling (Spec-016 §Budget Policies, B6). The surveyed fail-open zero-cost terminal is deliberately not ported.
  - **Subagent-lifecycle normalization.** Codex `multi_agent` and Claude `SubagentStart` / `SubagentStop` normalize into `subagent.started` / `subagent.completed` (`tool_activity`, provider-attributed, Spec-006 B1), carrying `parent_tool_use_id` copied verbatim so the subagent tree pairs correctly.
  - **`realtime_*` reserved-family handling.** The reserved `realtime_*` census family (Spec-006 B1) has no emitter in V1. The normalizer routes each of the eleven Codex realtime wire kinds — `thread/realtime/started`, `thread/realtime/closed`, `thread/realtime/error`, `thread/realtime/itemAdded`, `thread/realtime/sdp`, `thread/realtime/outputAudio/delta`, `thread/realtime/transcript/delta`, `thread/realtime/transcript/done`, `thread/realtime/item/started`, `thread/realtime/item/transcript/delta`, `thread/realtime/item/completed` (count re-derived eight → eleven at the 2026-08-28 `0.150.1` re-pin, where a full-list set difference against the prior generation shows the three item-scoped names **added beside** the older spellings rather than renaming them — no name was removed; [`codex.md`](../reference/provider-wire/codex.md) §`thread/realtime/*`) — to the default-branch diagnostic (each is gated / `typePending`), and the driver suppresses the family at the source via Codex `optOutNotificationMethods` (C-16) until the R8 realtime gate lifts (R8 leg 7, T3.15). Exact-name suppression is not silent-by-omission: any upstream-added **twelfth** `thread/realtime/*` name the eleven-name suppression list would miss still falls through to the same P0-1 default-branch diagnostic — never silently dropped.
  - **C-4 / C-5 — Claude read-loop + typed retry taxonomy.** The Claude normalizer's read-loop reads to EOF and never breaks on `result` — trailing events (`prompt_suggestion`) can arrive after `result` (C-4); the result subtypes (`success` / `error_max_turns` / `error_max_budget_usd` / `error_during_execution` / `error_max_structured_output_retries`) map into the run-lifecycle + budget taxonomy. Claude `system/api_retry`'s typed error enum (`authentication_failed` / `oauth_org_not_allowed` / `billing_error` / `rate_limit` / `overloaded` / `invalid_request` / `model_not_found` / `server_error` / `max_output_tokens` / `unknown`) normalizes so mid-session `authentication_failed` is detectable during a run (C-5) — it feeds the P3-3 `reauth-required` path (T3.14), not only the P0-5 preflight probe.
  - **Usage-delta accounting — cumulative in, per-turn delta out (2026-08-28).** Both pinned providers report usage as a running total for the provider session, not a figure for the turn just finished, so the pre-existing P1-6-producer / P2-6-producer legs would have emitted the total on every turn and overstated session spend by the conversation's length — measured **22×** on a long thread, landing on the `Spec-016 §Session Cost Receipt` committed-spend fold rather than on a display. `provider/usage-delta-accountant.ts` holds **one base register per provider thread and axis, advanced in stream order** as each declared-cumulative reading is consumed — never a base snapshot copied at turn dispatch, which re-counts interleaved turns against copies of one starting value — and attributes each interval to **the turn the metered frame itself names** (the Codex usage notification carries the naming `turnId`; a usage frame for one turn routinely lands after the next has opened, and named-turn attribution over the stream-ordered base meters that late interval to its own turn instead of flooring it — the bind-at-dispatch discipline the turn-scoped `effectivePrincipal` already imposes on these rows). Base establishment is two-armed per `Spec-005 §Required Behavior`: **fresh — replay-seeded included — bases at zero and the first reading meters in full** (the provider counter starts at zero, seeding spends nothing, and the first turn's large input is real billed spend), while **provider-native resume bases at the daemon's own prior-emitted cumulative sum for that thread**, rebuilt from the canonical record, never at the first post-resume reading — so pre-resume spend is never re-metered and the first post-resume interval meters exactly the new spend. A context compaction does **not** re-base, because the provider's counter is unaffected by it and re-basing there would forgive every pre-boundary token. Where the wire declares a per-turn figure beside the cumulative one (the Codex breakdown's `last`), the accountant consumes it as a **corroborating cross-check** — interval asserted equal to the declared figure for the naming turn, mismatch recorded as a typed diagnostic — never as a substitute metering path. An observed decrease on a declared-cumulative axis is a falsified declaration: the emission floors at zero and the floor-hit lands on the P0-1 diagnostic surface as a typed `DriverDiagnosticRecord`, never as negative spend and never silently. The normalized axes are a **partition** — the input axis is uncached input, with any cached-read component subtracted at this boundary **only where the breakdown's own sum identity confirms containment** (the vendor schema places the cached member beside the input member and proves nothing about nesting); an unconfirmed identity emits the input figure unsubtracted with a failed-identity diagnostic, a conservative overstatement surfaced for repair — and the cache-read / cache-write split is retained on the diagnostic band, since the Spec-006 `usage_telemetry` payload registers no per-cache-axis member and this amendment mints none. Cost derivation (P1-6-producer) prices from the provider's **full breakdown**, never by re-multiplying the normalized axes. **Tests:** a recorded cumulative sequence yields per-turn deltas that re-sum to the newest reading minus the establishment base; an interleaved two-turn sequence (0 → 100 naming turn A → 150 naming turn B) attributes exactly 100 and 50 with no interval counted twice; a fresh session's first reading meters **in full** from base zero, and a replay-seeded session behaves identically (seeding meters nothing, the first turn's reading meters whole); a provider-native resume whose first reading carries the full historical total meters only the excess over the prior-emitted sum, and zero when there is none; a compaction between two readings does not reset the delta; a turn-A usage frame delivered after turn B has opened is attributed to turn A rather than floored or credited to B; a synthetic decrease emits zero **and** a floor-hit diagnostic record; a declared per-turn figure disagreeing with the derived interval records a cross-check diagnostic without substituting; a wire reading whose input figure contains its cached figure produces normalized axes that sum to the uncached total exactly once; and a reading whose breakdown satisfies no containment identity emits unsubtracted with the failed-identity diagnostic.
  - **Child-thread frame routing — fail-closed, carve-out-before-suppress (2026-08-28).** Both pinned providers multiplex child-thread traffic — subagent, review, and compaction threads the provider spawns for itself — over the connection carrying the session's own thread. The normalizer therefore consults `provider/thread-frame-router.ts` **before** any projection decision, and the router's rule is **family-scoped** per `Spec-005 §Required Behavior`: a frame is first classified by its family against the pinned stream-surface census — a thread-scoped family routes by explicit thread identity and only the session's own thread projects; a censused connection- or account-scoped family (Claude `system/api_retry` → `usage.api_retry`, `rate_limits` → `usage.rate_limit_update`, the capability/initialization frames) routes to its registered normalization **without** a thread identity; an unrecognized family carrying no thread identity still quarantines. Thread identities enter the recognized set on the **registration path ahead of the refusal rule**: the session's own thread at establishment, and a child identity from the provider's parent-linked announcement — Codex `thread/started` carrying `parentThreadId` with a subagent `ThreadSourceKind`, Claude `SubagentStart` with `parent_tool_use_id` in the parent's own stream. A frame naming a **present-but-unregistered** thread — child traffic racing its own announcement — enters a **bounded pending-registration hold**, released into ordinary routing when the registration lands and shed with a recorded diagnostic on a declared timeout; a frame whose identity is **absent where its family requires one, or unrecognized by any registration path, is quarantined and reported**, never projected and never attributed to the parent by arrival order — the quarantine being a **bounded** diagnostic buffer (a declared cap, oldest-first shedding, each admission and each shed a `DriverDiagnosticRecord`), not a delivery queue, and distinct in semantics from the pending hold. Two carve-outs apply **ahead of** suppression. Usage counters are carved out first, so child content is dropped while child spend is not: provider-attributed subagent spend rides the already-registered (`runId`, `provider`, `subagentId`) triple the Subagent-lifecycle-normalization leg above already populates, and spend on a provider-internal child with no subagent identity (a compaction or memory-consolidation thread) attributes to the **parent run at run scope**, which is what it is. Neither arm invents a subagent and neither drops a token. Second, a child thread's **interactive requests** — tool-approval, permission, and input requests raised from inside a child — are carved out and routed through the same dispatch and approval pipeline as the parent run's, answered on the child's own correlation identity (mandatory per `Spec-016 §Provider-Native Subagents`: a subagent introduces no separate trust surface, and suppressing the request would hang the child mid-tool-call rather than hide it); only the child's **transcript projection** is suppressed. Child **lifecycle** still reaches the timeline only as `subagent.started` / `subagent.completed`; a child thread's own frames are never a second lifecycle channel. **Tests:** a foreign-thread assistant-output frame is suppressed and leaves a diagnostic; a censused connection-scoped frame (`system/api_retry`, `rate_limits`) routes to its normalization with no thread identity and is neither quarantined nor held; an unrecognized-family frame with no thread identity is quarantined rather than presumed connection-scoped; a thread-scoped frame with no thread identity is quarantined rather than projected, and quarantine admissions past the declared cap shed oldest-first; a child frame arriving **before** its parent-linked announcement is held and then released into ordinary routing when the announcement lands, and the hold's timeout sheds with a recorded diagnostic; a child identity announced with no recognized parent linkage does not register; a child-thread usage frame lands on the child's attribution and **never** on the parent's rows; a compaction-child usage frame lands on the parent run at run scope with no `subagentId`; a child-raised interactive request reaches the dispatch/approval pipeline and its answer returns on the child's own correlation while the child's content frames stay suppressed; and the `subagent.started` / `subagent.completed` pair is still emitted for a child whose content frames were suppressed.

- **T3.12 — Capability declaration + CLI-version floor + refresh cadence (EXTENDs T3.3 Codex + T3.8 Claude, campaign B10).**
  - Files: `packages/runtime-daemon/src/provider/drivers/codex/capabilities.ts` (EXTEND T3.3); `packages/runtime-daemon/src/provider/drivers/claude/capabilities.ts` (EXTEND T3.8); `packages/runtime-daemon/src/provider/capability-refresh.ts` (new — the daemon-side `CapabilityRefreshScheduler` poll-lifecycle owner)
  - **Spec coverage:** Spec-005 §Per-Driver Capability Matrix (seventeen flags + mechanism grades); Spec-005 §Required Behavior (semver floor at capability fetch; undeclared = unsupported); Spec-005 §Provider Parameter Vocabularies (effort vocabularies + per-turn overrides); Spec-005 §Resolved Questions and V1 Scope Decisions (refresh cadence)
  - **Verifies invariant:** I-005-2
  - **P0-2 — CLI version floor.** `getCapabilities()` reports carry the mandatory `cliVersion` (`DriverCliVersionReport { raw, semver }`); an unparseable version fails closed (`driver.cli_version_unparseable`), a parseable below-floor version refuses attach/refresh (`driver.cli_version_below_floor`). Floors pin by version anchor against the pair Spec-005 §Required Behavior ratifies (Claude Code `2.1.234`, codex-cli `0.141.0`), never fabricated and never copied from a reference file's pin — **T3.23 re-points this gate's version source** from a reported string to the in-band reading of the spawned process.
  - **P2-9 — refresh cadence + named lifecycle owner.** A daemon-side `CapabilityRefreshScheduler` (`provider/capability-refresh.ts`) owns the poll lifecycle — the finding was that the cadence was promised with no file that starts or stops it, so capability / CLI-floor / auth state could stay stale indefinitely. It is **started on runtime-node attach and stopped on detach** (driven by the daemon provider subsystem / `ProviderRegistry` node lifecycle — a sanctioned wiring call, claiming no bootstrap-file ownership), holds a per-runtime-node timer polling `getCapabilities()` **paired with the zero-turn `probeAuth()`** (auth state is NOT on `GetCapabilitiesResult` — capabilities/tools/`cliVersion` only — so a capabilities-only poll would leave admission auth state stale after a post-attach logout, Codex round 5) on a 15-minute bounded cadence (Spec-005 §Resolved Questions and V1 Scope Decisions) with optional provider push where available, drives the T2.4 writer **and refreshes the daemon's per-driver auth-state record that run admission consumes (`driver.not_authenticated`)** — a post-attach logout surfaces within one cadence period; mid-run credential expiry stays C-5's live-signal path (T3.14 P3-3) — and is torn down at daemon shutdown so no timer leaks. **Change-detected emission (Codex round 6):** a poll emits `runtime_node.capability_updated` (CP-005-5 — no new event type) only when the reconstructed capability snapshot (flags / tools / `contractVersion` / `cliVersion`) actually differs from the cached rows — the event payload carries only previous/new capability snapshots, so a no-op poll or an auth-only change appends nothing to the timeline (the auth-state record updates out-of-band of the event surface; unconditional 15-minute emissions would be false timeline changes waking every cache/timeline consumer each cadence). A cadence test asserts the poll fires on schedule with the paired probe, a changed snapshot emits `runtime_node.capability_updated`, a no-op poll and an auth-only change emit nothing, and the scheduler clears its timer on shutdown/detach.
  - **The six campaign flags + refusal tests.** Both drivers declare the six campaign rows to match the Spec-005 matrix — `structured_output: true` (both), `rollback: true` (both), `session_goals: true` (both), `callback_tools: true` (both), `subagents: true` (both), and `cost_cap` (**Claude `true`** via `--max-budget-usd`; **Codex `false`** — no native cap at the pin, fail-closed for native-cap admissions per Spec-016's capless-leg rule). A refusal test asserts a `cost_cap`-gated admission against Codex refuses statically.
  - **`structured_output` mechanism legs + conformance.** Both drivers map the T1.8 normalized `outputSchema` parameter to their provider mechanism: Claude `--json-schema` (its `error_max_structured_output_retries` result subtype is already in the C-4 census, so a retry-exhausted structured-output run normalizes into the run-lifecycle + budget taxonomy) and Codex `turn/start.outputSchema`. A conformance test per driver asserts the schema round-trips to the provider flag and that a schema-violating final output surfaces the provider's structured-output failure (not a silent unconstrained result). Named consumers: Spec-024 dispatch results and Plan-016 orchestration reads (Spec-005 §Per-Driver Capability Matrix).
  - **C-8 — current model families + effort vocab + per-turn overrides.** The tier-(b) pricing-table families and the P0-2 floors carry the current catalogs (Claude Sonnet 5 / Fable 5; Codex gpt-5.5 / gpt-5.4(-mini) / gpt-5.3-codex-spark). The matrix records per-provider effort vocabularies (Claude `low | medium | high | max`; Codex `minimal | low | medium | high | xhigh`, no `max`, per-model level lists on `ProviderModel.effortLevels`); Codex accepts per-turn model/effort/sandbox/approval overrides (`turn/start` params) where Claude binds per session at spawn.
  - **C-16 — Codex `initialize` capability set.** The Codex driver's `initialize` names its capability set (`experimentalApi` gate, `requestAttestation`) and uses `optOutNotificationMethods` (exact-name suppression) as the noise-control lever for unconsumed families (the reserved `realtime_*` suppression, T3.11 / T3.15 leg 7).
  - **Regenerate-bindings rule.** The Codex driver's protocol bindings are regenerated from the pinned binary (`codex app-server generate-ts` / `generate-json-schema`) and **never hand-transcribed** (one docs hallucination was confirmed during verification, Design §3.4); the generated schema is canonical over prose docs.

- **T3.13 — Tool metadata + MCP idempotency floor + MCP server-status census (EXTENDs T3.4 Codex + T3.9 Claude, campaign B10).**
  - Files: `packages/runtime-daemon/src/provider/drivers/codex/tools.ts` (EXTEND T3.4); `packages/runtime-daemon/src/provider/drivers/claude/tools.ts` (EXTEND T3.9). The `command_receipts.mcp_task_id` migration and its canonical-doc mirrors are deliberately **NOT in this task's Files** — they re-homed to **T5.1 (the gated Phase 5 below, Codex round 3)**: this task authors the driver-side receipt-write seam **dormant** (structured to record the handle at task-augmented dispatch, but writing nothing until the column exists), and T5.1 lands the ALTER + activates the seam once Plan-004 Phase 1's `command_receipts` CREATE has shipped
  - **Spec coverage:** Spec-005 §Tool Metadata (MCP-discovered tool always `manual_reconcile_only`; class never derived from annotations; the durable-task-handle carve-out); Spec-005 §Recovery Consequences (receipt recovery dispatched on `idempotency_class`)
  - **Verifies invariant:** I-005-3
  - **P2-7 — MCP floor + load-bearing negative test.** An MCP-discovered tool is **always** `manual_reconcile_only`; the runtime MUST NOT derive `idempotency_class` from MCP `ToolAnnotations` (MCP 2025-11-25 binds clients to treat annotations as untrusted unless from trusted servers, MUST-strength — the MUST binds trust classification, not behavior derivation, so classes bind only on the operator-governed assignment surface — Spec-028 §Tool-Level Overrides, landed 2026-07-22 via B18; the assignment overlay is Plan-028's per its CP-028-2). The **load-bearing negative test** asserts that a driver surfacing an MCP tool advertising `readOnlyHint` / `idempotentHint` still persists `manual_reconcile_only` — annotation self-claims never upgrade the class. **Durable task-handle persistence (Part-B carve-out (a), 2026-07-17):** where an MCP call is dispatched task-augmented under the experimental MCP 2025-11-25 Tasks utility, the receipt durably records the **receiver-generated `taskId` from the `CreateTaskResult` acceptance response** in the additive nullable `command_receipts.mcp_task_id` column (the id exists only once the receiver accepts, so a crash before that response is durably stored leaves the column NULL and the call stays on the floor's halt). That column is the durable handle Spec-015's recovery dispatch polls: instead of halting, recovery reads `mcp_task_id` and polls `tasks/get` to terminal status + `tasks/result` retrieval (result retrieval, never re-execution); a purged/expired or `input_required` task falls back to the halt with the handle preserved. This refines recovery dispatch without weakening the floor — the trust rule is untouched. **Phase split (Codex round 3):** this task ships the seam **dormant** — no `mcp_task_id` write, pre-Phase-5 recovery stays on the `manual_reconcile_only` halt — because the column's ALTER needs Plan-004 Phase 1's `command_receipts` CREATE as substrate; the migration, the canonical-doc mirrors (`local-sqlite-schema.md` column + CHECK, the `api-payload-contracts.md` durable-handle note at the two-phase command-receipt protocol), and the seam's activation are T5.1's (the gated Phase 5 below; the column an additive EXTEND on Plan-004's `command_receipts`, dep-map §1, never a Plan-004 migration edit).
  - **P2-10-L1 — runtime MCP discovery census + live server-status ingest.** The driver produces a per-session MCP **server** inventory at init (name + status) plus status-change updates, unified into one enum `unknown | starting | connected | needs-auth | failed` — **servers only, never a per-server tool-list assumption** (support is not visibility, Spec-005 §Per-Driver Capability Matrix). Claude leg: the `claude mcp list` zero-billed-turn probe; Codex leg: `mcpServerStatus/list`. This per-session status feed is a driver **census/telemetry surface** (no new persisted table, no new CREATE owner — DO disposition). The driver emits it through the **typed producer seam** `onMcpServerStatus` (the daemon-injected `McpServerStatusProducer`, T1.8 / api-payload-contracts.md) as typed `McpServerStatusEmission` values — `serverName` `wireFreeFormString`-bounded at this normalization seam before it reaches the producer (untrusted provider/CLI output, Codex round 5) — which the pre-bound closure stamps into the consumer-facing `McpServerStatusUpdate` (leg identity daemon-supplied, never driver-supplied; T1.8 seam bullet); the finding was that the feed had no contract carrier at all. Producer-only: the emission seam lands here; the **consumer** (status-and-health lane) is Plan-028's `McpStatusNormalizer` (Spec-028/B18 landed 2026-07-22; Plan-028 CP-028-2) — consumer semantics live there, none are authored here (producer landed, consumer registered).
  - **C-1 — ephemeral `--mcp-config` as the primary Claude scoping mechanism.** Per-session MCP scoping is delivered by an ephemeral `--mcp-config` relaunch (the documented Claude launch-time mechanism) as the **primary** path; the live `mcp_set_servers` reconcile exists upstream at CLI ≥ `2.1.210` / SDK ≥ `0.3.166`, streaming-input mode only (Spec-028 §Provider Capability Model, B18 2026-07-22). **Its version gate has collapsed (2026-08-26):** `2.1.210` sits below the ratified admission floor `2.1.234`, so the reconcile is upstream-available on every admitted Claude build, and the driver already runs in streaming-input mode because `--replay-user-messages` requires it — both stated premises of the deferral are met. Availability is still not established by that arithmetic — but it is **not established by a driver probe either**, and this is the sharper consequence of the same measurement. The probe that would establish it sent an **empty** desired set, which shows the subtype dispatches and returns the documented reconcile envelope; it does not show a non-empty mutation lands, and issuing it against a live session would _clear that session's servers_, because the operation replaces the full named-server set. So the flag fails the non-mutating admissibility conjunct, T3.24 declares it non-probeable and reports it `static`, and its live value is established by T28.3.2's own full-desired-set reconcile — run against the server set it is about to install, where the replacement is the intended effect rather than a side effect. The leg stays Plan-028-owned under CP-005-11. Reachability remains separately true and separately relevant (Verified 2026-08-26: the control request answers `success` at `2.1.234`, `2.1.245`, and `2.1.246`, and a cloud-hosted session refuses control requests a local one answers). This plan's own per-session scoping stays the ephemeral `--mcp-config` path — a scoping decision, not a version one.

- **T3.14 — Lifecycle + intervention + resume enrichment (EXTENDs T3.1/T3.2 Codex + T3.6/T3.7 Claude, campaign B10).**
  - Files: `packages/runtime-daemon/src/provider/drivers/codex/{lifecycle,intervention}.ts` (EXTEND T3.1/T3.2); `packages/runtime-daemon/src/provider/drivers/claude/{lifecycle,intervention}.ts` (EXTEND T3.6/T3.7); the two `event-normalizer.ts` (EXTEND T3.5 Codex + T3.10 Claude — the sole terminal-emission boundary the lifecycle legs signal into, per P1-1 / P1-2)
  - **Spec coverage:** Spec-005 §Required Behavior (spawn-env hygiene, intended-close, at-most-once terminal, interactive-request mechanisms, idempotency-key ride-through); Spec-005 §Fallback Behavior (resume position-compare, resume-failure taxonomy, `RecoveryCondition`); Spec-006 §Run Lifecycle (run_lifecycle) (at-most-once terminal per `(runId, runVersion)`)
  - **Verifies invariant:** I-005-5 (resume-failure surfaces `recovery-needed`, no silent replacement)
  - **P0-4 — spawn-env hygiene.** The child environment is **constructed, never inherited** — the daemon-side primary layer of Plan-012's I-012-20 (campaign B13 PR, Codex round 5): every provider child process both lifecycle modules spawn — first spawn and every resume relaunch alike (C-12: resume is a fresh spawn) — receives exactly `{...curatedBase, ...runProvisionedVars}` with the effective credential policy's `denyEnvVars` stripped at that construction; no code path spreads the ambient daemon `process.env` into a child, and the spawn fixtures assert a canary daemon variable and a denied variable are absent from both drivers' child environments. Beneath it, the provider-native defense-in-depth: the Claude leg strips `CLAUDE_*` / `CLAUDECODE*` and supplies config via `--settings`, never `~/.claude`; the Codex leg enforces the allow-list natively with `shell_environment_policy` `inherit="none"` + explicit `set` (C-7).
  - **P0-5 — zero-turn auth probe.** `probeAuth` (required of every driver, not capability-gated — T1.8) detects a logged-out provider before a turn is spent (`DriverAuthProbeResult`, `.strict()`; `driver.not_authenticated`). Codex leg cites the current probe surfaces `codex login status` / legacy `getAuthStatus` / `codex doctor --json` (redacted machine-readable health) (C-17); Claude leg uses its auth-state signal.
  - **P1-1 — intended-close discriminator.** The `event-normalizer.ts` is the **sole terminal-emission boundary** (T3.5 / T3.10 own the provider-native → `run_lifecycle` mapping): daemon-initiated `closeSession` signals intended-close state into the normalizer via the lifecycle module, and the **normalizer stamps** the `intendedClose` flag on the terminal payload so a clean shutdown is not misclassified as a crash by the recovery classifier (Spec-006 B1). Scoping this to the lifecycle module alone would leave it unimplementable — the module that owns the terminal frame must stamp the flag.
  - **P1-2-driver — duplicate-terminal suppression.** The **normalizer** (the terminal-emission boundary) suppresses a second terminal `run_lifecycle` frame for the same `(runId, runVersion)` epoch — the driver-side leg of the at-most-once-terminal property whose primary guard is Plan-004 T3.7 and whose schema backstop is Plan-006's partial unique index (Spec-006 §Run Lifecycle (run_lifecycle)); without this boundary-level suppression a duplicate frame would hit that fail-loud unique index.
  - **P1-4-driver — interactive-request mechanisms (two-provider).** Claude leg: the `--permission-prompt-tool` stdio round-trip (`can_use_tool`: `{ tool_name, input }` → `{ behavior: allow | deny, updatedInput?, message? }`, C-2) + ephemeral `--mcp-config`; Codex leg: native `item/tool/requestUserInput` + `mcpServer/elicitation/request` (C-3) for structured input, plus the approval server requests `item/commandExecution/requestApproval` / `item/fileChange/requestApproval` / `item/permissions/requestApproval` + legacy `execCommandApproval` / `applyPatchApproval` for permission asks (the pinned Codex wire reference's RPC catalog — campaign B13 PR, Codex round 5). Both surface as the four `driver_ask.*` types (Spec-006 B1) that the B13 T2.8 normalizer routes into the Cedar pipeline (CP-005-7). Retraction legs: neither pinned wire reference documents a native-ask retraction signal. At the MCP protocol layer, cancellation of an in-flight request by its issuer is a documented optional capability — `notifications/cancelled`, which the receiver MAY ignore ([MCP specification 2025-11-25 — Cancellation](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2025-11-25/basic/utilities/cancellation)) — so an in-flight `elicitation/create` input ask is protocol-cancellable; whether the Codex app-server boundary forwards such a cancellation across `mcpServer/elicitation/request` is undocumented — treat **every** retraction leg as unconfirmed until verified against the live binary at Phase-3 fixture time. A mechanism without a confirmed leg never fires Plan-012 T2.8's live-leg cancel ingress, its asks settling by every remaining passage: timer, answer, or a non-retraction cancel cause — run end, session close, rollback rewind, resume-supersede per `Spec-006 §Driver Ask Events`' cause row (campaign W2.5).
  - **P1-5 — resume emission + identical-cwd + deterministic session binding.** The `resumed` variant carries the normalized `sessionPosition`; the driver resumes from the **identical worktree cwd** (Claude resume silently returns a _fresh_ session on cwd mismatch — a failure mode the Spec-015 B5 position-compare catches and HALTs on, never silently accepted) and pins the session deterministically via `--session-id` / `--fork-session` (C-14) instead of parsing from init where available.
  - **P0-3 — client idempotency-key ride-through.** The driver threads the requester-generated `clientIdempotencyKey` through `applyIntervention` to the wire unchanged, so at-least-once delivery becomes exactly-once application via the `interventions` `UNIQUE(target_run_id, client_idempotency_key)` guard (the Plan-004 T1.7 accessor stays untouched).
  - **P3-1 — ambiguous intervention status.** The four intervention-status surfaces map to a single normalized outcome — an ambiguous provider ack never silently reads as success.
  - **P3-3 — resume-failure taxonomy.** The `failed` resume variant structurally omits `bindingId`; failure surfaces the closed `RecoveryCondition` — `recovery-needed` (generic) or `reauth-required` (mid-run credential/session expiry detected via C-5's `authentication_failed`, or at resume/probe) — routed by Plan-015's recovery dispatcher onto the existing `run.failed` event (no separate driver event, CP-005-5). The schema-side widening this leg emits through is T1.8's `DriverResumeResultSchema` failed-variant re-type to the two-value `RecoveryCondition` (contract-first — a driver emitting `reauth-required` against the T1.6 single-literal schema would dead-letter at parse, Codex round 3). The driver MUST NOT silently create a replacement session (I-005-5). The resume path also re-realizes the full spawn-bound surface from `runtime_bindings.spawn_config` (read through T2.6's typed `spawnConfig` accessor) + fresh function-leg injection per T1.8's resume re-realization bullet (Codex rounds 3–5).
  - **Frame routing is consumed here, not owned here (2026-08-28).** P1-1 makes `event-normalizer.ts` the sole terminal-emission boundary, which makes it the last place a foreign-thread frame could be caught — and exactly for that reason it is not where the catching is decided. The thread-identity registry, the fail-closed routing decision, and the bounded quarantine are **T3.11's**, in `provider/thread-frame-router.ts`; this task's boundary **consumes** that decision, so a terminal frame is emitted only for the session's own thread and a child thread's terminal never settles the parent's run. Stated explicitly because the two tasks touch the same two files: T3.14 adds no routing logic of its own, and a second routing decision at the terminal boundary would be a second source of truth for whose stream a frame came from.

- **T3.15 — R8 parity driver legs (Codex + Claude, campaign B10).**
  - Files: `packages/runtime-daemon/src/provider/drivers/codex/{lifecycle,intervention,capabilities}.ts` + `packages/runtime-daemon/src/provider/drivers/claude/{lifecycle,intervention,capabilities}.ts` (EXTEND); `packages/runtime-daemon/src/provider/drivers/codex/index.ts` (EXTEND T3.1 — transport selection at driver construction, leg 6); `packages/runtime-daemon/src/provider/callback-tool-host.ts` (new — the daemon-side callback-tool dispatcher, leg 3); driver `__fixtures__/`
  - **Spec coverage:** Spec-005 §Interfaces And Contracts (the seven parity legs); Spec-005 §Parity Capability Mechanism Grades (per-leg mechanism grades); Spec-012 §Required Behavior (execution-posture authorization + `approvalsReviewer` pin, B20); Spec-016 §Provider-Native Subagents (single-supervisor invariant, B6)
  - **Verifies invariant:** I-005-2 (each leg capability-gated), I-005-4 (unsupported ⇒ `degraded`)
  - The **seven R8 parity driver legs**, each recorded at its mechanism grade on the parity matrix (Spec-005 §Parity Capability Mechanism Grades):
    1. **Rollback** (`rollbackTo`) — Codex `thread/fork` at an inclusive `lastTurnId` boundary (native; **rebound from `thread/rollback` 2026-08-26** — that method carries its own deprecation in the generated type, so no handler for it is registered. A fork mints a **new thread**, so this leg re-points the run's live provider binding at the forked thread in the same operation and leaves the pre-rewind thread intact and unreferenced; the daemon's absolute `targetPosition` therefore resolves to a boundary turn id rather than to a drop-count, and a retried `rollbackTo` re-forks from the same boundary rather than compounding a drop. **Because the fork mints a binding, this arm reports `DriverRollbackResult.applied.bindingId` exactly as the Claude arm does** — asserted by a leg test on **both** V1 arms, since the surrogate is the daemon's only channel for the new thread and an `applied` result without it leaves the run bound to a thread the store never recorded, so it is not `resumeSession`-able across a restart, `api-payload-contracts.md §Plan-005 — Provider Driver Contract (Internal Interface)`); Claude `--resume-session-at <message-uuid>` + `--fork-session` (native composed), the driver running with `--replay-user-messages` so message-UUID rewind targets appear on the wire, resuming from the **identical worktree cwd** (T3.14 P1-5). Claude's own `--rewind-files` checkpointing is **not** relied on (Write/Edit-only coverage — the git leg is strictly richer and provider-uniform); file-state restore is the daemon turn-snapshot leg ([Plan-010](../plans/010-worktree-lifecycle-and-execution-modes.md)), never the driver's.
    2. **Session goals** (`setSessionGoal` / `clearSessionGoal`) — Codex `thread/goal/set` (native); Claude daemon-stored goal + system-prompt-append composition on turn/resume (emulated — Claude exposes session name/tag/`getSessionInfo` but no arbitrary metadata store).
    3. **Callback-tool hosting** — Codex `dynamicTools` + the `item/tool/call` server-request handler (native); Claude a daemon-hosted ephemeral MCP server via `--mcp-config` (native, MCP-shaped; tools surface as `mcp__<server>__<tool>`, the `--permission-prompt-tool` plumbing generalized). **Dispatch path (typed, never unanswered).** The driver translates the provider wire request into a `CallbackToolInvocation` and invokes the daemon-injected `onCallbackToolCall` dispatcher (T1.8), then answers the provider request with the returned `CallbackToolResult` — no invocation is left unanswered and no approval bypass is invented. The daemon-side dispatcher is Plan-005's **callback-tool host** (`provider/callback-tool-host.ts` — the component that runs Claude's ephemeral MCP server): it routes every invocation through Plan-012's approval pipeline via the **Plan-012-registered evaluation seam** — canonical `approval.requestCreate`-shaped inputs into the composed `check()`, evaluate-first per Plan-012 T2.10/D-012-18: a policy allow or valid remembered rule completes with **no minted request**, only an ask-policy outcome mints one, and a Cedar deny refuses outright — never a bare create that would force every invocation to a human prompt (CP-005-7 covers driver-host permission callbacks; the B13 T2.8 normalizer; wording aligned Codex round 4 of the B13 PR), Cedar-governed identically to provider tools (B20) and **authoring no Plan-012 symbols**, and the outcome lands as an ordinary `tool_activity` row. Tests: an allow round-trip (invocation → approved → `completed` answered to the provider) and a deny round-trip (invocation → denied → `denied`), each asserting the outcome lands as `tool_activity`. **Fail-closed availability (Codex round 5).** The Cedar route is a Tier-6 consumer seam (Plan-012/B13), so its absence is a live deployment state Phase 3 must handle without serializing behind Tier 6: while the daemon has no registered Plan-012 evaluation seam, spawn **withholds the `callbackTools` registry** (the leg-4 disabled-at-spawn pattern — tools never exposed, so the agent wastes no turns invoking them) and the host's runtime backstop answers any stray invocation `denied` plus a `DriverDiagnosticRecord` (the T3.11 surface) — **never `completed` without Cedar, never unanswered, never a Phase-3 machine gate on Plan-012**. The allow path activates when Plan-012's seam registers (CP-005-7). Tests: a no-seam spawn injects no `callbackTools`; a stray invocation against a seamless host answers `denied` with the diagnostic.
    4. **`subagentPolicy` pass-through** — Claude `--agents` `AgentDefinition`s (per-subagent model/tools/permissionMode/effort/maxTurns; depth cap 5); Codex `[agents]` config. `maxConcurrent` enforces natively on Codex and is **boundary-serialized on Claude** (beyond-cap subagents hold at the daemon tool-call boundary until a slot frees — concurrency cap docs-silent on Claude); subagent lifecycle normalizes via T3.11; budget roll-up is run-scoped (single-supervisor invariant, no escape hatch, Spec-016 B6). **Fail-closed at spawn (matrix requirement).** A subagent definition the daemon cannot boundary-mediate — a leg where the beyond-cap tool-call interception cannot be interposed — is **disabled at spawn** (withheld from `--agents`, or `enabled: false`) rather than admitted unenforceable: the single-supervisor invariant is never traded for coverage. Enforcement stays observability-only (never a failed run; a breach surfaces a diagnostic), mirroring the Claude cell of Spec-005 §Parity Capability Mechanism Grades ("subagents disabled at spawn when the leg cannot mediate tool calls") and Spec-016 §Provider-Native Subagents.
    5. **`executionPosture` mapping** (per B20 semantics) — Claude sandbox via `--settings`: `sandbox.enabled` + `failIfUnavailable: true` (fail-closed) + `allowUnsandboxedCommands: false`, `filesystem.allowWrite` ↔ `writableRoots`, `network.allowedDomains` ↔ `networkAccess`, plus the daemon-curated credential deny-list (Claude ships none) mirrored into permission-rule `Read` denies + env scrub (native-partial, Bash-scoped); Codex sandbox modes + `[permissions.<name>]` profiles + per-turn `sandboxPolicy` (native). The complete effective posture object is stamped on `run.running` (post-setup-gate spawn success); `profileName` resolves to daemon-side presets (provider-uniform).
    6. **Transport axis** — Codex `stdio` default; `unix://` / `ws://` app-server listeners config-gated with bearer auth. **Config resolution (`DriverTransportConfig` is consumed, not dead config).** The Codex driver **entrypoint** (`drivers/codex/index.ts`) selects the transport from `DriverTransportConfig` **at driver construction**: `stdio` when the config is absent (the V1 default), else `unix-socket` / `websocket` per the discriminated shape; `bearerTokenRef` is resolved through daemon config to the actual credential **at connection time, never inlined** (the ref-not-value pattern). An authenticated-listener startup test asserts a config-gated `unix://` / `ws://` listener starts with bearer auth and that an unauthenticated connection is refused. Claude has no local listener — remote Claude is Spec-024 cross-node dispatch, recorded as the parity mechanism (emulated, architectural).
    7. **`realtime_*` suppression** — the reserved `realtime_*` family is emitter-less in V1; the Codex driver suppresses the eleven `thread/realtime/*` server notifications (`thread/realtime/started`, `thread/realtime/closed`, `thread/realtime/error`, `thread/realtime/itemAdded`, `thread/realtime/sdp`, `thread/realtime/outputAudio/delta`, `thread/realtime/transcript/delta`, `thread/realtime/transcript/done`, `thread/realtime/item/started`, `thread/realtime/item/transcript/delta`, `thread/realtime/item/completed` — re-derived eight → eleven at the 2026-08-28 `0.150.1` re-pin, the three item-scoped names **added beside** the older spellings and not replacing them; [`codex.md`](../reference/provider-wire/codex.md) §`thread/realtime/*`) via `optOutNotificationMethods` (C-16) until the upstream feature flag stabilizes and the R8 realtime gate lifts; any upstream-added twelfth name still backstops through T3.11's default-branch diagnostic (never silent). No emulation is claimed (Claude exposes no realtime surface — absent).
  - **Approvals-reviewer pin (Codex spawn).** The Codex driver pins `approvalsReviewer: "user"` at session spawn so every approval routes through the daemon's Cedar pipeline (Spec-012 §Required Behavior); provider-side guardian / auto-review auto-adjudication is disabled by contract. The pin is verified against resolved config (it took effect), and the daemon **rejects/strips any per-turn `turn/start` override selecting a divergent reviewer** so a later turn cannot reroute adjudication to a guardian/`auto_review` path that bypasses Cedar. Guardian / auto-approval notifications normalize as observability rows, **never as approval decisions** (Spec-005 §Required Behavior).
  - **C-12 — native cost cap: two regimes.** The Claude driver realizes `--max-budget-usd` from the **server-stamped, per-run-frozen** cap, not from live session headroom: (1) an owner-admitted **native-cap unpriced** run passes exactly the run's `run.queued` `admittedUnpricedCapCents` (cents→USD) as `--max-budget-usd` — never recomputed from session headroom — and starts only inside a session spawned with the matching cap (posture-relaunch precedent); (2) for a **priced** run the CLI cap remains defense-in-depth sitting beneath the authoritative BudgetAccountant per Spec-016 §Budget Policies. At cap the run stops with `error_max_budget_usd` (metadata intact, resumable — normalized into the budget-exhausted taxonomy). Codex `token_budget` is under development at the pin (watched via the driver bundles' execution-time currency pass, no backlog item).

- **T3.18 — Provider-bound text neutralization + runtime tripwire (2026-08-25).**
  - Files: `packages/contracts/src/provider-driver.ts` (**EXTEND T1.6** — the additive-optional `refusalCode` member on `DriverInterventionResult` / `DriverInterventionResultSchema`, below); `packages/runtime-daemon/src/provider/drivers/outbound-frame.ts` (new — the shared outbound frame boundary: the closed origin union, the command-shaped-first-word predicate, the neutralizing frame writer, and the per-frame correlation mint); `packages/runtime-daemon/src/provider/drivers/claude/{lifecycle,intervention}.ts` (EXTEND T3.6 / T3.7); `packages/runtime-daemon/src/provider/drivers/codex/{lifecycle,intervention}.ts` (EXTEND T3.1 / T3.2); `packages/runtime-daemon/src/provider/drivers/{claude,codex}/event-normalizer.ts` (EXTEND T3.14 — the tripwire classification rides the sole terminal-emission boundary P1-1 already names, on **both** provider legs: the tripwire exists because a provider install can start command-dispatching between two runs, so wiring it only into the leg that dispatches today would leave the leg whose provider is not currently believed to command-dispatch with no detector for exactly the change the rule anticipates); driver `__fixtures__/`
  - **Spec coverage:** Spec-005 §Required Behavior (prose delivery, frame origin, runtime tripwire); Spec-005 §Parity Capability Mechanism Grades (the provider-bound text-neutrality row); Spec-005 §Pitfalls To Avoid (no invisible sentinel; no mutation upstream of the driver)
  - **Verifies invariant:** I-005-7
  - Consumes: T3.1 / T3.6 lifecycle modules (a run's opening content, and admitted queue content re-entering through `startRun`); T3.2 / T3.7 intervention dispatchers (a steer directive's content); T3.14 P1-1's normalizer-as-terminal-emission-boundary rule
  - Provides: the three provider-bound text paths funnel through one frame writer that neutralizes command-shaped `participant_text` with a single leading newline, routes `driver_command` verbatim, neutralizes `system_narration`, and **neutralizes an absent or unrecognized origin**. Coverage is **structural, not a call-site checklist**: the child process's writable handle is encapsulated by the frame writer's module and is not reachable from a driver module, so a later provider-bound path cannot bypass neutralization by forgetting to call it — the alternative, an audited list of call sites, decays the first time someone adds a path. The writer stamps each frame with a daemon-minted correlation value; the normalizer classifies the provider's response to that frame by requiring **positive typed evidence of a model turn** for it — a correlated frame that settles with no such evidence has tripped, and an unrecognized response envelope trips rather than passing, the polarity being fail-closed — and on a trip fails the run with `driver.text_neutralization_failed` ([error-contracts.md §Driver](../architecture/contracts/error-contracts.md#driver)) instead of recording the provider's zero-turn success as a completed turn. `driver_command` frames are tripwire-exempt. The origin union is declared **daemon-local** in this module and deliberately not hoisted onto `packages/contracts/src/provider-driver.ts`: it classifies frames the driver itself constructs, so putting it on the wire-facing contract would imply a caller supplies it and would invite a client-chosen origin — the one input that must never be client-chosen. The driver-level **status** vocabulary is restated unwidened (`applied | degraded`), and the shipped `DriverInterventionResultSchema` — `{ status, fallbackAction? }`, authored at T1.6 and merged as PR #157 — takes **one additive-optional member**, `refusalCode?: 'driver.text_neutralization_failed'`, declared as a closed literal union rather than a string so the envelope gains no free-form provider-output surface and needs no `wireFreeFormString` bound. `.strict()` is retained and stays meaningful: strictness rejects **unknown** keys, and a declared optional key is not unknown. This is a Phase-3 EXTEND of a Phase-1 contract on the same additive-optional footing Phase 3B already uses for its two seams — no shipped field changes type, no shipped call site is required to change, and no merged manifest entry changes meaning. The refusal lands on the run instead, one landing for both paths — the normalizer, P1-1's sole terminal-emission boundary and already in this task's file list, emits the run's ordinary `run.failed` terminal carrying `failureCategory: 'provider failure'`, `recoveryCondition: 'recovery-needed'`, and a `providerFailureDetail` composed in the single fixed form `Spec-005 §Required Behavior` pins — the code, one space byte, then `origin=` and one of `participant_text` / `system_narration` / `unknown` — which is what makes the detail parseable by a downstream reader rather than merely human-legible, and which is why the writer emits the literal `unknown` for an origin the union does not name instead of echoing it. That is CP-005-5's already-resolved handoff shape (a typed driver-side condition surfaced on the canonical run-lifecycle surface; no separate driver event, no persisted column), so this task mints no cross-plan obligation and edits no Plan-004 file — the driver→lifecycle intervention mapper at `packages/runtime-daemon/src/interventions/intervention-service.ts` is Plan-004 T2.4 / T2.6's and stays outside this scope. Neither path can raise a JSON-RPC error, and the run-start path could not have taken a synchronous refusal at all: `startRun` is daemon-internal with no wire method behind it (this plan's client-SDK surface deliberately omits the four session/run lifecycle ops, since exposing one would let a client bypass `runtime_bindings` persistence and canonical run/session event emission), and a run admitted from the queue or started by orchestration has no caller waiting — so the `run.failed` run-lifecycle event ([Spec-006 §Run Lifecycle](../specs/006-session-event-taxonomy-and-audit-log.md#run-lifecycle-run_lifecycle), an existing kind; none is registered here) is the asynchronous outcome that path surfaces through. An intervention does have a caller, so it also answers on its own result branch — settling `degraded` on the existing zero-effect arm, dispatch having occurred so pre-dispatch `rejected` is definitionally unavailable ([queue-and-intervention-model.md §Driver Result To Lifecycle Mapping](../domain/queue-and-intervention-model.md#driver-result-to-lifecycle-mapping)), with no `fallbackAction` and with `refusalCode` set. That member is **best-effort by construction** per `Spec-005 §Required Behavior`: it is set only when the trip is classified before the call resolves, the driver never holding `applyIntervention` open for its frame to settle, so tests assert the run terminal as the guarantee and the member as the fast-path convenience — never the reverse. The **consumer** of that member is Plan-004 **T2.6**, the `DriverInterventionResult` → 6-state lifecycle mapper, under the already-registered **CP-004-1** obligation (`Driver capability flags + DriverInterventionResult`, Consume from Plan-005, tasks T2.6 / T2.7) — its scope widens by one member and no new cross-plan obligation is minted; T3.18 authors the contract and edits no Plan-004 file. An intervention-path trip **fails the target run and disposes its provider binding**: the provider dispatched the frame, so it may have executed an unrecognized client-side action against the session state that run depends on. Failing the run alone leaves the process alive for the next run to bind to, so the driver closes the provider session and the process leaves the live provider-process registry; resuming work is a fresh spawn. Disposal is a **process fact, not a row state**: `runtime_bindings` owns no liveness column (the T2.1 note already rules `recovery_state` / `recovery_needed` off that table, and T2.2 rules the store returns superseded history too, the CALLER owning the liveness intersection against the process supervisor's registry), so a disposed binding is excluded from delivery by machinery already shipped, this task mints no column, and the refusal takes no attach-time arm — that arm would need the very marker being refused. The one exit from a terminal state — a rollback intervention's `failed -> paused` — is unaffected because disposal creates no new state: a terminal run with no live provider process is the ordinary case that path already faces (startup failure, resume failure, and an exited provider all reach `failed` the same way), and the fork-composed `DriverRollbackResult.applied.bindingId` arm exists for exactly that shape. A disposed process cannot be re-bound by a caller that forgets to read a flag.
  - Dispatch: **T3.18 dispatches with Phase 3** — the phase's three `preconditions:` gates are met (Phase 1 merged as PR #157, Phase 2 as PR #159, Plan-006 Phase 1 as PR #247), and the task adds no gate of its own. It is homed in Phase 3 rather than Phase 3B precisely because Phase 3B gates on `{ plan: 005, phase: 3, status: merged }`, which would push the neutralization behind the very driver code it must cover. (Landed-fact annotation, 2026-08-27: the conclusion holds, but the evidence as written never established it — under declared-⊆-shipped Phase 1 classified `partially_shipped` until T1.7/T1.8 merged at PR #369 ([§6 node NS-87](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md#6-active-next-steps-dag)) and Phase 2 until T2.6 merged at PR #372 ([§6 node NS-88](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md#6-active-next-steps-dag)), so the three gates read MET only from 2026-08-27 forward — on those two shipments in addition to PRs #157 / #159 / #247.)
  - Step 1 (currency, before any code lands): re-probe both pinned binaries and pin the observed behavior into [`claude.md`](../reference/provider-wire/claude.md) and [`codex.md`](../reference/provider-wire/codex.md), following the campaign's regenerate-don't-transcribe rule. **The Codex probe must exercise the app-server `turn/start` path this driver actually uses, not only `codex exec`** — the parity row grades this leg `emulated` today for exactly that reason: `exec` is a different frontend, and its input parsing establishes nothing about how app-server frames are handled. The probe's polarity follows `Spec-005 §Required Behavior`'s rule that a behavioral row states the grade governing today: verbatim delivery on `turn/start` re-grades the cell `native` by ordinary amendment, command dispatch leaves it `emulated`, and either way this leg neutralizes until the probe lands, so no code path is contingent on the probe's outcome. The 2026-08-25 first-party observations this task is authored against are Claude Code `2.1.245` (a message whose first word is an unrecognized command name returns a synthetic client-side reply, a zero-turn success, no model call and no error; a single leading newline restores an ordinary model turn) and codex-cli `0.149.1` (`codex exec` passes the identical text to the model verbatim — established by **positive evidence**, the model asked to echo the message's leading characters and returning them with the `/` intact, rather than by the weaker absence of an error; the same standard the tripwire above applies to a settled frame). The same probe pins each provider's **turn-evidence discriminants** — the typed response-envelope fields by which the normalizer recognizes that a correlated frame produced a model turn — and records them in the driver, since [`claude.md`](../reference/provider-wire/claude.md) and [`codex.md`](../reference/provider-wire/codex.md) document none at the pinned versions; the tripwire's fail-closed polarity is what keeps an unpinned or later-changed shape a loud trip rather than a silent pass, so this pin sharpens the detector and is never what its correctness rests on.
  - Downstream consumer: [Spec-017 §Command interception — the reserved `/` prefix (C-18)](../specs/017-workflow-authoring-and-execution.md#command-interception--the-reserved--prefix-c-18) holds the composer's `//` escape inactive on provider-bound composer paths until this task's code merges; activating it is a Plan-017 / Plan-023 composer follow-up that **no task yet authors**, not part of this task — T3.18's merge satisfies that gate without changing any composer code, so the refusal correctly stands until the follow-up is authored and ships, and [Spec-017 §Command interception — the reserved `/` prefix (C-18)](../specs/017-workflow-authoring-and-execution.md#command-interception--the-reserved--prefix-c-18) says so in those words rather than leaving a reader to infer scheduled work from a named gate. Recorded as reviewer-checkable prose rather than a `precondition_box_checked` gate, on the NS-73 precedent — a phase-level gate here would hold Phase 3 tasks that have no such dependency.
  - Tests: wire-level **byte** assertions (not string-equality on a rendered payload) that a command-shaped first word leaves the daemon newline-prefixed on all three provider-bound paths, and that the frame writer takes the leg's mechanism grade as an **input** — a writer constructed for a leg declared `native` emits the identical bytes it was handed, one declared `emulated` prepends exactly one newline — with both arms driven from the test's own declarations rather than from whichever grade the shipped rows carry, since `Spec-005 §Parity Capability Mechanism Grades` grades **both** provider legs `emulated` at this task's landing and a writer that hard-codes the transform on would otherwise pass every shipped path; **seeded discriminating controls** in the I-005-6 idiom, pinned to the spec's deliberately coarse predicate (first non-whitespace byte is `/`): a mid-text slash passes through unchanged, while the path-like `/etc/hosts`, the plugin-qualified `/foo:bar`, and a whitespace-then-`/` message are each neutralized — so an implementation that strips the leading `/`, that carves a path-like or qualified-name exception out of the predicate, or that trims before testing fails here and only here; the transform is asserted as **exactly one prepended newline** against the author's otherwise-identical bytes; a **history non-mutation** assertion that the persisted event row, the replayed timeline, and the rollback target for a neutralized turn all carry the author's original bytes with no sentinel; an **unknown-origin** case (a frame constructed with an origin the union does not name) neutralizes rather than passing through; the **conformance fixture** — the recorded `2.1.245` zero-turn synthetic reply, checked in as the normative instance `Spec-005 §Required Behavior` names (a client-composed reply item, no model-turn attribution for the correlated frame, no token usage accounted to it), which every driver's classifier must trip on — fails the run with `driver.text_neutralization_failed` leading the `run.failed` terminal's `providerFailureDetail` rather than completing the turn, paired with its **negative control**, an ordinary model-turn transcript that must not trip, and with the **same pair run against the Codex normalizer** on a synthesized command-dispatch response, so the Codex leg's detector is proven present rather than assumed unnecessary; a `driver_command`-origin frame is delivered verbatim and does not trip the tripwire; and an **intervention-path trip** — the same recorded transcript replayed against a steer rather than a run start — settles the intervention `degraded` with no `fallbackAction` and with `refusalCode: 'driver.text_neutralization_failed'` on the result envelope (parsed through `DriverInterventionResultSchema`, so a build that added the member to the interface but not the schema fails here; the fixture can assert the member because the zero-turn synthetic reply settles inside the call — the assertion is on that fixture's timing, not on a universal guarantee, and the run-terminal assertion below is what holds for every trip), emits no JSON-RPC error, **disposes the run's provider binding** — a subsequent attach against it is refused with the same code, which is the assertion separating _failed the run_ from _quarantined the process_ — and **fails the target run** with `failureCategory: 'provider failure'`, `recoveryCondition: 'recovery-needed'`, and a `providerFailureDetail` asserted as the **exact composed string** `driver.text_neutralization_failed origin=participant_text` rather than as a prefix match, since a detail a consumer cannot parse is the defect the fixed form exists to prevent — so an implementation that lets the run continue after a misfired steer, that leaves the binding attachable, or that carries the cause on only one of the two surfaces, fails here. Two further controls pin the classifier's **polarity** rather than its enumeration, which is the half a recorded transcript cannot cover: a correlated frame that settles with a response envelope the driver does not recognize trips (fail-closed — no dispatch shape is matched, and none needs to be), and a frame whose origin the union does not name yields the exact detail `driver.text_neutralization_failed origin=unknown`, the unrecognized value appearing nowhere in it.
  - Estimate: 1 PR

  - **Spec coverage:** Spec-005 §Canonical Transcript Export And Replay (the projection-never-a-store rule; the ordered pipeline); Spec-005 §Interfaces And Contracts (the two new driver operations); Spec-005 §Pitfalls To Avoid (no re-minted tool-call ids; no strip-after-repair; no cached render)
  - **Verifies invariant:** I-005-8

  - Provides: the two `ProviderDriver` operations `exportTranscript` / `replayTranscript` (the operation surface sixteen members after this task) with their param/result shapes and the `CanonicalTranscriptTurn` element shape this plan owns — the fold's per-turn members are exactly what the ordered pipeline operates on, so the element is authored beside the pipeline that consumes it rather than mirrored from a doc that does not run it ([api-payload-contracts.md](../architecture/contracts/api-payload-contracts.md) carries the projection's identity and defers the element here); the capability flag on `DriverCapabilityFlag` (fourteen members after this task), with its `driver_capabilities` backfill row per the [schema doc's widening note](../architecture/schemas/local-sqlite-schema.md). The two legs of that widening settle differently and must not be conflated: the **CHECK** needs no second widening, because T1.7's catch-up migration admits all fourteen values at once (a CHECK is a whitelist, so admitting a value before any row uses it costs nothing); the **row set** does, because `DriverCapabilitiesWriter`'s snapshot reader enforces an exact `DRIVER_CAPABILITY_FLAGS.length` cardinality and throws on a mismatch — so a cache left at thirteen rows fails the next hydrate **before** any refresh could heal it, and the backfill (`supported = 0` for every existing `driver_name`, undeclared = unsupported per I-005-2) must land in the same migration ordinal that widens the union rather than being deferred to a refresh. Plus a pure projection and a pure transform. The pipeline's step order is the contract, so the tests assert **order**, not only outcome: a fixture whose tool result was stripped with the reasoning must emerge repaired, and a fixture run through a deliberately reordered pipeline must fail the same assertion. Tool-call identity is a stable bidirectional map, so a round trip through export and back is the identity function on ids.
  - Tests: a transcript containing a tool call whose result was removed emerges carrying a synthetic error result rather than an unpaired call or a dropped one; export is idempotent on ids across two runs; a canonical transcript rendered twice at the same log position renders identically, and rendered again after an appended event differs — the projection-not-a-store property asserted positively rather than by comment.
