- **T3.20 — `replayTranscript` + the post-replay assertion, both drivers (2026-08-26).**
  - Files: `packages/runtime-daemon/src/provider/drivers/codex/lifecycle.ts` (**EXTEND T3.1** — the Codex replay leg over its seeding surface); `packages/runtime-daemon/src/provider/drivers/claude/lifecycle.ts` (**EXTEND T3.6** — the Claude replay leg); `packages/runtime-daemon/src/provider/transcript/replay-assertion.ts` (new); `packages/runtime-daemon/src/provider/drivers/codex/capabilities.ts` (**EXTEND T3.3** — flipping the declared `transcript_replay: true` off T3.19's false backfill); `packages/runtime-daemon/src/provider/drivers/claude/capabilities.ts` (**EXTEND T3.8** — the probe-derived declaration), so the declaration and the probe have an owned landing surface
  - **Spec coverage:** Spec-005 §Required Behavior (a replay is verified by what the session answers, never by what the call returned); Spec-005 §Canonical Transcript Export And Replay (capability discovery; the probe-valued Claude cell); Spec-005 §Per-Driver Capability Matrix (`transcript_replay`)
  - **Verifies invariant:** I-005-8
  - Consumes: T3.19's exported frames; T3.3 / T3.8 capability declaration; T3.21's memo projection — the refusal-path settlement this task's tests route into, named here so the task DAG cannot level this task beside the floor it invokes; **and `session_events.content_payload`** (CP-005-13 ⇄ CP-006-15, registered 2026-08-30 by the machine-authored prose amendment, §6 node NS-94)
  - **Task-scoped hold (2026-08-30): this task dispatches only after [Plan-006](./006-session-event-taxonomy-and-audit-log.md) Phase 3B merges.** The hold binds T3.20 alone — the BL-153 `held task-scoped` form, not a phase gate — and is deliberately narrow: T3.19's fold and export were authored against a body-less log and stay correct over one, so nothing earlier in Phase 3 is blocked. What this task cannot do without the column is the single thing its `Tests` bullet calls the most important test in the task. Until assistant and tool bodies are durably logged, every replayed turn is content-free, and a post-replay assertion over content-free turns cannot separate a provider that accepted every frame from one that silently dropped them all: both answer with the same structurally-present, semantically-empty turns. The assertion is `Spec-005 §Canonical Transcript Export And Replay`'s only admissible evidence a replay worked, so running it before the column exists would ship the evidence and not the proof. Phase 3B additionally converts the two step-1 (Fold) loss members from universal to exceptional — `'turn_content_unavailable'` firing on an undecryptable row rather than on every assistant turn, and `'turn_content_truncated'` on a stored prefix — which is what makes a `declaredLosses` array read as a report rather than as noise.
  - Provides: replay over each provider's own seeding surface, and the assertion that gates the result. The Claude leg **declares `transcript_replay` from a probe of the installed build** rather than from a constant, because no stable seeding contract is published for it — a `false` declaration routes to T3.21 and is a supported outcome, not a test failure. This task also owns the **replay-target lifecycle**: targets are fresh and disposable, never the session the participant is watching, and a partially-seeded target is **abandoned, never reused** — on a mid-seeding ambiguity and equally on an interior structural refusal after a prefix was accepted — because a partial native prefix left beside any later delivery, a reseed or the memo settlement, duplicates participant facts and can recreate exactly the unpaired tool history the pipeline exists to repair; the memo settlement (AC10) therefore always lands in a **fresh** target.
  - Tests: a driver whose replay call returns success but whose session answers with zero turns fails the operation — the single most important test in this task, and it is written against a **stubbed provider that lies**, because a provider that returns success on a bad frame is the documented case, not the hypothetical one; a `transcript_replay: false` declaration routes to the memo path and reports `degraded`; a replay never writes to the source session; a target that accepts a prefix of the seeding frames and then structurally refuses is abandoned — the memo settlement lands in a fresh target and **no surviving session holds both native frames and the memo**, asserted across the abandoned and replacement targets; a mid-seeding ambiguity likewise leaves no surviving session holding a duplicated frame; the capability-discovery pair lands here too — Codex's `getCapabilities()` declares `transcript_replay: true` off the T3.3 surface (the declared flip of T3.19's `supported = 0` backfill), and Claude's declared value **tracks the probe result in both directions** — the suite flips the probe double and asserts the declaration follows — so a leg left at the backfill or hard-coded on either cell fails discovery rather than passing every replay test (the AC12 carrier).
  - Estimate: 1 PR

- **T3.21 — Memo projection floor (2026-08-26).**
  - Files: `packages/runtime-daemon/src/provider/transcript/memo-projection.ts` (new)
  - **Spec coverage:** Spec-005 §Canonical Transcript Export And Replay (the four normative memo properties); Spec-005 §Fallback Behavior
  - **Verifies invariant:** I-005-9
  - Consumes: T3.19's canonical projection (the memo renders the same projection, never a second source)
  - Provides: a budget expressed as a fraction of the **target's** context window; whole-exchange eviction with a protected tail; once-only delivery under a **derived** memo-identity key rather than a durable claim — a deterministic function of the canonical projection at the export log position and the target session's identity, recomputable by any daemon at any time from state that is already durable, persisted in **no daemon-side claim row**, and **carried on the sent memo turn as a visible marker** so the target itself holds the value reconciliation compares against; suppression when native replay succeeded — a **caller routing decision, asserted at the caller**: the memo module takes no dependency on the replay operation (this task's Consumes names T3.19 alone), so the caller-side Consumes edges into this floor stay one-directional and the task graph acyclic. Every operation settled here reports `degraded` and carries the declared-loss list, which always includes `conversation_history_summarized` — the memo floor's own loss, without which a memo settlement's list could be empty and would then read as "nothing was dropped".
  - **Reconcile before every send; there is no durable claim to leak.** A claim written before the send would need a durable owner and has none: T2.1's standing prohibition rules `runtime_bindings` out for recovery state, and the local schema has no generic claim store — so a claim would mint a table to hold a single in-flight marker for something the event log already determines, which is the second record of an ordered fact that [ADR-029](../decisions/029-canonical-transcript-is-authoritative.md) rejects on its own terms. The once-only property therefore rests on **derivation**: the memo-identity key is computed from the canonical projection at the export log position plus the target session's identity, which makes it stable across restarts and identical for any two daemons rendering the same switch. T3.19's determinism property — _a canonical transcript rendered twice at the same log position renders identically_ — is exactly what that stability rests on, and is why this task consumes that projection rather than rendering one of its own. Derivation alone would be inert, though: the key is also **carried on the memo turn** as a visible marker — never an invisible or zero-width sentinel, which Spec-005 prohibits — and that carriage is what puts the value in the target to be found, derivation without it leaving nothing to match. So **every send reconciles first**: recompute the key and read the target session's recent turns for it, treating a match as already delivered. The ambiguous send needs no special path — a timeout, a dropped connection, or a daemon restart between send and acknowledgment leaves the outcome unknown, and the unknown resolves the way every other pre-send question does, by reading the target. Where the target cannot be read back, nothing is sent and the operation settles on what did land: a duplicated memo corrupts the conversation the participant is watching, while a missing one only degrades it, visibly. This **removes** state rather than adding it, and mints no table, no column, and no migration ordinal.
  - Tests: a budget tight enough to evict must never emit a tool call without its result or a result without its call — asserted by construction over generated transcripts rather than by one fixture; **a send that succeeds at the target and then loses its acknowledgment delivers the memo exactly once** — the reconciliation path asserted against the failure that actually produces duplicates, not against a clean retry; an unreadable target after an ambiguous send sends nothing; the memo-identity key is a **pure function** of the projection and the target — one daemon across a restart, and two daemons rendering the same switch, derive the same key, asserted by recomputation rather than by storage — while an appended event changes it, so the key tracks the transcript it identifies; **no durable claim is written on any path**, asserted as an absence rather than assumed; the rendered outbound memo frame **contains** the derived key and contains it as visible characters, asserted on the frame rather than on the projection, since a key reconciliation can recompute but the target never received would make every send look new; a successful native replay emits no memo — asserted at the caller's routing seam, since suppression is the caller's decision and the memo module holds no replay dependency; a memo settlement never renders identically to an applied one.
  - Estimate: 1 PR

- **T3.22 — Permanent structural-refusal classification (2026-08-26).**
  - Files: `packages/runtime-daemon/src/provider/transcript/failure-mapping.ts` (new — the permanent-vs-transient refusal classifier, homed in `transcript/` per the ownership map's T3.19-T3.22 registration so the `drivers/` row's 13-file enumeration stays exact); `packages/runtime-daemon/src/provider/drivers/codex/lifecycle.ts` (**EXTEND T3.1** — wiring the classifier at the Codex request-dispatch seam, where a poisoned history first surfaces as a refusal on an ordinary turn); `packages/runtime-daemon/src/provider/drivers/claude/lifecycle.ts` (**EXTEND T3.6** — the Claude dispatch seam), so classification precedes reconstitution rather than riding inside it — the permanent arm's response **is** T3.20's replay, and a refusal arising inside that replay settles on T3.21's memo floor (AC10), never on a second reconstitution
  - **Spec coverage:** Spec-005 §Required Behavior (a structurally invalid history is a permanent refusal, never a retry); Spec-005 §Fallback Behavior
  - **Verifies invariant:** I-005-9
  - Consumes: T3.14 P3-3's `RecoveryCondition` taxonomy (the recovery classes the permanent classification must stay distinct from, per Spec-005's never-absorbed rule); T3.20's replay operation and its replay-target lifecycle rules (the permanent arm's reconstitution target — never re-entered on its own interior refusal); T3.21's memo-projection floor and its target-readback reconciliation surface (the replay-interior refusal's settlement and the ambiguity read — named here so the task DAG cannot dispatch this task ahead of the floor it routes to)
  - Provides: a structural rejection is classified permanent and never enters the retry ladder — it disposes the run's provider binding and reconstitutes from the canonical transcript. The classification is by the provider's typed refusal shape, never by matching message text, on the standing §Pitfalls prohibition. The classifier owns **both arms** of the distinction — the transient arm classifies a transport fault retryable here, not by mapping onto a T3.14 `RecoveryCondition` class, and the permanent arm is never absorbed into `recovery-needed`. The transient arm retries only a failure that is **definitely unsent** — refused before the provider consumed the request. An outcome a connection loss left unknown (applied-but-unacknowledged) is never blindly retried, because a repeated turn repeats its spend — and the reconciliation is **positional, never content-based**: an ordinary turn carries no identity marker (that carriage is T3.21's memo mechanism, and participant text may legitimately repeat), so the driver compares the provider session's readback count of **participant-originated turns** — the one unit both sides count identically, since a readback interleaves assistant turns the daemon's acknowledged set does not hold — through the same target-readback surface T3.21 reconciles through — against the participant sends the daemon has acknowledged, which the canonical transcript already orders, and the read cannot race: it happens **before any further send on that session**, the same reconcile-before-send ordering T3.21 gives the memo path, so no concurrent append moves either count; a count that includes the ambiguous turn settles it as delivered, one that does not clears it for retry. Where the driver cannot read the target back, the ambiguity is **unrecoverable**: the turn fails visibly on the run's existing failure path, never silently re-sent (duplicate spend) and never silently assumed delivered (a suppressed request). Mid-replay ambiguity and replay-interior refusals are **T3.20's target-lifecycle rules** — abandon the partial target, reseed or settle the memo into a fresh one — this task only classifies and routes.
  - Tests: a stubbed provider that refuses identically on every attempt produces exactly **one** attempt, not a ladder — the assertion is on the call count, because the defect being prevented is expenditure, and the refusal is driven **through the request-dispatch path**, never by importing the classifier directly; a genuinely transient fault still retries, so the two classes are proven distinguishable rather than merged; a fault after the provider applied the request but before its acknowledgment arrived produces **zero duplicate turns** — the positional readback-count reconcile is asserted against the failure that actually produces duplicates, not against a clean retry; the same ambiguity against a driver whose target cannot be read back sends nothing and fails the turn visibly, asserted as zero re-sends **and** a failed settlement, never a silent success; a refusal raised inside the replay operation settles on the memo floor with exactly **one** reconstitution attempted, so recovery never repeats the operation already in progress (the abandoned-target assertions live in T3.20, beside the lifecycle they verify).
  - Estimate: 1 PR

- **T3.23 — Spawn-time binary resolution, in-band version read, and the ratified floor gate (EXTENDs T3.12 P0-2, 2026-08-26).**
  - Files: `packages/runtime-daemon/src/provider/version-gate.ts` (new — resolve / spawn / read / compare, one reading shared by the gate and the binding write); `packages/runtime-daemon/src/provider/drivers/codex/capabilities.ts` (EXTEND T3.3); `packages/runtime-daemon/src/provider/drivers/claude/capabilities.ts` (EXTEND T3.8); `packages/runtime-daemon/src/provider/runtime-binding-store.ts` (EXTEND T2.2 — the write seam persists the same reading)
  - **Spec coverage:** Spec-005 §Required Behavior (the ratified V1 floors Claude Code `2.1.234` / codex-cli `0.141.0`; the floor-is-this-spec's / pin-is-the-reference-family's split; the version a driver reports is the version that spawned)
  - **Verifies invariant:** I-005-10
  - Consumes: T3.12's `DriverCliVersionReport` + floor-compare path (this task re-points its source, not its shape); T2.2's binding write seam
  - Provides: the resolved-executable spawn, the in-band version read (Claude `get_binary_version` control request → `{ version, buildTime }`, adopted as-is; Codex `initialize` result → the composite `userAgent`, **extracted**). The Codex extraction is specified here because the string is not a version: at `0.149.1` it reads `<clientName>/<codexVersion> (<os>; <arch>) <terminal> (<clientName>; <clientVersion>)`, so the caller's own name and version appear inside it and a naive parse can return the client's version instead of the provider's. The rule: the daemon supplies a `clientInfo.name` containing no `/` and no whitespace, the driver takes the substring between the **first** `/` and the next whitespace, and that substring must parse as semver — anything else, including a `userAgent` whose leading token is not the name the daemon supplied, refuses as `driver.cli_version_unparseable` rather than falling back to a shell-out. There is no structured alternative to fall back to: `server/diagnostics` refuses without the `experimentalApi` capability and, granted it, returns process gauges with no version field (Verified 2026-08-26 at `0.149.1`), and reaching it at all would mean negotiating `experimentalApi`, which V1's posture realization is deliberately built not to need — T3.25 realizes the **non-experimental** `sandboxPolicy` arm, the floor comparison against the ratified pair, and the `cli_version_raw` / `cli_version_semver` persistence of **that same reading** with the resolved executable path on `spawn_config`. A launcher symlink is never the version source. **No column and no migration ordinal:** both carriers ship at Phase 2.
  - Tests: a below-floor build refuses attach as `driver.cli_version_below_floor` before session creation, before any capability probe, and before any billed turn — the minimal version-handshake process is permitted to spawn, since the version is read in-band from it, and the assertion is that the refusal precedes every use of that process beyond the handshake; an above-pin build attaches; the version the binding row records equals the version the spawned process reported, asserted against a path-addressed spawn whose launcher names a different build (the launcher-drift arm — this is the case a `--version` shell-out gets wrong); an unparseable in-band report still refuses as `driver.cli_version_unparseable`.
  - Estimate: 1 PR

- **T3.24 — Per-capability zero-turn detection with detection source (EXTENDs T3.12, 2026-08-26).**
  - Files: `packages/runtime-daemon/src/provider/capability-probe.ts` (new — the per-driver probe table, its negative controls, and the classifier); `packages/contracts/src/provider-driver.ts` (**EXTEND T1.6** — the closed-literal `CapabilityDetectionSource` and the additive-optional `detectionSource` member on `GetCapabilitiesResult`, whose api-payload-contracts.md doc-mirror **already landed with this amendment**: this task implements the registered shape rather than authoring it); `packages/runtime-daemon/src/provider/drivers/codex/capabilities.ts` + `.../claude/capabilities.ts` (EXTEND T3.3 / T3.8); `packages/runtime-daemon/src/provider/capability-refresh.ts` (EXTEND T3.12 — the cadence re-probes)
  - **Spec coverage:** Spec-005 §Required Behavior (per-capability zero-turn detection; registry membership neither necessary nor sufficient); Spec-005 §Capability discovery (declaration is a reading of the installed build)
  - **Verifies invariant:** I-005-10
  - Consumes: T3.23's resolved spawn (a probe must run against the build that will run the session); T3.12's `CapabilityRefreshScheduler` lifecycle and its change-detected emission
  - Provides: a **declared detection-mechanism table** — one entry per `DriverCapabilityFlag` per driver, each naming its mechanism and, for a `static` entry, the admissibility conjunct that fails. Probeable entries pair a probe with a negative control and classify into exactly one flag: Claude probes over the control-request channel (a supported subtype answers `success`; an unsupported one answers the typed `Unsupported control request subtype: <name>`); Codex probes over `initialize` capability negotiation plus the `-32600` unknown-method reply, whose message enumerates the accepted method set. Two flag entries are **declared non-probeable** and this task does not probe them. `mcp` fails the **zero-turn** and **non-mutating** conjuncts together: the flag asserts only that the provider can invoke MCP server tools, and the sole direct probe of that is invoking one, which consumes a turn and performs the tool's own effect — so it resolves from the matrix. `rollback` on the Codex leg fails the **granularity** conjunct: the `-32600` enumeration establishes that `thread/fork` is accepted, not that `ThreadForkParams.lastTurnId` is present, and the reference verifies that field at the `0.150.1` pin rather than at the `0.141.0` floor ([Codex wire reference](../reference/provider-wire/codex.md)) — so the flag resolves from the matrix until a parameter-level probe exists, and an invocation against a build that accepts the method without the boundary field refuses at invocation as `driver.capability_unsupported` rather than silently rewinding to the wrong position. Separately — and **not** as a table entry, because it is **not a capability flag** — the set-replacing `mcp_set_servers` control request is off limits to this task entirely: no probe issues it under any flag's mechanism, since `Spec-028 §Required Behavior` defines the operation as replacing the full named-server set and an empty-set call would clear a live session's servers. Its runtime availability is Plan-028's to establish from that plan's own full-desired-set reconcile (CP-005-11), and a refusal there withdraws **no** capability flag — least of all `mcp`, which denotes MCP tool invocation and is unaffected. The member is **additive-optional** and **live-scoped**: total over the flag set on a live read, and absent on a `DriverCapabilitiesWriter.hydrate()` reconstruction, because the cache persists flag values and not provenance — so no shipped construction site is required to change and no cache column is minted. Withdrawal reuses the shipped surfaces — no new event type (the changed snapshot rides `runtime_node.capability_updated`, CP-005-5) and no new error code (invocation refuses as `driver.capability_unsupported`).
  - Tests: a probe suite asserts **zero billed turns at the provider transport** — a recording transport double asserts that an attach that probes issues no turn-start and no user-message request, which a daemon-side assertion on `usage.cost_update` and run-lifecycle events cannot establish (a probe that billed before normal event handling attached would produce neither row); the mechanism table is **total** over the flag set for each driver, every `static` entry names a failing conjunct, and an entry claiming `probed` without a declared probe fails the suite; no probe issues `mcp_set_servers` at all, asserted at the same transport double; the negative control is refused at every probed build, and a probe run whose negative control **succeeds** fails the suite rather than reporting capabilities available; a driver whose one probe refuses keeps every other flag and the session; a flag moving `true` → `false` across two polls emits exactly one `runtime_node.capability_updated` and an unchanged poll emits none.
  - Estimate: 2 PRs

  - Estimate: 1 PR

- **T3.26 — Console-parity driver operations, the three capability flags, and the driver-ask choice set (2026-08-29).**
  - Files: `packages/contracts/src/provider-driver.ts` (EXTEND — the `ProviderDriver` interface and `DRIVER_CAPABILITY_FLAGS`); `packages/runtime-daemon/src/provider/capability-probe.ts` (**EXTEND T3.24**, added 2026-08-29 at the round-3 fold — the three new flags' per-driver detection-mechanism entries, without which T3.24's totality test fails the moment the union grows); `packages/runtime-daemon/src/migrations/` (NEW — the next-ordinal `driver_capabilities.capability_flag` CHECK widening); `packages/runtime-daemon/src/provider/drivers/codex/` and `.../claude/` (EXTEND — both drivers' implementations and normalize boundaries).
  - Provides, in four legs. **(a) `compactContext`** — the operation surface's seventeenth and eighteenth members land in this task; this one is gated on `context_compaction`, `native` on the Codex leg through `thread/compact/start` and `emulated` on the Claude leg through a `driver_command` frame carrying the provider's own `/compact`. It settles on the provider's **typed compaction evidence** — the frame that already produces `usage.context_compacted` — and never on the request being accepted, because the Codex method answers an empty ack and the Claude frame only settles. The Claude leg is the **first V1 producer of a `driver_command` frame**, so it carries both guards `Spec-005 §Required Behavior` requires of that tripwire-exempt arm: the command name is checked against leg (b)'s enumeration for that session's binding **before** dispatch, and the typed evidence is required **after** it. There is no prompt-injected emulation arm and adding one is prohibited. The wait for that evidence is **bounded and twice-terminated**: a per-driver declared compaction bound (declared beside the driver's other declared timeouts, not a corpus constant — the two mechanisms do not take comparable time) and the run's provider binding ceasing to be live, whichever lands first. Expiry settles `failed` with a diagnostic naming the terminal, never `applied`, and a compaction frame arriving **after** settlement still normalizes into `usage.context_compacted` through the ordinary unsolicited-compaction path, so the bound constrains the operation and never the boundary's record. The settlement is the **discriminated `DriverCompactionResult` union** ([api-payload-contracts.md](../architecture/contracts/api-payload-contracts.md)) — `applied` carrying a required `boundaryPosition` (`number | null`), `refused` where nothing was sent, `failed` where something was sent and no boundary was witnessed — and it carries **no** `capability_undeclared` arm, because an undeclared flag refuses at T4.6's static capability gate before this operation is reached. **(b) `listProviderCommands`** — gated on `provider_commands`, a **live read** returning the provider's own command and skill enumeration as driver-session state: Codex through `skills/list` with `SkillsChangedNotification` triggering a **re-read rather than a patch**, Claude through the three enumerated sets its session handshake already emits. Every entry carries the `(driverName, providerAccountId)` it was read under. Nothing is persisted and no projection folds it, so this leg adds **no table and no column**. Two round-3 corrections land here. The result is the **per-binding group list** `ProviderCommandListResult` — one `ProviderCommandBindingGroup` per live binding, each naming its run and the `(driverName, providerAccountId)` it was read under, with its **own** entry cap and `complete` flag — because an agent can hold several live bindings at once (V1 admits concurrent runs and a run-boundary switch leaves older runs on their spawn binding), and a flat array would hand the composer an arbitrary leg's commands with the provenance stripped. And the entry carries the provider's **`enabled` declaration verbatim** where the surface publishes one (Codex `skills/list` does; the Claude handshake enumeration does not, and the driver **synthesizes nothing** for it): a disabled entry is **returned, never filtered**, since a driver-side drop stops the result being the enumeration as observed and leaves a consumer unable to tell a disabled command from a nonexistent one. The flag governs offerability, not presence. The leg builds a **discovery** surface and not a dispatch channel: no returned member is a dispatch handle and this task registers no route that takes one, the sole entry V1 sends being leg (a)'s compaction command through leg (a)'s own presence check (`Spec-005 §The provider command and skill surface`). **(c) The three flags and the CHECK widening** — `context_compaction`, `provider_commands`, and `output_speed` join `DriverCapabilityFlag` (fourteen → **seventeen**), with `output_speed` declared **`static`** (Claude `true`, Codex `false`) because reading the provider's declared speed state costs a user-message request and `Spec-005 §Capability discovery`'s admissibility test is conjunctive on zero-turn. A driver declaring `output_speed` **also publishes that axis's value set** as `GetCapabilitiesResult.outputSpeedLevels`, declared from the same per-driver table the flag comes from and served through to the client-facing capability read — never obtained by reading the provider, which is the act that makes the flag `static` in the first place. Absent or empty, the axis is unsettable and Plan-016 T2.16's validation refuses fail-closed. The axis's **two carriers land here as well, and neither substitutes for the other**. The REQUEST rides `CreateSessionParams.outputSpeed` and `ResumeSessionParams.outputSpeed` — the resume leg reconstructing it from the durable `runtime_bindings.spawn_config` record T2.6's write seam already persists, as its **fifth** reconstructed data leg on exactly the ground the four before it stand on: a resume is a fresh spawn, so a value that does not re-realize there relaunches at the provider's default while the agent row still records the accepted mode. The DECLARED state is **not** carried on either establishment path (corrected 2026-08-29 at the round-3 fold, where the round-2 wording put it on `ProviderSessionHandle` and `DriverResumeResult`'s `resumed` arm): both resolve before the first turn-bearing exchange, and `Spec-005 §The output-speed axis`'s detection-source ruling establishes that the declaring handshake is emitted only as part of one — a member on either would have been structurally always-absent, a field minted ahead of its producer. The driver instead records the `ProviderOutputSpeedState` **against the binding when the handshake actually arrives**, on the first turn-bearing exchange the participant's own work produces, and holds it for the binding's life; no synthetic turn is spent and neither establishment call blocks on it. Both of its strings stay `wireFreeFormString`-bounded and neither is written to any column, an observation stored being one that outlives the session that made it. Both drivers implement the pair; the Codex leg declares the flag `false` and reports no state, which is the complete declaration rather than a gap. **The detection table grows with the union (2026-08-29, round-3 fold).** T3.24 requires one entry per flag per driver and its totality test fails when the union grows without matching entries, so this leg lands **six** entries in that same table — mechanisms where one is admissible and a named failing conjunct where none is, never an invented probe. `context_compaction` is **`probed`** on the Codex leg through the `-32600` unknown-method enumeration T3.24 already uses (`thread/compact/start` takes `{ threadId }`, the session identity the driver already holds, so unlike the Codex `rollback` entry no parameter-level fact is left unestablished and method acceptance is decisive at consumption granularity) and **`static`** on the Claude leg, where the failing conjunct is **zero-turn**: the `/compact` presence this leg's emulation depends on is knowable only from the session-handshake enumeration, which is turn-bearing. `provider_commands` is likewise **`probed`** on Codex (`skills/list` takes `{ cwds?, forceReload? }`, both optional, so method acceptance is again decisive) and **`static`** on Claude on the same **zero-turn** conjunct, the enumeration riding that same handshake. `output_speed` is **`static`** on both, and the two entries fail **different** conjuncts, which is why each names its own: on Claude **zero-turn** fails, exactly as `Spec-005 §The output-speed axis`'s detection-source ruling states; on Codex the failing conjunct is **decisiveness**, because the driver's only zero-turn channel is the method enumeration and this axis is a handshake-declared state rather than a method, so that channel cannot decide it in either direction — the `false` is a complete declaration from the per-driver table, not an unprobed gap. The `driver_capabilities.capability_flag` CHECK is **already shipped** at fourteen values in migration `0011`, so this leg ships a **next-ordinal table-rebuild migration** in the documented `lang_altertable` shape — widen the CHECK, backfill `supported=0` rows for the three new flags on every existing `driver_name` so the hydrator's exact-cardinality guard still holds, and add no table and no column. **(d) The driver-ask choice set** — where a provider's input-kind ask declares a closed option set (Codex `item/tool/requestUserInput` and MCP elicitation; the Claude control round-trip does not), the normalize boundary reports that set on **this plan's own normalized interactive-request value**, bounded there: a declared cardinality cap and `wireFreeFormString` on each option's `value` and `label`, with an over-large set **dropped and recorded as a driver diagnostic** rather than forwarded — the ask still reaches the participant and the card's unconditional free-text arm still answers it, which is the outcome an interactive-request family must reach, since a set large enough to breach the canonical event ceiling would otherwise fail at append and leave the run waiting on a card no one recorded. **Ownership stops at that value.** The `options` payload member `Spec-006 §Driver Ask Events` registers is written by **Plan-012 T2.8**, the sole emitter of `driver_ask.*` events, across the already-registered CP-005-7 ⇄ CP-012-6 pair; this task authors no Plan-012 symbol and takes no dependency on Plan-012 Phase 1. It is **never synthesized**: absence means the provider offered no choice set this boundary could represent, and a set dropped for size is legible in the driver diagnostics rather than silent.
  - **Spec coverage:** Spec-005 §Desktop Console Parity Surfaces (all three subsections); Spec-005 §Interfaces And Contracts (the eighteen-op surface); Spec-005 §Per-Driver Capability Matrix (the three added rows and their `false`-cell fallbacks); Spec-005 §Required Behavior (the re-derived `driver_command` count and its two guards); Spec-006 §Driver Ask Events (the `options` producer).
  - **Verifies invariant:** I-005-2 (the three flags join the enum whose exhaustiveness that invariant is enforced by); I-005-13.
  - Consumes: T3.23's resolved-executable spawn and T3.24's detection-source reporting (the three flags report through the same surface); T1.7's hoisted `DRIVER_CAPABILITY_FLAGS` const and its `0011` CHECK, which this leg's migration rebuilds.
  - Tests: a `compactContext` whose provider returns its ack and emits **no** compaction frame settles as a failure — asserted on the settlement rather than the call, since the empty ack is precisely what must not count; a Claude-leg dispatch whose `/compact` is **absent** from the enumeration refuses before sending, asserted on the send call count; an enumeration read under one `(driverName, providerAccountId)` is not returned for another; a `SkillsChangedNotification` triggers a full re-read rather than a merge into the held copy; the widened CHECK admits all seventeen values and the backfill leaves every existing `driver_name` at the union's exact cardinality; `output_speed` reports `static` on both drivers and reports the provider's **declared** state rather than the requested one when the two disagree; and an input-kind ask normalized from a provider that declares no choice set carries **no** `options` member rather than an empty array — the assertion that keeps absence meaningful. Added 2026-08-29 at the review fold: a `compactContext` whose provider acknowledges and then emits nothing settles `failed` at the declared bound rather than pending indefinitely, and a compaction frame delivered **after** that settlement still produces a `usage.context_compacted` row — the pair that proves the bound constrains the operation and not the record; a binding disposed mid-wait settles `failed` immediately rather than waiting the bound out; the result type admits no `applied` value without a `boundaryPosition` key and no `capability_undeclared` reason at all, asserted at the schema; and a driver declaring `output_speed: true` while publishing no `outputSpeedLevels` makes the axis unsettable rather than pass-through. Added 2026-08-29 at the round-2 fold: a session created with a requested mode and then resumed spawns the second process **with that same mode**, read from the persisted spawn record and not from the original request — the assertion that a resume does not shed the axis; a provider that accepts the opt-in and declares the mode off yields a **binding-held** declared state and reason that differ from the request, and no path rewrites either into the other; a declared state naming a level absent from `outputSpeedLevels` is carried **verbatim** rather than coerced to off; an option set exceeding the declared cardinality cap, and one whose option string breaches the bound, are both dropped with a diagnostic while the ask itself still normalizes; and an enumeration exceeding the entry cap returns `complete: false` while `compactContext`'s presence check — reading the driver's held enumeration — still finds a command the cap truncated away. Added 2026-08-29 at the round-3 fold: neither `createSession` nor `resumeSession` reports a declared speed state and neither blocks waiting for one — asserted on the transport double as **zero** additional requests, the arm that would otherwise buy the reading with a synthetic turn — while the binding's held state is **absent** until the first turn-bearing exchange delivers the handshake and **present** immediately after, the pair that makes absent-until-observed a tested property rather than a described one; an agent with **two** live bindings yields **two** groups, each carrying its own run, `(driverName, providerAccountId)`, entries, and `complete` flag, and one group truncated leaves the other's flag untouched; a provider entry declared **disabled** is **present** in the returned group rather than filtered out, while an entry from the surface that publishes no such distinction carries **no** `enabled` member rather than a synthesized `true` — the same absence-stays-meaningful assertion the `options` arm already makes; and the detection-mechanism table is **total at seventeen flags per driver** with every added `static` entry naming its failing conjunct, the two `output_speed` entries naming **different** ones (Claude zero-turn, Codex decisiveness), so T3.24's totality suite passes against the grown union rather than failing on it.
  - Estimate: 1 PR

- **Dispatch (T3.23–T3.26).** T3.23–T3.25 dispatch **with Phase 3**, and Phase 3 entry is **not** satisfied by the phase's declared `preconditions:` gates alone. Those gates are met (Phase 1 merged as PR #157, Phase 2 as PR #159, Plan-006 Phase 1 as PR #247), and these tasks mint no new gate and open no phase of their own — but PRs #157 / #159 shipped only T1.1–T1.6 and T2.1–T2.5, leaving the campaign-B10 catch-up tasks **T1.7 / T1.8 and T2.6** unshipped, which is why both phases' Done-Checklist boxes are still open. (Landed-fact annotation, 2026-08-27 — **fully discharged**: T1.7 / T1.8 merged at PR #369, squash `ae194e2e` ([§6 node NS-87](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md#6-active-next-steps-dag)), and T2.6 at PR #372, squash `0c0f276b` ([§6 node NS-88](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md#6-active-next-steps-dag)). Both declared sets now close under declared-⊆-shipped, both Done-Checklist boxes are checked, and this sentence is a dated record of the pre-catch-up tree; Phase 3's `{ plan: 005, phase: 1 }` and `{ plan: 005, phase: 2 }` entries both resolve MET, so this trio dispatches with Phase 3.) T3.23–T3.25 consume exactly those surfaces — the widened flag union and `cliVersion` on the capability contract (T1.7 / T1.8), and the version columns plus the `spawn_config` store seam (T2.6) — so shipping the catch-up trio is a **prerequisite of this trio, not a parallel track**: preflight Gate 5 compares every declared task against the shipment manifest and halts until they land, and bypassing it would author against contract and persistence surfaces that do not yet exist. T3.23 precedes T3.24 and T3.25 within the phase, because both consume its resolved spawn.

- **Dispatch (T3.26).** T3.26 likewise dispatches with Phase 3 and adds no gate of its own: its four legs extend interfaces, drivers, and a migration chain Phase 3 already owns, and it carries **no cross-plan edge at all**. Leg (d) stops at this plan's own normalized interactive-request value and hands the choice set to Plan-012 T2.8 across the already-registered CP-005-7 ⇄ CP-012-6 pair, so nothing here waits on Plan-012 T1.5. That reading is structural rather than cosmetic (2026-08-29): Plan-012 Phase 1 is machine-gated on **this plan's Phase 4**, and Phase 4's T4.9 consumes T3.26, so a T3.26 → Plan-012 Phase 1 edge would have closed the cycle T3.26 → Plan-012 Phase 1 → Plan-005 Phase 4 → T3.26. Correcting the ownership dissolves it rather than gating around it, and the corrected reading is also the accurate one: a driver reports what a provider declared, and the emitter decides what the durable event carries.

**Phase 3 Acceptance Mapping:** T3.1 + T3.6 lifecycle tests → `Spec-005 §Acceptance Criteria` (AC1 — driver-integration semantics-preserving). T3.3 + T3.8 capability declaration tests → `Spec-005 §Acceptance Criteria` (AC2 — undeclared capabilities not invocable). T3.1 + T3.6 resume-failure tests → `Spec-005 §Acceptance Criteria` (AC3 — recovery-needed condition surfaces). T3.18 neutralization + tripwire tests → `Spec-005 §Acceptance Criteria` (AC8 — command-shaped participant text reaches the model as prose, or the turn fails visibly). T3.19 projection + T3.20 replay-assertion tests → `Spec-005 §Acceptance Criteria` (AC9 — a reconstituted session carries every participant-visible fact, with stable tool-call identity and no unpaired call). T3.20 refusal-path + T3.21 memo-floor tests → `Spec-005 §Acceptance Criteria` (AC10 — the three replay-unavailable triggers all settle on the memo projection and report `degraded`). T3.19 strip-stage tests → `Spec-005 §Acceptance Criteria` (AC11 — private reasoning exported nowhere and declared on every dropping operation, visible summaries carried as plain text). T3.20 capability-discovery tests → `Spec-005 §Acceptance Criteria` (AC12 — every client-invocable capability is one `getCapabilities` declares). T3.23 floor-gate + T3.24 withdrawal tests → `Spec-005 §Acceptance Criteria` (AC13 — a build above the pin attaches, and only a capability whose probe refuses is withdrawn). T3.24 probe-cost + negative-control tests → `Spec-005 §Acceptance Criteria` (AC14 — probes complete before the first billed turn and spend none, and the negative control is still refused). T3.24 mechanism-table totality + non-interference tests → `Spec-005 §Acceptance Criteria` (AC16 — every flag declares a mechanism, each `static` entry names its failing conjunct, and no driver-side probe issues the set-replacing MCP mutation). T3.23 in-band-read + T3.25 child-environment tests → `Spec-005 §Acceptance Criteria` (AC15 — the recorded version is the one the spawned process reported, and every driver-spawned child carries the auto-update opt-out). T3.25 posture-realization tests → `Spec-005 §Acceptance Criteria` (AC17 — exactly one member of a mutually-exclusive posture pair is realized, and a turn carrying the other is refused rather than sent). T3.11 usage-delta tests → `Spec-005 §Acceptance Criteria` (AC18 — session spend equals the newest cumulative reading minus the base, axes never double-count a cached token, and a late-arriving usage frame is differenced against its own turn's base). T3.11 thread-routing tests → `Spec-005 §Acceptance Criteria` (AC19 — a child-thread frame never reaches the parent timeline, an unidentifiable frame is quarantined rather than projected, and child spend lands on the child's attribution or the parent run rather than disappearing). T3.26 legs (a) and (b) → `Spec-005 §Acceptance Criteria` (AC20, AC21). T3.26 leg (b) → (AC22, the live-read and routing half). T3.26 leg (c) → (AC23).

### Phase 3B — Provider-account seam + typed usage-limit signal

**Goal:** the driver contract carries the two seams the account plane needs — an opaque account identity threaded through spawn and resume, and a typed provider usage-limit signal that is recognized rather than guessed.

A supplement phase rather than growth on the shipped Phases 1–2, following the campaign-B10 catch-up precedent this plan already uses: both legs are **additive-optional** widenings of contract surfaces whose code has merged (PRs #157 / #159), so no shipped field changes type and no shipped call site is required to change. Homing them here keeps the shipped phases' manifest entries truthful — a reopened phase would claim the merged PRs shipped work they did not.

<!-- prettier-ignore -->
```yaml
preconditions:
  - { type: plan_phase, plan: 005, phase: 3, status: merged }
```

#### Tasks

- **T3.16 — Typed provider usage-limit signal (2026-08-18).**
  - **Files:** `packages/contracts/src/provider-driver.ts` (EXTEND), `packages/runtime-daemon/src/provider/drivers/codex/event-normalizer.ts` (EXTEND), `packages/runtime-daemon/src/provider/drivers/claude/event-normalizer.ts` (EXTEND) — paths corrected 2026-08-25 to the canonical `provider/drivers/` topology this plan owns ([cross-plan-dependencies.md §2](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md#2-package-path-ownership-map)); the earlier `src/drivers/` spelling named a directory that does not exist.
  - **Provides:** the signal shape as a **sibling axis beside `RecoveryCondition`** — a closed-set cause plus an optional reset boundary carrying its own provenance, so a consumer can tell a provider-stated boundary from a runtime-derived one — and the two driver normalizer legs that emit it. Recognition is typed-only: each leg keys on a structured provider event it can name, and no leg matches message prose, exit codes, or a bare HTTP status. An unrecognized shape emits nothing. The Codex leg normalizes the account rate-limit read and its update notification, whose shapes are published on the provider's own wire surface; the Claude leg normalizes the retry notification, which is the weaker of the two — it is recorded at **Derived** trust on the provider-wire trust axis and its reset boundary is marked runtime-derived rather than provider-stated, because the notification does not itself carry a documented boundary field. Adds **no** capability flag: like `probeAuth`, the obligation is uniform across drivers, and a driver with nothing to report emits nothing rather than declaring a false negative capability. The V1 capability matrix is therefore restated **unwidened** by this task.
  - **Consumes:** T1.2 contract surfaces; T3.5 and T3.10 normalizers.
  - **Spec coverage:** Spec-005 §Fallback Behavior; Spec-005 §Non-Goals.
  - **Verifies invariant:** I-005-6.
  - **Tests:** each driver leg emits the signal from its named structured event, with the Claude leg's boundary marked runtime-derived and the Codex leg's provider-stated; a **seeded discriminating control** feeds each normalizer a prose message and an exit code that a text-matching implementation would classify as a usage limit, and requires **no** signal — an implementation that infers from text fails here and only here; an unparseable or unrecognized shape yields no signal rather than a default-caused one; the signal is never emitted as a `RecoveryCondition` member and no consumer path treats the two as interchangeable; the capability matrix is unchanged by this task.
- **T3.17 — Provider-account identity threading at spawn and resume (2026-08-18).**
  - **Files:** `packages/contracts/src/provider-driver.ts` (EXTEND), `packages/runtime-daemon/src/provider/runtime-binding-store.ts` (EXTEND) — path corrected 2026-08-25 (the shipped file is under `provider/`, singular, as the §2 ownership row records).
  - **Provides:** the additive-optional account identifier on the session-creation and resume parameter shapes, persisted through the existing `runtime_bindings.spawn_config` carrier so a resumed run rebinds to the account it was admitted against rather than to whatever the current default happens to be. The identifier is opaque to the driver and is never parsed or used to locate credential material. The daemon supplies the constructed credential environment at spawn, and the driver reads no ambient provider credentials when an account is bound. Omitting the identifier is the unchanged pre-amendment path.
  - **Consumes:** T1.2 contract surfaces; T2.6 store seams; the Plan-029 resolver via CP-005-9.
  - **Spec coverage:** Spec-005 §Interfaces And Contracts.
  - **Verifies invariant:** none — the fail-closed binding rule is Plan-029's I-029-3 and the reserved-variable rule is its I-029-4, consumed here per CP-005-9 rather than restated as Plan-005 invariants.
  - **Tests:** a create with no identifier behaves byte-identically to the pre-amendment path (the additive-optional proof); an identifier round-trips through `spawn_config` and a resume rebinds to it after the provider default has been changed mid-session; a client-supplied identifier is treated as an input to resolution and the server-resolved value is what is recorded; with an account bound, a spawn whose constructed environment is asserted over its full variable set carries no ambient credential inheritance.

### Phase 4 — Client SDK exposure + degraded-fallback

**Goal:** Author the typed SDK surface for capability + intervention controls under a dedicated `driver.*` JSON-RPC namespace (per Plan-007 CP-007-6). Daemon-side IPC handlers + SDK-side Zod-validated wrappers ship together. Subscription primitive reuses `LocalSubscriptionProducer<T>` per Plan-007 CP-007-4 precedent.

<!-- prettier-ignore -->
```yaml
preconditions:
  # Local self-gates (Codex round 6): the same reopened-prerequisite class as Phase 3's —
  # T4.8's exposure consume rides T1.8's carriers (P3-4) and T2.6's cliVersion hydration,
  # so Phase 4 gates on the reopened Phases 1-2 catch-up. Deliberately NOT gated on
  # Phase 3: the round-1 decision keeping Phase 4 parallel to Phase 3 stands.
  - { type: plan_phase, plan: 005, phase: 1, status: merged }
  - { type: plan_phase, plan: 005, phase: 2, status: merged }
```

**Ratified design decisions (Tier 4 audit, 2026-05-27):**

1. **Factory pattern**: daemon-only `createDaemonProviderClient(JsonRpcClient): DriverClient` — no control-plane variant. Aligns with `Spec-005 §Required Behavior` (driver authority is local-daemon-resident; no V1.1 remote-provider workflow on roadmap). Matches MCP/LSP best practice (single-transport for local-only capability servers).
2. **Namespace**: dedicated `driver.*` (added to Plan-007 §Tier 4 namespace registry per CP-007-6). **9 client-facing method names** (7 from the 2026-05-27 ratification, 2 added 2026-08-29) follow canonical long-form per ADR-009 + Plan-007 I-007-9: `driver.listCapabilities`, `driver.interruptRun`, `driver.applyIntervention`, `driver.respondToRequest`, `driver.listModels`, `driver.listModes`, `driver.compactContext`, `driver.listProviderCommands`, plus the subscription method `driver.subscribeEvents`. **Extension-with-rationale (desktop-console parity amendment, 2026-08-29):** the two added verbs are admitted by the governing principle below rather than as exceptions to it — `compactContext` and `listProviderCommands` both operate on an already-existing session, and neither establishes, restores, starts, nor tears down a session-or-run domain object, so neither is orchestration-owned under the rule that narrowed this set from eleven to seven. They differ from the R8 parity operations kept daemon-internal below in the one respect that decided those: those verbs each had an existing client route (`rollbackTo` through Plan-004's `rollback` intervention, the goal pair through Plan-016's goal service), and these have none — a composer affordance and a command autocomplete can be reached by no verb this namespace already carries, and inventing a sixth intervention for compaction would widen `Spec-004`'s deliberately closed V1 control set. Registration rides the existing CP-007-6 obligation, whose enumeration is updated in the same swap on a **recorded no-flip** for Plan-007: what that plan builds is the `register()` substrate, which is unchanged, and the count there is descriptive of this plan's consumption. **Reversal-with-rationale (Codex round-3 review, PR #124):** the 2026-05-27 ratified design originally exposed all 10 driver-contract operations + `subscribeEvents` (11) over the client JSON-RPC namespace. The four session/run **lifecycle** operations — `driver.createSession`, `driver.resumeSession`, `driver.startRun`, `driver.closeSession` — are now **daemon-internal**: invoked only by the session/run orchestration layer on the in-daemon `ProviderRegistry` (T2.3), never registered as client-callable JSON-RPC methods. Governing principle: a `driver.*` method is client-facing iff it operates on an already-existing session/run (capability introspection, intervention/control, model/mode reads, event subscription); methods that **establish, restore, start, or tear down** a session-or-run domain object are orchestration-owned, because orchestration must persist the runtime binding and emit the canonical lifecycle event around the driver call. This honors `Spec-005 §Example Flows` (the driver "only sees `interruptRun` followed later by `startRun`" — those lifecycle calls are driven BY orchestration, not by clients) and the Phase-4 goal above ("capability + intervention controls"). Exposing the lifecycle verbs as client-callable would let a renderer/CLI start or destroy a provider session/run while bypassing the orchestration that owns `runtime_bindings` persistence + canonical `run.*` event emission. The in-daemon `ProviderDriver` interface (T1.1's ten ratified ops — the B10 T1.8 adds four more, all daemon-internal per T4.8) is unchanged by this narrowing — only its client JSON-RPC exposure narrows. Clients reach lifecycle indirectly through the orchestration namespaces (`session.*` per Plan-001 — `session.create` maps to `directoryService.createSession` per `docs/architecture/contracts/api-payload-contracts.md §Tier 1 (cont.): Plan-008 — Plan-008-Bootstrap (control-plane tRPC + SSE substrate)`, NOT `driver.createSession`; `run.*` per Plan-004).
3. **Envelope shape**: Zod-validated `DriverInterventionResult` at SDK seam (`{ status: 'applied' | 'degraded'; fallbackAction?: string }`) mirroring driver-internal shape (`Spec-005 §Required Behavior`, [Spec-005 §Fallback Behavior](../specs/005-provider-driver-contract-and-capabilities.md#fallback-behavior-1)) with wire-level validation. Distinct from orchestration `InterventionState` lifecycle enum per LSP/MCP separation-of-concerns precedent.
4. **Subscription primitive**: `driver.subscribeEvents` returns `LocalSubscriptionProducer<DriverEvent>` per Plan-007 CP-007-4 (the shared streaming primitive). `DriverEvent` is the Plan-006-owned union of existing event-category types relevant to driver runtime — `run_lifecycle`, `assistant_output`, `tool_activity`, `interactive_request`, `artifact_publication`, `usage_telemetry`, and `runtime_node_lifecycle` (the last category supplies `runtime_node.capability_declared` + `runtime_node.capability_updated` for driver-capability events per CP-005-5; no new category is introduced). `usage_telemetry` is the canonical category name per [Spec-006 §Usage Telemetry](../specs/006-session-event-taxonomy-and-audit-log.md#usage-telemetry-usage_telemetry) and `packages/contracts/src/event.ts` literal union.
5. **Recovery-needed surface**: surfaced via a typed return-value contract from `resumeSession()` (T3.1, T3.6), then propagated by Plan-015's recovery dispatcher to the existing `run.failed` event with `recoveryCondition: 'recovery-needed'` per `Spec-006 §Run Lifecycle (run_lifecycle)`. Plan-005 does NOT emit a separate `driver.recovery_needed` event — see CP-005-5 for the full handoff sequence.

Without this the client-facing `driver.listCapabilities` reply would carry `output_speed: true` with no values to render, and Plan-016 T2.16's fail-closed rule would make the axis permanently unsettable for a cache-served client — a stored capability turning into an unsettable one, which is the outcome that rule exists to prevent, not to cause.

The `reauth-required` widening consumes C-5's mid-run `authentication_failed`. T4.7 is the return-value **contract-test** leg that asserts the `DriverResumeResult.failed` carrier shape — not a recovery dispatcher (dispatch onto `run.failed` is Plan-015's, per CP-005-5).

The seven methods ratified in 2026-05-27 are unchanged **by that campaign**; the set later grew to nine on 2026-08-29 for two verbs with no existing client route at all (§Phase 4 decision #2), which is the distinction this note turns on rather than an exception to it.

- **T4.9 — Client-facing `driver.compactContext` and `driver.listProviderCommands` (EXTENDs T4.1 / T4.2 / T4.3, 2026-08-29).**
  - Files: `packages/contracts/src/provider-driver.ts` (EXTEND from T4.2 — the SDK-seam Zod schemas the two verbs validate against: `CompactContextRequestSchema`, `DriverCompactionResultSchema`, `ListProviderCommandsRequestSchema`, `ProviderCommandEntrySchema`, `ProviderCommandListResultSchema`; named here rather than left implied, because a wire verb whose schema has no owning file is a verb that ships unvalidated. The two **request** schemas are the run- and agent-addressed wire shapes, not the binding-addressed driver params — those cross no wire and are composed by this handler after resolution); `packages/runtime-daemon/src/ipc/handlers/driver-handlers.ts` (EXTEND from T4.1 — the same handler module, never a second one); `packages/runtime-daemon/src/ipc/registry.ts` (EXTEND — two `register()` calls); `packages/client-sdk/src/` (EXTEND — the two SDK methods on T4.3's factory).
  - Provides: the eighth and ninth client-facing `driver.*` verbs, registered against the Plan-007 namespace registry under CP-007-6 (whose enumeration this swap updates on a recorded no-flip for Plan-007). Both are Zod-validated at the SDK seam on T4.2's pattern. `driver.compactContext` returns the settlement T3.26 leg (a) produces and never a bare acknowledgment. `driver.listProviderCommands` returns the enumeration **with each entry's binding** as the `ProviderCommandListResult` envelope, whose `complete` flag makes a cap-truncated read distinguishable from a provider that published little, so the routing invariant is enforced at the daemon rather than trusted to the renderer. **Neither verb takes a `bindingId` (2026-08-29).** No client-facing read publishes one anywhere, so a binding-addressed request would be unconstructible from every response a client can obtain. Each verb instead takes the identifier its own authorization and its own consumer already use, and the handler resolves the live binding: `driver.compactContext` takes `{ sessionId, runId }` because the run is what its `Action::"intervene"` evaluation adjudicates, and `driver.listProviderCommands` takes `{ sessionId, agentId }` because it is a membership-only read whose consumer is the agent-addressed composer (Plan-023 CP-023-8). Resolution refuses in a fixed order on **already-registered codes only** — `session.not_found`, then `run.not_found` / `agent.not_found`, then `driver.unavailable` where no live binding exists — so the addressing mints nothing, and a caller naming another leg's binding is not merely refused but **unrepresentable**, which is strictly stronger than the daemon-interior dispatch check that still stands beneath it. An invocation against a driver declaring the gating flag `false` refuses the same way — the already-registered code, on T4.6's existing undeclared-capability path; **no error code is minted**. **Authorization, added 2026-08-29, runs at this handler before the capability gate and before any driver call, and mints nothing.** A caller who is not an **active participant** of the named session is refused the already-registered `session.not_found` on both verbs — one byte-identical refusal for a non-member and for an unknown session, so neither verb is a session-existence oracle. `driver.listProviderCommands` requires nothing further: any active role reads. `driver.compactContext` additionally runs the **identical `Action::"intervene"` evaluation** the intervention path runs, on the run owning the named binding — the `run.pause` / `run.resume` reuse, not a new action — and a deny settles on the operation's own `refused` arm as `not_permitted` rather than as a JSON-RPC error, the shape an authorization-rejected intervention already uses. I-004-19's non-local hosting-node leg is unreachable here by construction: a binding this daemon holds is hosted on this node, so this task takes no dependency on the per-run hosting-node carrier Plan-004 still holds open. **No Cedar action, no permission-matrix row, and no error code is minted.**
  - **Spec coverage:** Spec-005 §Interfaces And Contracts (the two operations and the eighteen-op surface); Spec-005 §Capability discovery (the client-facing set at nine); Spec-005 §Desktop Console Parity Surfaces.
  - **Verifies invariant:** I-005-2 (both verbs refuse against an undeclared flag); I-005-13.
  - Consumes: T3.26's operations and flags; T4.1's handler module and T4.5's capability cache.
  - Tests: each verb refuses with `driver.capability_unsupported` against a driver declaring its flag `false`, asserted through the registered SDK client rather than the handler directly; a non-member caller receives a refusal **byte-identical** to the one an unknown session id produces, asserted on both verbs by comparing the two responses rather than by matching a code; a viewer-role member reads the enumeration successfully and is refused `not_permitted` on `compactContext` — the pair that proves the two verbs carry different gates — with the driver call count asserted zero on the refusal, so authorization is shown to precede both the capability gate and the dispatch; a `listProviderCommands` result's entries each carry a binding, and the wire request type admits **no** binding member at all — asserted at the schema, since the round-2 addressing makes the cross-binding request unconstructible rather than merely refused, while the driver-side dispatch check keeps its own separate assertion that a binding mismatch refuses before any provider call; a request naming a run of another session, one naming an agent of another session, and one naming a target holding no live binding refuse as `run.not_found`, `agent.not_found`, and `driver.unavailable` respectively, with the driver call count asserted zero; an enumeration truncated at the entry cap returns `complete: false` rather than a short array indistinguishable from a small one; the namespace registry rejects a duplicate registration of either name, which is the assertion that catches a second handler module being added instead of this one being extended.
  - Estimate: 1 PR

**Phase 4 Acceptance Mapping:** T4.6 → `Spec-005 §Acceptance Criteria` (AC2). T4.7 → `Spec-005 §Acceptance Criteria` (AC3). T4.3 SDK exposure → `Spec-005 §Acceptance Criteria` (AC1) for the externally-observable contract. T4.9 → `Spec-005 §Acceptance Criteria` (AC22, the cross-binding-dispatch-refusal half, and AC24, the wire-boundary authorization of both verbs).

### Phase 5 — MCP Task-Handle Durability (gated `command_receipts` EXTEND, campaign B10)

**Goal:** Land the durable MCP Tasks recovery handle — the additive nullable `command_receipts.mcp_task_id` column plus its canonical-doc mirrors — and activate T3.13's dormant receipt-write seam. Split out of Phase 3 (Codex round 3, 2026-07-20) because the ALTER's substrate is Plan-004 Phase 1's `command_receipts` CREATE: folding that gate into Phase 3 would serialize the entire driver phase behind Plan-004's schedule, while a gate-less Phase 3 could dispatch an ALTER against a table that does not exist yet. The narrow leg gets its own phase with both orderings machine-checked; Phases 3–4 stay dispatchable on their own preconditions.

<!-- prettier-ignore -->
```yaml
preconditions:
  # Plan-004 Phase 1 CREATEs command_receipts — the substrate this phase's ALTER extends
  # (the cross-plan-dependencies.md section-1 command_receipts row). Plan-004 Phase 1 itself gates on
  # { plan: 005, phase: 1 }, so the chain is acyclic at phase granularity:
  # 005.P1 -> 004.P1 -> 005.P5.
  - { type: external_plan_phase_merged, plan: 004, phase: 1 }
  # Phase 3 ships the two tools.ts receipt-write seams this phase activates — T3.13 authors
  # them DORMANT (no mcp_task_id write; recovery stays on the manual_reconcile_only halt)
  # until this migration lands.
  - { type: plan_phase, plan: 005, phase: 3, status: merged }
```

#### Tasks

- **T5.1 — MCP task-handle migration + receipt-write activation (EXTENDs Plan-004's `command_receipts` per dep-map §1, campaign B10).**
  - Files: a new additive Plan-005-owned migration `packages/runtime-daemon/src/migrations/000M-command-receipt-mcp-task-handle.ts` (+ its `migration-runner.ts` guarded-block registration; number allocated at branch time per the T1.7 allocation rule, and the `{ plan: 004, phase: 1 }` gate above additionally orders the ALTER after Plan-004 Phase 1's `command_receipts` CREATE — never a Plan-004 migration edit, never reshaping Plan-004's columns); `packages/runtime-daemon/src/provider/drivers/codex/tools.ts` + `packages/runtime-daemon/src/provider/drivers/claude/tools.ts` (EXTEND T3.13 — flip the dormant receipt-write seam live: record the handle at task-augmented dispatch); `docs/architecture/schemas/local-sqlite-schema.md` (verify the shipped column + CHECK against the canonical `command_receipts` block) + `docs/architecture/contracts/api-payload-contracts.md` (verify the durable-handle doc-mirror at the two-phase command-receipt protocol)
  - **Spec coverage:** Spec-005 §Tool Metadata (the durable-task-handle carve-out; the floor's trust rule is untouched — the handle refines recovery dispatch, never the class); Spec-005 §Recovery Consequences (receipt recovery dispatched on `idempotency_class`)
  - **Verifies invariant:** I-005-3 (the floor holds around the carve-out: a crash before the acceptance response is durably stored leaves `mcp_task_id` NULL and the call on the `manual_reconcile_only` halt)
  - Consumes: Plan-004 Phase 1 `command_receipts` CREATE (machine-gated above); T3.13's dormant receipt-write seam; T1.7's migration-number allocation rule
  - Provides: the nullable `command_receipts.mcp_task_id` column, bounded `CHECK (mcp_task_id IS NULL OR (length(mcp_task_id) > 0 AND length(mcp_task_id) <= 256 AND instr(mcp_task_id, char(0)) = 0))` — the receiver-generated `taskId` is untrusted remote-peer output, so it is bounded at the DB layer AND the write-seam guard exactly like the persisted provider-declared strings (the T2.1 defense-in-depth convention; Codex round 3); the live receipt-write at task-augmented dispatch; the Spec-015 recovery-poll handle (`tasks/get` → `tasks/result`) replacing the halt where the handle exists. Tests assert the dormant pre-state (Phase-3 seam writes no handle before this migration) and the active-state semantics (handle recorded on acceptance; NULL on crash-before-acceptance keeps the halt).
  - Estimate: 1 PR

**Phase 5 Acceptance Mapping:** no dedicated `Spec-005 §Acceptance Criteria` row — the carve-out rides `Spec-005 §Recovery Consequences`, and the floor's load-bearing negative test (I-005-3) stays at T3.13/P2-7.

### Campaign B10 Amendment — CLI Currency + R8 Parity Driver Tasks (2026-07-19)

**Classification: FLIP (`approved` → `review`, per the runbook [§Status Flip Rule](../operations/plan-implementation-readiness-audit-runbook.md#status-flip-rule) rows 3–4).** This amendment discharges the earlier campaign forward-notes' promise (the amended Spec-005 surface's operations, per-driver implementations, normalizers, and conformance tests) as `#### Tasks` folded into Phases 1–4 plus the round-3-added gated Phase 5 — T1.7 / T1.8 (Phase 1), T2.6 (Phase 2), T3.11–T3.15 (Phase 3), T4.8 (Phase 4), T5.1 (Phase 5). The bundle drafted as additive, but the 2026-07-20 review round surfaced two changes that push it past the additive default into a flip: **(a) a new cross-plan dependency (row 3)** — Plan-005 Phase 3 now consumes Plan-006 Phase 1's `EVENT_DISPOSITION_BY_KIND` as the single normalization-disposition source, adding a Plan-005 → Plan-006 header dependency, a machine-checkable Phase-3 gate, and a [cross-plan-dependencies.md §3](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md#3-inter-plan-dependency-graph) edge, plus an additive Plan-005 EXTEND of Plan-004's `command_receipts` (the `mcp_task_id` recovery handle) recorded in [§1](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md#1-table-ownership-map); and **(b) mandatory driver behaviors in the plan body (row 4)** — the fail-closed subagent-at-spawn branch (T3.15 leg 4), the `approvalsReviewer: "user"` pin (T3.15), the native-cap `unpriced_native_cap` cost provenance (T3.11), and the reorder-overflow diagnostic + `driver.reorder_buffer.overflow` metric (T3.11). The 2026-07-20 round-3 review additionally split the `command_receipts` EXTEND into a **new gated Phase 5** (T5.1 — the `mcp_task_id` migration + receipt-write activation, machine-gated on Plan-004 Phase 1 + this plan's Phase 3), so the amendment DOES add a phase; no new plan invariant is introduced, and every R8 event still reuses an existing Spec-006 surface (`run_lifecycle` / `session_lifecycle` / `tool_activity` / `usage_telemetry` / `runtime_node_lifecycle`, CP-005-5). Status is flipped to `review` and restored to `approved` by **Plan-005's W2.5 targeted re-audit** (the promotion vehicle); the README plan-status census is re-derived in this same commit (19 → 18 `approved`, 7 → 8 `review`, 1 `completed`). Governing docs: capability-enhancement campaign [design §4 W2 B10 row + §5.1 parity matrix](../superpowers/specs/2026-07-01-capability-enhancements-design.md), campaign plan Task 14.

**Count-claim re-derivation.** T1.7 / T1.8 widen the ratified ten-op / seven-flag base to the fourteen-op / thirteen-flag post-campaign surface, and T3.19 widens **both** axes again (2026-08-26): the flag axis to fourteen (`transcript_replay`) and the **operation** axis to sixteen (`exportTranscript` / `replayTranscript`, [Spec-005 §Interfaces And Contracts](../specs/005-provider-driver-contract-and-capabilities.md#interfaces-and-contracts)). T3.26 widens both again (2026-08-29): the flag axis to **seventeen** (`context_compaction` / `provider_commands` / `output_speed`) and the operation axis to **eighteen** (`compactContext` / `listProviderCommands`). So a task-scoped claim naming what T1.7 / T1.8 themselves deliver stays at thirteen flags and fourteen ops and is not re-derived, while an unqualified claim about the canonical matrix reads **seventeen** and one about the canonical interface reads **eighteen**. The operation axis is called out beside the flag axis deliberately: the 2026-08-26 amendment moved two sets, and a rule naming only one of them would license exactly the stale-ordinal claim it exists to prevent. Whole-interface totals across Phases 1–4 (the Phase-1 Goal, the T1.3 wrapper rationale, the Phase-4 client-exposure reversal note, and the T4.1 / T4.3 spec-coverage lines) are re-derived to the post-campaign total here; each base task's own-deliverable count (T1.1 ten ops, T1.2 seven flags, and the T1.5 conformance test pinning the seven-flag base) stays at the ratified base it ships. The count re-derivations are themselves citation/consistency updates (additive); the amendment's overall `approved` → `review` flip is driven by the new cross-plan dependency + mandatory driver behaviors (see Classification above), not by these count updates.

**Scope (counts re-derived from the B10 phase tasks above — T1.7 / T1.8, T2.6, T3.11–T3.15, T4.8, T5.1).** **Eighteen corpus-item enrichments** — P0-1, P0-2, P0-3, P0-4, P0-5, P1-1, P1-2, P1-4, P1-5, P1-6, P2-1, P2-6, P2-7, P2-9, P2-10-L1, P3-1, P3-3, P3-4. **Ten CLI-currency duties** — C-1, C-4, C-5, C-7, C-8, C-12, C-14, C-16, C-17, and the regenerate-bindings rule (C-2 / C-3 additionally appear in T3.14's P1-4 interactive-request mechanisms, and C-9 as T3.11's P0-1 rate-limit wire basis — mechanism citations for those corpus items, not counted among the ten currency duties). **Seven R8 parity driver legs** — rollback, session goals, callback-tool hosting, `subagentPolicy` pass-through, `executionPosture` mapping, the transport axis, and `realtime_*` suppression. Plus the P2-5 `Reference:` lines on T3.5–T3.10 and the P-2 phantom fix at T4.1 (applied in place — the prior `ipc/router.ts` cite was a file no plan creates). Each is named at the task that owns it. The 2026-07-20 review round additionally hardened four items into the flip above: the `approvalsReviewer: "user"` pin (T3.15) and the normalized `outputSchema` structured-output parameter + per-driver mapping legs (T1.8 / T3.12) extend the parity surface; the native-cap `unpriced_native_cap` provenance completes P1-6, and the fail-closed subagent-at-spawn branch completes leg 4's `subagentPolicy` mediation.

**Consumed contracts (all post-W1.5, `approved` 2026-07-18).** [Spec-005](../specs/005-provider-driver-contract-and-capabilities.md) §Required Behavior / §Interfaces And Contracts / §Per-Driver Capability Matrix / §Tool Metadata / §Fallback Behavior (the landed B3 + PR-F4 surface: `cliVersion` / `DriverCliVersionReport`, `probeAuth` / `DriverAuthProbeResult`, `clientIdempotencyKey`, the closed `RecoveryCondition` set `{ recovery-needed, reauth-required }`, the resume union, the MCP `manual_reconcile_only` floor + its two Part-B carve-outs, the parity mechanism grades, and the `rollbackTo` / goal / callback-tool / `subagentPolicy` / `executionPosture` / transport surfaces); the Plan-006 disposition registry `EVENT_DISPOSITION_BY_KIND` (the disposition map Plan-006 Phase 1 adds to `packages/contracts/src/event.ts` per [Plan-006](../plans/006-session-event-taxonomy-and-audit-log.md) Phase 1 — Contracts, campaign B11; the code leg lands with Plan-006's Phase 1, this plan consumes it) as the **single source of normalization-disposition truth**; [Spec-006](../specs/006-session-event-taxonomy-and-audit-log.md) §Run Lifecycle (`run_lifecycle`) / §Usage Telemetry (`usage_telemetry`) / §Tool Activity (`tool_activity`) (B1 registrations — `usage.rate_limit_update`, `subagent.*`, `run.rolled_back`, the reserved `realtime_*` family); [Spec-012 §Required Behavior](../specs/012-approvals-permissions-and-trust-boundaries.md#required-behavior) (execution-posture authorization semantics, B20); [Spec-016 §Budget Policies](../specs/016-multi-agent-channels-and-orchestration.md#budget-policies) (3-tier cost + goal/subagent orchestration, B6); and the B19 version-pinned provider-wire references [`codex.md`](../reference/provider-wire/codex.md) + [`claude.md`](../reference/provider-wire/claude.md), each cited at the pin its own §Version pin table records rather than at a number restated here.

**CLI-currency re-verification (Design §3.4 rules 1–3).** Every CLI-mechanism claim below is re-verified at authoring time against the binaries the [provider-wire reference](../reference/provider-wire/README.md) family pins, read from that family rather than restated here (behaviors pin by version anchor, never date; the driver's own admission floors are Spec-005 §Required Behavior's). Codex protocol claims are regenerated from the binary's own `codex app-server generate-ts` / `generate-json-schema` output (canonical over prose; hand-transcription prohibited); Claude `--help` is non-authoritative — the docs census governs. Provider rewind methods were re-verified 2026-08-26 and the Codex leg is **resolved**: `thread/rollback` carries its deprecation in the generated type itself, and the non-experimental successor is `thread/fork` at an inclusive `lastTurnId` boundary, to which Spec-005 §Parity Capability Mechanism Grades rebinds the rollback row — so T3.15 leg 1 binds the successor and registers no `thread/rollback` handler ([`codex.md`](../reference/provider-wire/codex.md)). The Claude leg stays open: `--resume-session-at` is binary-probe-only and absent from the live CLI reference, so before any driver code invoking that rewind path lands the implementer re-verifies it against the then-installed binary per [ADR-017](../decisions/017-shared-event-sourcing-scope.md) Decision Log and [`claude.md`](../reference/provider-wire/claude.md) §Gaps recorded. The daemon turn-snapshot file leg ([Plan-010](../plans/010-worktree-lifecycle-and-execution-modes.md)) is the durable path regardless of the provider leg.

**No-silent-loss + fail-open/fail-closed boundary (governs every normalizer and parser task).** A wire kind the driver recognizes but cannot map to a registered `SessionEventType` — an unknown-but-parseable kind outside the census, an interim `typePending` kind whose contracts registration has not yet landed (the nine B18-pending kinds — literals minted in Spec-006 by the 2026-07-22 B18 amendment; the registry flips at Plan-006 T1.10), **or** a flipped kind whose `SessionEventSchema` payload variant has not yet landed (Plan-006 T1.10's flip-≠-emission rule) — is **fail-open at the parse boundary**: it routes to the T3.11 default-branch diagnostic and is **never dropped and never forced into an envelope against a missing type or a missing union variant**, exactly the seam Plan-006's `EVENT_DISPOSITION_BY_KIND` and its exhaustiveness test delegate to the Plan-005 normalizer. The nine kinds' payload variants are Plan-005-owned (the emitter-authors-payload precedent): each variant registers through the `SessionEventSchema` union-registration seam with — or before — the first normalizer change that emits its kind, so emission turns on variant-by-variant, never by the T1.10 registry flip alone (duty recorded 2026-07-22, the B18 PR's review round). The variant duty carries the Plan-022 call-site PII obligation with it: the two free-text-bearing kinds' emitters route their classified fields through the Plan-022 `splitPii` splitter into `pii_payload` per the field-by-field classifications recorded on their Spec-006 rows (`session.notice.message`; `session.renamed.name`/`previousName`), with splitter tests landing beside each variant — generic error sanitization is not a PII classification, and a variant whose classified fields bypass the splitter does not ship (B18 round 2, 2026-07-22). A **malformed frame** — bytes that do not parse as a valid provider wire message at all — is **fail-closed**: the driver rejects it with a typed parse error rather than fabricating a partial event. Provider correlation identifiers (`tool_use_id`, `parent_tool_use_id`, Codex item/call ids) are copied **verbatim** onto the normalized event — never reformatted, re-cased, re-padded, or re-encoded — because tool-event pairing is by exact-string match and a reformatted correlation id is the single most common documented cause of pairing failure.
