**Where the B10 tasks live.** The driver work these forward-notes promise is folded directly into the per-phase `#### Tasks` blocks above — the Phase 1/2 contract catch-up as **T1.7 / T1.8** (Phase 1) and **T2.6** (Phase 2), the Codex + Claude driver enrichment as **T3.11–T3.15** (Phase 3), the SDK/handler exposure as **T4.8** (Phase 4), and the gated `command_receipts` EXTEND as **T5.1** (Phase 5 — added by the 2026-07-20 round-3 fold) — so `/plan-execution` extracts them from the per-phase task DAGs (a `#### Campaign Phase` subhead outside a `### Phase N` section is invisible to the Gate-3 task oracle, so none is used here). This section retains only the cross-cutting governance above: classification, count-claim re-derivation, scope, consumed contracts, CLI-currency re-verification, and the no-silent-loss / fail-open-fail-closed boundary that governs every normalizer and parser task.

## Parallelization Notes

- Phase 1 (contracts) must complete before Phase 2-4 begin.
- Phase 2 (registry + persistence) blocks Phase 3 (drivers consume the registry).
- Phase 3 Codex + Claude tasks can proceed in parallel once T2.3 ProviderRegistry is shipped.
- Phase 4 SDK exposure can proceed in parallel with Phase 3 driver implementation once T2.3 + T2.4 ship — Phase 4 daemon-side IPC handlers depend on the registry, not on the specific drivers being implemented. (Both Phase 3 and Phase 4 carry local `plan_phase` self-gates on the reopened Phases 1–2 — the B10 catch-up tasks must merge first — while Phase 4 stays ungated on Phase 3, Codex round 6.)
- Phase 5 (MCP task-handle durability) is independent of Phase 4 and gated by its own `preconditions:` YAML — Plan-004 Phase 1's `command_receipts` CREATE plus this plan's Phase 3 (the dormant seam it activates) — so it dispatches whenever both are merged, without serializing Phase 3 behind Plan-004's schedule.

## Test And Verification Plan

- **Phase 1 contract tests** (T1.5): Type-system conformance via TypeScript compile check + minimal mock driver. Verifies AC1 + AC2.
- **Phase 2 integration tests** (T2.5): Real SQLite (better-sqlite3 in-memory) + ProviderRegistry round-trip. Verifies I-005-1, I-005-2 + AC2.
- **Phase 3 driver tests**: the per-driver lifecycle suite (createSession → resumeSession → startRun → interruptRun → closeSession) + capability declaration + per-tool idempotency_class + event normalization verifies AC1 + AC2 + AC3; T3.18's shared outbound-frame suite — origin classification, sentinel application, the persisted-text non-mutation proof, and the tripwire's per-path landing — verifies AC8; and the T3.19-T3.22 transcript suites — projection fidelity and pipeline ordering, the post-replay assertion on both drivers, the memo-projection floor under each of the three replay-unavailable triggers, and structural-refusal classification — verify AC9 + AC10 + AC11 + AC12; and the T3.23-T3.25 version-tolerance suites — the ratified-floor gate with its above-pin attach arm and its launcher-drift arm, the per-capability zero-turn probe with its negative control and zero-spend assertion, the in-band spawned-version read paired with the child auto-update opt-out, and posture-pair single-member realization — verify AC13 + AC14 + AC15 + AC16 + AC17. T3.11's usage-delta suite — the cumulative-sequence re-sum, the interleaved named-turn attribution, the fresh-first-reading and replay-seeded-is-fresh arms, the resume prior-emitted-sum re-base, the compaction non-re-base, the late-frame named-turn differencing, the negative-delta floor with its diagnostic, the declared-figure cross-check mismatch, the nested-cache non-overlap, and the failed containment identity — verifies I-005-11 + AC18; T3.11's thread-routing suite — foreign-thread suppression, the connection-scoped census pass, unknown-family quarantine, absent-identity quarantine with bounded oldest-first shedding, pending-registration hold release and timeout shed, child-spend attribution on both arms, the child-request carve-through, and the surviving `subagent.started` / `subagent.completed` pair — verifies I-005-12 + AC19.
- **Phase 4 SDK tests** (T4.6, T4.7): Degraded-fallback orchestration + recovery-needed return-value contract. Verifies AC2 + AC3 + I-005-4 + I-005-5.
- **Integration tests across phases**: Proving driver lifecycle and policy enforcement stay daemon-local even when the provider endpoint itself is remote — verifies I-005-1.

## Rollout Order

1. Land Phase 1 contracts (T1.1-T1.6; + campaign B10 T1.7-T1.8) — blocks all downstream.
2. Land Phase 2 persistence + registry (T2.1-T2.5; + campaign B10 T2.6) — blocks Phase 3.
3. Land Phase 3 Codex driver (T3.1-T3.5), Phase 3 Claude driver (T3.6-T3.10), the campaign B10 driver enrichment (T3.11-T3.15), the provider-bound text neutralization (T3.18), the canonical transcript export and replay (T3.19-T3.22), and the provider-CLI version tolerance (T3.23-T3.25) in parallel, T3.23 first within its own trio.
   - Phase 3B (T3.16-T3.17) lands after step 3 merges, on its own `preconditions:` YAML gate. It is carried as a sub-step rather than a numbered one so steps 4 and 5 keep the ordinals other surfaces cite.
4. Land Phase 4 SDK exposure (T4.1-T4.7; + campaign B10 T4.8) — may proceed in parallel with late-Phase-3 work once T2.3 ships (its own `preconditions:` gate requires the Phase 1–2 catch-up merged).
5. Land Phase 5 (T5.1) once Plan-004 Phase 1's `command_receipts` CREATE and this plan's Phase 3 are both merged (the Phase-5 YAML gates) — activates T3.13's dormant receipt-write seam.

## Rollback Or Fallback

- Keep one driver behind a compatibility adapter if the full contract rollout regresses.
- If Phase 4 SDK exposure regresses, fall back to direct daemon-internal use of ProviderRegistry (Phase 2 surface) until the SDK surface is restored.

## Risks And Blockers

- Contract churn while both initial drivers are under construction → mitigated by Phase 1 contract-stabilization gate before Phase 3 starts.
- Recovery semantics may diverge before enough conformance tests exist → mitigated by I-005-5 invariant + T4.7 explicit recovery-needed return-value contract test.
- Remote provider APIs can be mistaken for permission to centralize driver execution unless the local-runtime boundary stays explicit in code and docs → mitigated by I-005-1 invariant + `Spec-005 §Required Behavior` reinforcement throughout this plan.
- The Codex rewind leg is **resolved (2026-08-26)** and the Claude one is not. `thread/rollback` is deprecated in the generated type itself, so Spec-005 §Parity Capability Mechanism Grades rebinds the rollback row onto the non-experimental successor `thread/fork` + inclusive `lastTurnId` and T3.15 leg 1 registers no `thread/rollback` handler; because a fork mints a **new thread**, that leg also re-points the run's live provider binding at the forked thread in the same operation ([`codex.md`](../reference/provider-wire/codex.md)). Claude `--resume-session-at` remains binary-probe-only and absent from the live CLI reference, so before any driver code invoking **that** rewind path lands, re-verify it against the then-installed provider binary per [ADR-017](../decisions/017-shared-event-sourcing-scope.md) Decision Log (2026-07-02) and [`claude.md`](../reference/provider-wire/claude.md) §Gaps recorded. Rollback is feature #19 — its B2 spec amendment is in-tree (PR #205) with Spec-004 re-promoted `approved` 2026-07-18 via the W1.5 batch gate; rollback dispatch — riding Plan-004's Phase-3B explicit-label supplement since the NS-69 PR #346 round-1 re-home — gated on the B21→B23 turn-snapshot leg landing first (met 2026-08-09: the B23 leg shipped via PR #303) and on the campaign's W2 plan-task bundles per [cross-plan-dependencies.md](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md) §5 (met 2026-08-15: the campaign plan closed `completed` with all W2 bundles in-tree).
- The leading-`/` dispatch hazard and the leading-newline sentinel that defeats it are properties of a provider parser this repo neither owns nor pins — both were verified first-party 2026-08-25 against Claude Code `2.1.245` and codex-cli `0.149.1`, and a provider update can move either between two runs of the same install → mitigated twice over: T3.18 step 1 re-probes both binaries at implementation time and pins the result into [`claude.md`](../reference/provider-wire/claude.md) / [`codex.md`](../reference/provider-wire/codex.md) before code lands, and I-005-7's runtime tripwire is the **standing** detector rather than a one-time check — if a later provider build parses through the sentinel, the turn fails loudly instead of vanishing, and the escalation ladder in `Spec-005 §Required Behavior` is the ordered response.
- A terminal-source rollback's driver leg addresses the run's live provider binding, and no governing document states how that binding is acquired when the terminal run has none — the ordinary case, since startup failure, resume failure, and an exited provider all reach `failed` with the process gone. Pre-existing and **Spec-004-owned**: T3.18's binding disposal adds a producer to that case and no case of its own, so no task here is blocked on it; recorded so the gap is not misread as something this amendment introduced.
- Both amended behaviors are **invisible in the emitted row**: a cumulative figure forwarded as a per-turn delta and a child's frame projected under the parent's name both produce well-formed, schema-valid events, so neither failure is detectable downstream of the normalize boundary — which is why both are refused there and asserted at T3.11 rather than left to a consumer-side check. The residual is that the cache-read / cache-write split is retained only on the driver's diagnostic band: the Spec-006 `usage_telemetry` payload registers no per-cache-axis member, and minting one here would mint it ahead of any reader, so a consumer that later needs the components separately takes an ordinary Spec-006 growth. Recorded rather than silently absorbed.

## Invariants

The following load-bearing properties are promoted from Spec-005 and apply across all phases. Each invariant is referenced by Task `Verifies invariant:` fields above.

- **I-005-1** — Driver authority remains local even when provider endpoint is remote. Source: `Spec-005 §Required Behavior`, `Spec-005 §Default Behavior`. Enforced by: ProviderRegistry residing in-daemon (T2.3); SDK factory is daemon-only (T4.3). Test: integration test asserting driver lifecycle stays daemon-local with remote endpoint mock.
- **I-005-2** — Runtime treats undeclared capabilities as unsupported. Source: `Spec-005 §Required Behavior`. Enforced by: ProviderRegistry capability-check gating (T2.3); `DriverCapabilityFlag` enum exhaustiveness at the type system (T1.2 — seven flags as shipped, thirteen post-B3/B6, fourteen once T3.19 lands `transcript_replay`, seventeen once T3.26 lands `context_compaction` / `provider_commands` / `output_speed`). Test: SDK test asserts invocation against undeclared flag is rejected with `driver.capability_unsupported` (T4.6).
- **I-005-3** — Per-tool `idempotency_class` defaults to `manual_reconcile_only` when undeclared. Source: `Spec-005 §idempotency_class`. Enforced by: the `ProviderToolMetadataSchema` ingress field `idempotency_class: IdempotencyClassSchema.optional().default("manual_reconcile_only")` (T1.3) — a driver that OMITS the field is accepted, NOT rejected, at ingress, and parsing yields the `manual_reconcile_only` default in `z.output` (`NormalizedProviderToolMetadata`), so every value persisted to the NOT NULL `driver_tools.idempotency_class` column or emitted on a `runtime_node.capability_*` event is populated; Plan-015's recovery dispatch table reads the normalized value (`Spec-015 §Idempotency Classes and Recovery Behavior`). Test: contract test asserts a tool declared WITHOUT `idempotency_class` is accepted at ingress AND resolves to `manual_reconcile_only` in the normalized (persisted / emitted) shape.
- **I-005-4** — Unsupported intervention types return structured `degraded` result; orchestration layer dispatches fallback. Source: `Spec-005 §Required Behavior`, [Spec-005 §Fallback Behavior](../specs/005-provider-driver-contract-and-capabilities.md#fallback-behavior-1). Enforced by: DriverInterventionResultSchema Zod validation at SDK seam (T1.4 + T4.2); Claude driver intervention dispatcher (T3.7) returns `degraded` on `steer` invocation. Test: T4.6 asserts the Zod-validated result shape.
- **I-005-5** — Drivers MUST NOT silently create replacement provider sessions on resume failure; failure surfaces an explicit `recovery-needed` condition. Source: [Spec-005 §Fallback Behavior](../specs/005-provider-driver-contract-and-capabilities.md#fallback-behavior), `Spec-005 §Acceptance Criteria` (AC3). Enforced by: lifecycle `resumeSession()` implementations (T3.1, T3.6) return a typed failure result carrying `recoveryCondition: 'recovery-needed'` rather than calling `createSession()`. Plan-015's recovery dispatcher (Tier 7) receives the typed result and emits the existing `run.failed` event with `recoveryCondition: 'recovery-needed'` per `Spec-006 §Run Lifecycle (run_lifecycle)` — no separate driver event. Test: T4.7 asserts the typed return shape from a simulated Codex resume failure path; verifying that no `createSession()` call is issued is covered by the same test (mock-spy on the driver's `createSession` method).
- **I-005-6** — A provider usage limit is recognized only from a typed, structured provider event the driver can name; it is never inferred from message text, exit code, or bare HTTP status, and an unrecognized shape yields **no** signal rather than a synthesized one. The signal is a sibling axis to `RecoveryCondition`, never a member of it. Source: `Spec-005 §Fallback Behavior`. Enforced by: the two driver normalizer legs and the shared shape (T3.16); the reset boundary's own provenance marker, which keeps a runtime-derived boundary distinguishable from a provider-stated one. Test: T3.16's seeded discriminating control — a prose message and an exit code that a text-matching implementation would classify as a usage limit must produce no signal, so the assertion fails against exactly the implementation the invariant forbids.
- **I-005-7** — Participant-authored text bound for a provider is delivered as prose or the turn fails visibly. The driver neutralizes command-shaped text at its outbound frame boundary; the transform is transport-only and never mutates the text this repo persists, events, replays, rewinds to, or shows a participant; an absent or unrecognized frame origin neutralizes; and a frame the provider dispatches as its own command anyway fails the turn with `driver.text_neutralization_failed` rather than recording a zero-turn success as a completed turn — on both paths that refusal lands as the run's ordinary `run.failed` terminal carrying `failureCategory: 'provider failure'`, `recoveryCondition: 'recovery-needed'`, and a `providerFailureDetail` in the fixed form `<code> origin=<participant_text|system_narration|unknown>`, never as a JSON-RPC error; the trip also disposes the run's live provider binding, so no later run re-binds to a session whose client-side state may have been mutated; the landing declares no new transition, `starting -> failed` and `running -> failed` both being declared already ([run-state-machine.md §Complete Transition Table](../domain/run-state-machine.md#complete-transition-table)); an already-dispatched intervention frame additionally settles `degraded` on the zero-effect arm with no `fallbackAction` and — best-effort, whenever the trip is classified before that call resolves — with the closed-literal `refusalCode` carrying the cause to its caller, the run terminal above being the guarantee on both paths, and fails the target run, because a frame the provider dispatched as a command may have mutated the session state that run depends on. Source: `Spec-005 §Required Behavior`. Enforced by: the shared outbound frame boundary and the tripwire classification (T3.18); the encapsulated child-stdin handle, which makes bypassing the frame writer unrepresentable rather than merely discouraged. Test: T3.18's wire-level byte assertions across all three provider-bound paths, its seeded discriminating controls, pinned to the predicate as settled — a mid-text slash passes through unchanged, while the path-like `/etc/hosts`, the plugin-qualified `/foo:bar`, and any message whose first non-whitespace byte is `/` after leading ASCII whitespace are each neutralized, so an implementation that strips the leading `/`, carves a path-like or qualified-name exception back out, or trims before testing fails there and only there, its history non-mutation assertion, its unknown-origin default, and its replayed command-dispatch transcript paired with an ordinary-model-turn negative control.

- **I-005-8** — A provider session is reconstituted from the daemon's canonical transcript, and a replay is complete only when the reconstituted session's own answer confirms it. The canonical transcript is a projection rebuilt from the session event log, never a store and never cached across turns; the export pipeline's five steps run in the specified order, tool-call identity is preserved by a stable bidirectional map and never re-minted, and an unpaired call takes a synthetic error result rather than being dropped. A provider's success return is **not** evidence that a replay worked. Source: `Spec-005 §Required Behavior`, [Spec-005 §Canonical Transcript Export And Replay](../specs/005-provider-driver-contract-and-capabilities.md#canonical-transcript-export-and-replay), [ADR-029](../decisions/029-canonical-transcript-is-authoritative.md). Enforced by: T3.19 (projection + ordered pipeline), T3.20 (replay + post-replay assertion). Test: a stubbed provider that returns success on a malformed replay fails the operation (T3.20); a reordered pipeline fails the pairing assertion (T3.19).
- **I-005-9** — What cannot be carried is declared, and a diminished continuation is visibly diminished. Every operation that reconstitutes a session under a different provider, model, or account emits a **required** declared-loss list whose empty value is a positive claim that nothing was dropped; an operation settled on the memo projection reports `degraded` and never `applied`; and a structurally invalid history is classified a permanent refusal rather than retried. Source: `Spec-005 §Required Behavior`, [Spec-005 §Canonical Transcript Export And Replay](../specs/005-provider-driver-contract-and-capabilities.md#canonical-transcript-export-and-replay). Enforced by: T3.21 (memo floor + `degraded` settlement), T3.22 (permanent classification). Test: a tight memo budget never splits a tool exchange (T3.21); a permanently-refusing provider is called exactly once (T3.22).
- **I-005-10** — Version decides admission and nothing else; every capability is decided on its own. A build at or above the configured floor attaches, above the pin included, and a build below it refuses fail-closed; above the floor no capability is resolved from a version comparison or a registry census, and every capability carrying an **admissible** probe — zero-turn, non-mutating, and decisive at the granularity the capability is consumed at — is resolved from that probe against the running build rather than from a static table, with each result reported under its detection source; a capability with no admissible probe resolves from the matrix and is **declared** `static` with the failing conjunct named, which is a stated property rather than an unwritten probe. Either way a moved provider surface withdraws exactly one flag and never a session. The version compared, the version recorded on the run's binding, and the version of the process that runs the session are one reading, and that process's auto-updater is disabled so the reading stays true for the session's life. Source: `Spec-005 §Required Behavior`, [Spec-005 §Capability discovery](../specs/005-provider-driver-contract-and-capabilities.md#capability-discovery). Enforced by: T3.23 (resolution, in-band read, floor gate, binding persistence), T3.24 (per-capability probes + detection source), T3.25 (auto-update suppression + single-member posture realization). Test: an above-pin build attaches and keeps every still-answering capability while a refusing probe withdraws only its own flag (T3.24); the binding row's version equals the spawned process's own report under launcher drift (T3.23); every probe-table entry declares a mechanism, each `static` entry names its failing admissibility conjunct, and the suite fails if any entry claims `probed` without one (T3.24); a probe suite spends zero billed turns — asserted at the provider transport, not at the daemon's event stream — and fails when its negative control succeeds (T3.24).

- **I-005-11** — A driver never emits a provider's cumulative counter as a per-turn figure, and no normalized token axis counts a token twice. Every usage emission is a per-axis delta derived from **one base register per provider thread and axis, advanced in stream order** — never a base snapshot copied at turn dispatch — and attributed to **the turn the metered frame itself names**, never to whichever turn is open at arrival. Base establishment is two-armed: a fresh provider session — replay-seeded included — bases at **zero**, its first reading metering in full, and a provider-native resume bases at **the daemon's own prior-emitted cumulative sum for that thread**, never at the first post-resume reading. A context compaction does not re-base; a decrease on a declared-cumulative axis floors at zero with a recorded diagnostic rather than emitting negative spend; and a wire-declared per-turn figure **corroborates** the derived interval — mismatch diagnosed — and never substitutes for it. The normalized axes are a partition — the input axis is uncached input, subtracted only where the breakdown's own sum identity confirms containment, with an unconfirmed identity emitting unsubtracted plus a diagnostic — and cost is derived from the provider's full breakdown, never by re-multiplying that partition. Source: [Spec-005 §Required Behavior](../specs/005-provider-driver-contract-and-capabilities.md#required-behavior), [Spec-005 §Interfaces And Contracts](../specs/005-provider-driver-contract-and-capabilities.md#interfaces-and-contracts). Enforced by: `provider/usage-delta-accountant.ts` as the single metering path both normalizers emit through (T3.11), with the floor-hit, the cross-check mismatch, the failed containment identity, and the retained cache split all landing on the T3.11 `DriverDiagnosticRecord` surface. Test: T3.11's cumulative-sequence, interleaved-attribution, fresh-first-reading, replay-seeded-is-fresh, resume-prior-emitted-sum, compaction-no-re-base, late-frame, floor-hit, cross-check-mismatch, nested-cache, and failed-identity assertions (AC18).
- **I-005-12** — Only the session's own thread projects, and no child's spend or interactive request is lost. Every inbound provider frame is classified by family against the pinned stream-surface census before any projection decision: a thread-scoped family routes by explicit thread identity, a censused connection- or account-scoped family routes without one, and an unrecognized family with no thread identity quarantines. Thread identities register ahead of the refusal rule — the session's own at establishment, a child's from the provider's parent-linked announcement — with a present-but-unregistered identity held in a bounded pending-registration buffer (released on registration, shed with a diagnostic on timeout) distinct from the quarantine an absent or unrecognized identity enters, both bounded and reported, never projected and never attributed to the parent by arrival order. Usage counters and child-raised interactive requests are both carved out **ahead of** suppression — provider-attributed subagent spend lands on the (`runId`, `provider`, `subagentId`) triple, provider-internal child spend lands on the parent run at run scope, and a child's request routes through the same dispatch/approval pipeline as the parent's and is answered on the child's own correlation — so suppression governs the child's transcript projection alone; child lifecycle reaches the timeline only as `subagent.started` / `subagent.completed`. Source: [Spec-005 §Required Behavior](../specs/005-provider-driver-contract-and-capabilities.md#required-behavior), [Spec-016 §Provider-Native Subagents](../specs/016-multi-agent-channels-and-orchestration.md#provider-native-subagents). Enforced by: `provider/thread-frame-router.ts` as the single routing decision both normalizers consult (T3.11), consumed unchanged by T3.14's terminal-emission boundary, which adds no second routing decision. Test: T3.11's foreign-thread-suppression, connection-scoped-pass, unknown-family-quarantine, absent-identity-quarantine, quarantine-shed, pending-hold-release, hold-timeout-shed, child-spend-attribution, compaction-child-run-scope, child-request-carve-through, and surviving-lifecycle-pair assertions (AC19).
- **I-005-13** — A console-parity operation settles on the provider's own evidence, and a provider command is bound to where it was read from. `compactContext` reports success only after the provider's typed compaction frame is observed, never on the request being accepted; a `driver_command` frame carrying a provider command is dispatched only after that command's literal name is found in the enumeration read for **that session's** `(driverName, providerAccountId)`; and an entry from `listProviderCommands` is offerable and dispatchable only through agents of that same binding. Source: `Spec-005 §Desktop Console Parity Surfaces`; `Spec-005 §Required Behavior` (the re-derived `driver_command` count and its two guards). Enforced by: T3.26 legs (a) and (b). Test: an ack-without-a-compaction-frame settles as a failure; an absent command name refuses before sending; a cross-binding dispatch refuses as `driver.capability_unsupported` (T3.26; T4.9).

## Cross-Plan Obligations

This section makes Plan-005's reciprocal obligations to other plans visible to a Plan-005 reviewer without requiring them to read every consuming or consumed plan first. Mirrors the bidirectional-citation pattern established by [Plan-001 §Cross-Plan Obligations](./001-shared-session-core.md#cross-plan-obligations) and [Plan-007 §Cross-Plan Obligations](./007-local-ipc-and-daemon-control.md#cross-plan-obligations).

### CP-005-1 — `runtime-binding-store.ts` recovery-aware extension contract owed to [Plan-015](./015-persistence-recovery-and-replay.md)

Plan-005 Phase 2 (T2.2) owns `packages/runtime-daemon/src/provider/runtime-binding-store.ts`. Plan-015 at Tier 7 extends the store with recovery-aware persistence methods (per `docs/architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md §2. Package Path Ownership Map` ownership row). The extension contract:

- The `RuntimeBindingStore` exposes `findResumableBindings(...)` as a queryable seam where Plan-015 may add recovery-aware predicates (e.g., bindings with stale checkpoints, bindings marked recovery-needed via the dedicated `recovery_checkpoints` table at `docs/architecture/schemas/local-sqlite-schema.md §GDPR and Recovery Tables (Spec-022, Plan-015)`).
- Row-level recovery state lives in Plan-015's `recovery_checkpoints` table (not on `runtime_bindings`). Plan-005's `resumeSession()` returns the typed failure result (T3.1/T3.6); Plan-015's recovery dispatcher (Tier 7) receives the result and writes the corresponding row to `recovery_checkpoints` via a Plan-015-defined extension method on `RuntimeBindingStore`, then emits the existing `run.failed` event with `recoveryCondition: 'recovery-needed'` per `Spec-006 §Run Lifecycle (run_lifecycle)`.
- Plan-005 does NOT add `recovery_state` / `recovery_reason` columns to `runtime_bindings`. The ownership boundary is: `runtime_bindings` carries identity + active binding state; `recovery_checkpoints` carries recovery-state-machine state. (Recovery state belongs to Plan-015's dedicated table.)

**Why bidirectional.** Plan-015 reviewers see the consumer dependency; Plan-005 reviewers (especially Phase 2 PR authors) must know that the binding-store's public method surface is contractually required to support Plan-015's recovery dispatch without further Plan-005 amendments.

### CP-005-2 — `idempotency_class` per-tool metadata shape owed to [Plan-015](./015-persistence-recovery-and-replay.md)

Plan-005 Phase 1 (T1.3) owns the `IdempotencyClass` enum + `ProviderToolMetadata` shape. Plan-015 at Tier 7 consumes these types in its `Spec-015 §Idempotency Protocol` recovery-dispatch table (`Spec-015 §Idempotency Classes and Recovery Behavior`) — the `manual_reconcile_only` row (`Spec-015 §Idempotency Classes and Recovery Behavior`) is load-bearing for Plan-015's recovery dispatcher: Plan-015 reads the `IdempotencyClass` enum to dispatch (replay vs skip vs halt), and on halt emits the existing `run.failed` event with `recoveryCondition: 'recovery-needed'` per `Spec-006 §Run Lifecycle (run_lifecycle)`. The driver's `resumeSession()` failure-path return-value is the upstream signal that triggers Plan-015's dispatch; the event emission stays on Plan-015's side.

**Why bidirectional.** The `IdempotencyClass` symbol must exist in Plan-005's contracts file before Plan-015 can compile. Plan-005 cannot move this enum without coordinating with Plan-015's spec language.

### CP-005-3 — `PtyHost` contract consumption from [Plan-024](./024-rust-pty-sidecar.md)

Plan-005 Phase 3 Codex driver (T3.1) consumes the `PtyHost` contract from `packages/contracts/src/pty-host.ts`. Plan-024 Phase 2 ships this contract at Tier 1 (per NS-05). The boundary:

- Plan-005 imports the `PtyHost` interface; it does NOT depend on the Rust binary directly. The binary is a Plan-024-owned implementation detail behind the contract.
- The Codex /resume substrate (PtyHost-based persistent process for `resumeSession()`) is a Plan-024 contract surface — Plan-005's Codex driver consumes it. If Plan-024 changes the PtyHost contract, Plan-005 amendments may be required.

**Why bidirectional.** Plan-024's Phase 2 is the canonical landing-point for the `PtyHost` contract; Plan-005 reviewers must know which Plan-024 surface they're consuming so a Plan-024 contract change triggers a Plan-005 review.

### CP-005-4 — `driver.*` JSON-RPC namespace registration owed to [Plan-007](./007-local-ipc-and-daemon-control.md) Tier 4

Plan-005 Phase 4 (T4.1) registers 7 client-facing `driver.*` JSON-RPC method handlers (6 non-lifecycle verbs + `driver.subscribeEvents`; the 4 session/run lifecycle ops — `createSession`/`resumeSession`/`startRun`/`closeSession` — are daemon-internal per §Phase 4 decision #2, invoked by orchestration on the in-daemon `ProviderRegistry`) against Plan-007's NamespaceRegistry (`packages/contracts/src/jsonrpc-registry.ts` per Plan-007 CP-007-3 → no-mirror disposition; canonical source: code). Plan-007 §Tier 4 namespace enumeration must include `driver.*` for Plan-005's registration to land without orphaning.

**Why bidirectional.** Plan-007 Tier 4 PR authors must know that `driver.*` is part of the registered namespace set; otherwise the Plan-005 SDK seam fails to register at daemon startup. Plan-007 CP-007-6 is the reciprocal entry on Plan-007's side.

### CP-005-5 — Driver capability event surface owed to [Plan-006](./006-session-event-taxonomy-and-audit-log.md) / [Spec-006](../specs/006-session-event-taxonomy-and-audit-log.md)

**Capability-declaration + capability-change events reuse existing Spec-006 surface.** `Spec-006 §Runtime Node Lifecycle (runtime_node_lifecycle)` already enumerates two events in the `runtime_node_lifecycle` category that name "provider driver" as a capability type:

- `runtime_node.capability_declared` — payload `{capability, capabilityDetails}`. Plan-005 emits this on driver registration (T2.3) and on cold-start (T4.5) with `capability: "provider-driver-{codex|claude}"` and `capabilityDetails: { flags: Record<DriverCapabilityFlag, boolean>, contractVersion: string, tools: NormalizedProviderToolMetadata[] }` (the wrapper-shape contents from T1.3; `tools` carries the normalized shape because the event crosses the persistence / event-payload boundary).
- `runtime_node.capability_updated` — payload `{capability, previousState, newState}`. Plan-005 emits this on capability refresh (T3.3, T3.8; consumed by T4.5 cache invalidation) with `capability: "provider-driver-{codex|claude}"` and previous/new state carrying the same shape as `capabilityDetails` above.

No new event types, no new category. The capability-flag matrix (twelve flags post-B3; thirteen with B6's `cost_cap`; fourteen with the 2026-08-26 `transcript_replay` addition; seventeen with the 2026-08-29 desktop-console parity additions) + per-tool metadata travel inside the existing `capabilityDetails` / `previousState` / `newState` payload fields. Plan-006 audit confirms these payload shapes are admissible under the existing event surface (`Spec-006 §Runtime Node Lifecycle (runtime_node_lifecycle)` already lists "driver version bump, tool addition" as a documented example of `capability_updated`).

**Recovery-needed condition reuses existing `run.failed` surface.** [Spec-005 §Fallback Behavior](../specs/005-provider-driver-contract-and-capabilities.md#fallback-behavior) calls for drivers to "surface a visible `recovery-needed` condition" on resume-handle failure. `Spec-006 §Run Lifecycle (run_lifecycle)` already declares this surface: `run.failed` payload includes `recoveryCondition?: 'recovery-needed'`. Plan-005 does NOT emit a separate `driver.recovery_needed` event. Instead:

1. Plan-005's `resumeSession()` returns a typed failure result (T3.1, T3.6).
2. Plan-015's recovery dispatcher (Tier 7) receives the typed result.
3. Plan-015 emits the existing `run.failed` event with `recoveryCondition: 'recovery-needed'`.

This keeps the recovery-needed signal on the canonical run-lifecycle surface where downstream consumers already subscribe; avoids creating a parallel driver-specific recovery event.

**Why bidirectional.** Plan-006 audit (concurrent Tier 4) confirms the existing `runtime_node.capability_declared` / `runtime_node.capability_updated` payload shapes accept the wrapper-shape contents — no new event types are added. Plan-005 reviewers (especially Phase 2-4 implementers) must know that the driver-capability event surface is the existing `Spec-006 §Runtime Node Lifecycle (runtime_node_lifecycle)` row, not a new category. Plan-005 reviewers must also know that recovery-needed flows through Plan-015 (not directly from the driver) so the driver's `resumeSession()` return-type stays a pure typed failure result.

**Resolution (Plan-006 audit synthesis 2026-05-28 — closes CP-005-5).** Plan-006 audit ratifies two concrete deliverables that discharge this carry-forward:

1. **Plan-006 Phase 1 T1.4 binds a typed `CapabilityDetails` interface** in `packages/contracts/src/event.ts` (extends top-level per Plan-001's contracts package layout precedent; resolved as "extend in place"). Shape: `{ flags: Record<DriverCapabilityFlag, boolean>, contractVersion: string, tools: readonly NormalizedProviderToolMetadata[] }` (`tools` is the normalized shape — `CapabilityDetails` is an event payload, so it crosses the boundary). Plan-006 Phase 1 T1.6 doc-mirrors the same shape into [`api-payload-contracts.md` §Plan-006](../architecture/contracts/api-payload-contracts.md#plan-006--session-event-taxonomy) (between `GetCapabilitiesResult` and `EventEnvelope`) with explicit references to `Spec-006 §Runtime Node Lifecycle (runtime_node_lifecycle)` and this carry-forward.

2. **Plan-006 Phase 3 ratifies `providerFailureDetail?: string` on `run.failed` payload** (the `RunStateChangeEvent` member, in [`api-payload-contracts.md` §Plan-004 — Queue Steer Pause Resume](../architecture/contracts/api-payload-contracts.md#plan-004--queue-steer-pause-resume)). Mirrors the `failed`-variant `providerFailureDetail` field of `DriverResumeResult` (in [`api-payload-contracts.md` §Plan-005 — Provider Driver Contract (Internal Interface)](../architecture/contracts/api-payload-contracts.md#plan-005--provider-driver-contract-internal-interface), shipped by Plan-005 T1.6) — Plan-015's recovery dispatcher reads the typed failure detail from the driver result and emits it on the canonical `run.failed` event so the audit log carries the operator-actionable reason without re-querying the driver. ADR-018-compliant additive-only MINOR addition (no consumer breakage; optional field).

3. **Plan-006 Phase 4 confirms read-shape generic envelope is sufficient.** Replay endpoints (`EventReadAfterCursorResponse`, `EventReadWindowResponse`, `EventSubscription`) return `EventEnvelope[]` with `payload: Record<string, unknown>`. Consumers narrow via Phase 1's discriminated-union taxonomy (the typed `RuntimeNodeCapabilityDeclaredPayload` + `RuntimeNodeCapabilityUpdatedPayload` payload interfaces). No replay-shape tightening required.

**Status: CP-005-5 RESOLVED.** Discharge artifacts land in Plan-006 audit PR (this PR or its successor); Plan-005 author confirms by re-reading `api-payload-contracts.md` `CapabilityDetails` + `RunStateChangeEvent.providerFailureDetail` definitions and Plan-006 working-copy T1.4 + Phase 3 T3.x integration.

### CP-005-6 — Cross-cutting run-control symbol co-location (`InterventionType` enum + `RunId` brand / `RunIdSchema`) owed to [Plan-004](./004-queue-steer-pause-resume.md) + [Plan-012](./012-approvals-permissions-and-trust-boundaries.md)

The cross-cutting `InterventionType = "steer" | "interrupt" | "cancel" | "rollback"` enum (`docs/architecture/contracts/api-payload-contracts.md §Shared Enums`; the `rollback` member is Spec-004 content, campaign B2 — the shipped `provider-driver.ts` union stays three-membered until **T1.8** (this plan's campaign bundle) widens it to four; `ApplyInterventionParams` deliberately stays three-armed since the rollback driver leg is the dedicated `rollbackTo` op, and Plan-004's Tier-5 consumers arrive only after the widening — its Phase 1 machine-gates on `{ plan: 005, phase: 1 }` — so union and consumers never diverge) is consumed by both Plan-004's `runControl.ts` (Tier 5) and Plan-005's `provider-driver.ts` (Tier 4). Resolution: Plan-005 Phase 1 (T1.4) co-locates the `InterventionType` re-export in `provider-driver.ts` since Plan-005 ships earlier. Plan-004 Tier 5 imports the enum from Plan-005 (Tier 4 → Tier 5 tier-consistent import).

The **`RunId` brand (`type RunId`) and its validator `RunIdSchema`** co-locate in the same `provider-driver.ts` by the identical logic, and for an additional structural reason: `RunId` is `InterventionType`'s struct-mate in `ApplyInterventionParams` (`{ type: InterventionType; targetRunId: RunId; … }`, `docs/architecture/contracts/api-payload-contracts.md §Plan-005 — Provider Driver Contract (Internal Interface)`). Lowest-tier-consumer placement governs each symbol independently: `type RunId` is first consumed by the Phase 1 `ProviderDriver` interface method params (T1.1, Tier 4), and `RunIdSchema` is first consumed by the Phase 4 `InterruptRunParamsSchema` (T4.2, Tier 4) — so both home in Plan-005 (Tier 4), the `type` and `Schema` paired in one file per the established `session.ts` `brandedUuidIdSchema` convention. Plan-004 (Tier 5) imports `RunId`; Plan-012 (Tier 6) imports both `RunId` and `RunIdSchema` — each an upward, forward edge. Authoring `RunIdSchema` in Plan-004's `runControl.ts` (Tier 5) — as the pre-correction `CP-012-3` cite asserted — would be a backward import for Plan-005's own Tier-4 `InterruptRunParamsSchema` consumer, i.e. structurally forbidden; that latent orphan is corrected by this plan (see §Cross-Plan Amendments).

**Precedent.** This matches Plan-001's ownership pattern for the brands it actually ships (`SessionId`, `ParticipantId`, `MembershipId`, `ChannelId` co-located in `session.ts` Phase 2 — each `type X` paired with its `XSchema` per the `brandedUuidIdSchema` convention; their lowest-tier consumer is Tier 1). The governing rule is **lowest-tier-consumer placement**: a cross-cutting symbol is declared in the contract file of its lowest-tier (earliest-shipping) consumer, and every higher-tier plan imports it upward — never re-inventing a parallel definition. `RunId` / `RunIdSchema` are **not** in Plan-001 / `session.ts` (Plan-001 ships no run-domain symbol); their lowest-tier consumer is Plan-005 (Tier 4), so they home in `provider-driver.ts`.

**Why bidirectional.** Plan-004 reviewers see the import-from-Plan-005 of `InterventionType` + `RunId`; Plan-012 reviewers see the import of `RunId` + `RunIdSchema`; Plan-005 reviewers must know these symbols are shared and must not relocate them without coordinating with Plan-004 + Plan-012.

### CP-005-7 — Driver approval-request normalization owed to [Plan-012](./012-approvals-permissions-and-trust-boundaries.md)

Plan-012 owns the normalizer that maps Plan-005's driver-surfaced permission asks (`interactive_request` driver events / driver-host permission callbacks) into canonical `approval.requestCreate` inputs; it lands with Plan-012's driver integration, not in Plan-005 (Tier-6 audit, 2026-06-10 — Plan-012 CP-012-6, A-18). Until that normalizer lands, the schema-validated `approval.requestCreate` boundary is the enforced normalization gate, and a driver that cannot surface granular permission requests inherits the stricter daemon boundary per [Spec-012 §Fallback Behavior](../specs/012-approvals-permissions-and-trust-boundaries.md#fallback-behavior). Pre-registration availability is fail-closed on the Plan-005 side (Codex round 5): until Plan-012 registers the seam, the T3.15 leg-3 callback-tool host withholds the `callbackTools` registry at spawn and its backstop answers any stray invocation `denied` — never `completed` without Cedar, never unanswered — so this consume edge is runtime-registered, not a Phase-3 dispatch gate.

**Why bidirectional.** Plan-012 reviewers see the consume edge on the Plan-012 §3 dependency row; Plan-005 reviewers must know the driver event surface has a downstream approval consumer and must not reshape `interactive_request` driver-event payloads without coordinating with Plan-012.

### CP-005-8 — Typed provider usage-limit signal owed to [Plan-029](./029-provider-accounts-and-credential-homes.md)

Plan-005 **mints** the signal and its two driver normalizer legs (T3.16). Plan-029 **scopes** it to the account that hit the limit, keying on `(accountId, credentialGeneration)` so a node running two accounts of one provider does not present one account's exhaustion as the other's (CP-029-2). The reciprocal is registered at `Plan-029 §Cross-Plan Obligations`. The split is deliberate: the signal's _shape_ and its typed-only recognition rule are driver-contract concerns and belong here, while _whose_ allowance was spent is an account-plane fact Plan-005 has no registry to answer from. The pacing consumer that acts on the signal is Plan-017's park-and-resume surface, which reads it through the same contract without a further widening — its NS-68 carrier box names this delta as its producer.

### CP-005-9 — Provider-account identity seam consumed from [Plan-029](./029-provider-accounts-and-credential-homes.md)

Plan-029 **owns** account registration, defaults, resolution, credential-home construction, and fail-closed validation. Plan-005 **consumes** the resolved identity as an opaque spawn input and persists it through `runtime_bindings.spawn_config` for resume rebinding (T3.17). Plan-005 asserts none of Plan-029's invariants and mints no account-plane symbol — the direction is one-way by construction, which is why T3.17 declares `none` on its invariant marker and return-cites I-029-3 / I-029-4 instead. The reciprocal is CP-029-1.

### CP-005-10 — Turn-scoped effective-principal stamp consumed from [Plan-004](./004-queue-steer-pause-resume.md)

The `usage.cost_update` rows this plan's drivers emit carry the turn's `effectivePrincipal` ([Spec-006 §Usage Telemetry](../specs/006-session-event-taxonomy-and-audit-log.md#usage-telemetry-usage_telemetry), instance (4) of the [api-payload-contracts.md](../architecture/contracts/api-payload-contracts.md) §Authenticated Principal class rule). The driver **does not resolve** the value and must not attempt to — it has no principal knowledge and no access to the `interventions` rows the resolution reads. Plan-004 T3.19 publishes each turn's resolved effective principal as an **immutable per-turn scope** on the run execution context under CP-004-16; this plan's usage normalizer stamps from **the scope bound to the turn whose stream produced the frame**, never from a mutable current-turn read — a `usage.cost_update` for turn A routinely lands after turn B has opened, so a read-at-emission stamp would bill A's spend to B's principal. The shape is otherwise the one already shipped — a daemon-supplied value stamped onto a driver-sourced event, exactly as the account identity rides `usage.rate_limit_update` (Plan-029 CP-029-5, the precedent); what bind-at-dispatch adds is _which_ value, not who supplies it. A driver that cannot read the context stamps nothing rather than guessing: the receipt's stated degrade — an unstamped turn attributes to the run starter — is the fail-safe, and a fabricated principal would not be.

**Consumed by:** T3.1 / T3.6 — the stamp lands on the usage-emission path those tasks share. (The producer is **Plan-004's** T3.19; this plan's own T3.19 is the transcript-projection task and carries no part of this obligation — the task ids collide across plans and are not the same task.) **Reciprocal:** Plan-004 CP-004-16.

### CP-005-11 — Driver-spawn capability report and the MCP set-replacement non-interference guarantee owed to [Plan-028](./028-mcp-server-configuration-and-governance.md)

Plan-028 T28.3.2 declared a dependency on this plan's driver-spawn capability reading before this amendment, naming only Phase 3 as its source and no obligation as its artifact. T3.24 is that artifact, in two parts. **The report:** a per-capability result carrying each flag's `CapabilityDetectionSource`, produced at spawn and refreshed on the T3.12 cadence. **The guarantee:** `mcp_set_servers` is **not a capability flag** and appears on no report — it is a control-request subtype the driver **never issues**, under any flag's detection mechanism, because the operation replaces the full named-server set and the only probe available issues an empty one, which would clear a live session's servers. A refusal of Plan-028's own reconcile therefore withdraws no flag on this report; it selects that plan's `user_config_write` grade. What this obligation therefore owes Plan-028 is the report plus an explicit guarantee of _non-interference_: no driver-side probe mutates the server set Plan-028 owns. The 2026-08-26 collapse of that leg's version deferral changes no code on either side — `2.1.210` now sits below the ratified admission floor `2.1.234`, so the reconcile is reachable on every admitted Claude build — and the live availability answer comes from T28.3.2's own full-desired-set reconcile, since reachability is still not availability (a cloud-hosted session refuses control requests a local one answers). Producer-only here: this plan reports and abstains; Plan-028 owns the reconcile and what its leg does with the answer.

**Owed to:** Plan-028 T28.3.2. **Reciprocal:** the `Consumes:` line on that task, which this amendment re-points at this obligation.

### CP-005-12 — Console-parity surfaces published to [Plan-013](./013-live-timeline-visibility-and-reasoning-surfaces.md), [Plan-023](./023-desktop-shell-and-renderer.md), and [Plan-016](./016-multi-agent-channels-and-orchestration.md)

This plan publishes four things the 2026-08-29 desktop-console parity legs consume, and authors nothing on their side. **(a)** The already-registered client-facing `driver.respondToRequest` is the input-ask answer ingress; its `response` is unconstrained, which is why the Spec-013 card's free-text answer arm needs no wire growth and why this amendment mints no method for it. **(b)** `driver.compactContext` (T4.9) and the `context_compaction` declaration, read through the existing `driver.listCapabilities` cache, gate and drive the composer's compaction control. **(c)** `driver.listProviderCommands` (T4.9) and the `provider_commands` / `output_speed` declarations feed the composer's autocomplete and its speed control. The **routing invariant travels with the data, not with the consumer's discipline**: every entry the read returns carries the `(driverName, providerAccountId)` it was read under, so a consumer that re-addresses its composer re-reads rather than filters, and a cross-binding dispatch refuses at this plan's boundary as `driver.capability_unsupported` rather than relying on the renderer to have filtered correctly. **(d)** Added at the round-2 fold and **re-homed at the round-3 fold**: the provider's own **declared** output-speed state and its provider-supplied reason, **held as driver-session state against the binding** (T3.26). The round-2 wording put it on `ProviderSessionHandle` and `DriverResumeResult`'s `resumed` arm, which cannot hold it — both resolve before the first turn-bearing exchange, and `Spec-005 §The output-speed axis`'s detection-source ruling establishes that the declaring handshake is emitted only as part of one — so the driver records it **when the handshake arrives** on the first turn-bearing exchange and holds it for the binding's life. It is readable per live binding, so Plan-016's `agent.list` binder can project it as the live-scoped `observedOutputSpeed` beside the committed column, **absent until observed** and never defaulted. Reading it costs **no provider round-trip** — the binder reads state the driver already holds, and never waits for, forces, or spends a turn to obtain it — which is what lets a list read serve it without starting, resuming, or interrogating a provider process, and it is deliberately stored nowhere on either side: a persisted observation would be a second and staler record of a fact the running session already holds, and would let a mode that stopped being available across a restart keep reading as accepted.

**Owed to:** Plan-013 T4.10 / T4.11, Plan-023 T-023r-6-2, and — since the round-2 fold — Plan-016 T3.20. **Reciprocals:** Plan-013's `CP-005-12` obligation row, Plan-023's `CP-023-8`, and Plan-016's `CP-016-21`. No reciprocal task is owed on this plan beyond T3.26 and T4.9, which it already carries: leg (d) publishes a value T3.26 already captures, so the obligation count stays **twelve** and no new row is minted for it.

### CP-005-13 — Machine-authored content column consumed from [Plan-006](./006-session-event-taxonomy-and-audit-log.md) (⇄ CP-006-15)

Registered 2026-08-30 by the machine-authored prose amendment ([cross-plan-dependencies.md §6](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md) node NS-94). **Two round-2 clarifications (Codex PR #383 round 1).** (i) What T3.20 consumes is the **`HydratedSessionEvent`** projection Plan-006 T3.6 declares and T3.8 builds — the verified event paired with a closed two-arm `content` union — never a body spliced into `payload`; the `unavailable` arm carries the reason, which is where this plan's `'turn_content_unavailable'` gets its truth value instead of being inferred from an absent key. (ii) The column is **node-local**: it is never relayed or backfilled, so on a node that received a row rather than authoring it the body is absent and the fold reports `'turn_content_unavailable'` for that turn. That is a named residual owned by the swap that distributes the session content key, not a defect in this obligation — but it bounds what a post-replay assertion can prove on a peer, and T3.20's tests must therefore be written against origin-authored history rather than assuming a body is present on every node. Plan-006 Phase 3B creates `session_events.content_payload` — the durable encrypted home for assistant- and tool-generated prose — together with its session-key sealer, its extended write path, and the read path that opens it. This plan is the **consumer and the reason the column exists**: [ADR-029](../decisions/029-canonical-transcript-is-authoritative.md) rules the daemon's canonical transcript authoritative for the content of a provider session, `Spec-005 §Canonical Transcript Export And Replay` scopes that transcript's content to what [Spec-006](../specs/006-session-event-taxonomy-and-audit-log.md)'s taxonomy carries, and until this column that taxonomy carried a record that an assistant spoke rather than a record of what it said.

This plan authors **nothing** on Plan-006's side — no column, no migration, no codec — and takes one task-scoped hold in return: **T3.20 dispatches only after Plan-006 Phase 3B merges**, for the reason stated in that task's own entry (a post-replay assertion over content-free turns cannot distinguish an accepted replay from a silently dropped one). T3.19 is deliberately **not** held: its fold and export are correct over a body-less log, which is the state they were authored against.

**Owed to:** nothing — this is a consumer-side registration. **Reciprocal:** Plan-006's `CP-006-15`.

**Not discharged here (NS-92's named residuals, unchanged).** The Fold's declaring clause in `Spec-005 §Canonical Transcript Export And Replay` (residual 1) and T3.19's emission leg and test (residual 2) remain owed by the swap that registers the fold's emission — the PR building T3.19, in flight at this amendment's authoring. This amendment settles only the **condition** those clauses will name, because the condition is its own content: an unreadable `content_payload` is what `'turn_content_unavailable'` reports, and a `contentTruncated` row is what the new `'turn_content_truncated'` reports. **That second member's emission leg is a third residual on the same owner**, named here rather than left implicit: Plan-006 T3.6 writes the `contentTruncated` flag and T3.7 tests it, but no task in this plan yet reads it into `declaredLosses`, and the leg that would is a fold-emission leg — the class this amendment deliberately declined to author. The vocabulary entry preceding its producer is the ordering `api-payload-contracts.md` prescribes (a loss kind is an amendment, never a code-first free string), not a field minted ahead of its reader.

### Cross-Plan Amendments

**PR #157 (2026-06-15) — `RunId` brand / `RunIdSchema` provenance correction across the cross-plan corpus.**

Phase 1's `ProviderDriver` interface surfaced two latent provenance orphans in the doc corpus — branded symbols cited as living in a file that does not (and structurally cannot) author them:

1. **`type RunId`** was cited as consumed "from `packages/contracts/src/session.ts`" by this plan (L12 / T1.1), Plan-010 (T1.2 / T3.2), and Plan-016 (T1.1). But `session.ts` (Plan-001 Phase 2) ships no run-domain symbol — a repo-wide grep confirms `RunId` appears in zero files under `packages/`. `type RunId`'s lowest-tier consumer is this Plan-005 (Tier 4 — the `ProviderDriver` method params), so it originates in `provider-driver.ts` (T1.1) per CP-005-6.
2. **`RunIdSchema`** was cited as imported "from Plan-004's `runControl.ts` (Tier 5)" by Plan-012's `CP-012-3` cluster. But Plan-004 never authors `RunIdSchema` (its task bodies only _consume_ the `RunId` type in-package), and authoring it at Tier 5 is structurally impossible: Plan-005's own Phase 4 `InterruptRunParamsSchema` (Tier 4) consumes it, and a Tier-4 file cannot import from a Tier-5 file without a backward edge. `RunIdSchema`'s lowest-tier consumer is therefore Plan-005 (Tier 4); it co-locates with `type RunId` in `provider-driver.ts` (authored at T4.2) per CP-005-6.

This PR corrects the provenance cites at their source: this plan (L12 / T1.1 / T4.2 / CP-005-6), `cross-plan-dependencies.md` (§2 ownership row for `provider-driver.ts` + the Plan-012 §3 dependency-graph row), Plan-010 (T1.2 / T3.2), Plan-016 (T1.1), and Plan-012 (header dep, `CP-012-3` + its Phase-1 precondition, and the `RunId` / `RunIdSchema` consume cites on its Phase-1 + Phase-4 tasks). Plan-004's task bodies are left unchanged — their `RunId` "(in-package)" type-consume cites stay true (`provider-driver.ts` and `runControl.ts` are both in `packages/contracts/src/`); Plan-004's missing `RunIdSchema`-consume cite is an omission its own Tier-5 G4 audit closes against the now-corrected ownership map.

**Cross-plan touch rationale.** The Plan-010 / Plan-012 / Plan-016 plan docs and `cross-plan-dependencies.md` are normally each plan's own / the map's surface. We applied a fix-in-place housekeeping exception because: (a) the change is doc-cite-level only (provenance attribution; zero code, zero behavioral change), (b) Plan-005 Phase 1 is the first surfacer — its `ProviderDriver` interface is the originating declaration of `type RunId`, (c) the sweep is a one-time provenance correction well under the sub-30-LOC / sub-day bar, and (d) bundling the corpus sweep in the surfacing PR avoids cross-plan PR-coordination ceremony and stops the two orphans from propagating into Tier-4/5/6 code. This is a one-time correction, not Plan-005 claiming ongoing edit rights on Plan-010 / Plan-012 / Plan-016 files. Encoded per the §2 §Ownership Rule **Housekeeping Exception** clause ([cross-plan-dependencies.md §Ownership Rule](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md#ownership-rule)).

**Refs:** [Plan-004](./004-queue-steer-pause-resume.md) (`runControl.ts` — `RunId` in-package consumer), [Plan-010](./010-worktree-lifecycle-and-execution-modes.md), [Plan-012](./012-approvals-permissions-and-trust-boundaries.md) (`CP-012-3`), [Plan-016](./016-multi-agent-channels-and-orchestration.md), [cross-plan-dependencies.md §Ownership Rule](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md#ownership-rule).

**PR #157 (2026-06-15) — `InterventionDriverResult` → `DriverInterventionResult` naming canonicalization.**

Phase 1's T1.4 code authoring surfaced an internal naming inconsistency in the canonical contract doc: the two sibling driver result-envelope types used different conventions. `DriverResumeResult` (`docs/architecture/contracts/api-payload-contracts.md §Plan-005 — Provider Driver Contract (Internal Interface)`) leads with the `Driver` prefix, but its explicit sibling — the `applyIntervention` envelope at `docs/architecture/contracts/api-payload-contracts.md §Plan-005 — Provider Driver Contract (Internal Interface)` — buried the qualifier in the middle as `InterventionDriverResult`. The doc is the defect; the fix is the doc, and Phase 1 code then mirrors the corrected doc (doc-first preserved).

Canonicalized to the `Driver`-prefixed form `DriverInterventionResult`, which aligns with (a) its explicit sibling `DriverResumeResult` (T1.6 — the plan itself calls this shape the "sibling" of T1.4's), (b) the `X` + `XSchema` pairing convention (the Zod schema is `DriverInterventionResultSchema`, T1.4), and (c) the `Driver`-prefixed contract family in this surface (`DriverCapabilityFlag`, `DriverCapabilities`). Renamed at source across `api-payload-contracts.md` (the `ProviderDriver.applyIntervention` return at :660 + the `interface` declaration at :762), this plan (T1.4 / T1.6 `Consumes:` cites + the §Done Checklist doc-mirror item), [Plan-004](./004-queue-steer-pause-resume.md) (the §Dependencies cell, `CP-004-1`, and the two T2.6 mapping-task consume cites), and [queue-and-intervention-model.md](../domain/queue-and-intervention-model.md) (the §Boundary note). Zero behavioral change — the `{ status: 'applied' | 'degraded'; fallbackAction? }` shape is untouched; only the type's name changes.

**Cross-plan touch rationale.** Plan-004's cites are normally Plan-004's own surface. We applied a fix-in-place housekeeping exception because: (a) the change is doc-cite-level / type-name only (zero code-behavior, identical shape), (b) Plan-005 Phase 1 T1.4 is the first surfacer — it authors the `DriverInterventionResult` code type that Plan-004 (Tier 5) consumes, so leaving Plan-004's cites on the old name would dangle them against the renamed type, (c) the sweep is a one-time correction well under the sub-30-LOC / sub-day bar, and (d) bundling it in the surfacing PR keeps the consumer cites resolvable rather than propagating a stale name into Tier-5 code. Encoded per the §2 §Ownership Rule **Housekeeping Exception** clause ([cross-plan-dependencies.md §Ownership Rule](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md#ownership-rule)).

**PR #287 (2026-08-04) — `driver-capabilities-writer.ts` internals restructured onto Plan-006's T3.1 append seam.**

[Plan-006](./006-session-event-taxonomy-and-audit-log.md) Phase 3 landed `EventLogService` as the daemon's sole durable event-append path, and this plan's `packages/runtime-daemon/src/provider/driver-capabilities-writer.ts` is one of its adopters: the writer's `runtime_node.capability_declared` / `capability_updated` emission now routes through that seam instead of writing an event row beside its own table writes. The seam is ASYNC-transactional — the append awaits a daemon signing-key unseal (and, on PII rows, an encrypt), and a better-sqlite3 transaction cannot span an `await` — so the writer's three table writes are handed DOWN as the append's `transactionalPrelude` and run inside the same transaction as the event-row INSERT, immediately before it. Three internals follow from that inversion: an **optimistic-retry declare/update flow** (read-decide outside the write transaction, re-read as the prelude's first statement inside the append's `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` where a racer that committed earlier is visible, retry on divergence with a fresh snapshot, bounded attempts); **deferred snapshot capture** (the equality check that picks the declare-vs-update branch can no longer sit inside a write transaction this writer no longer owns); and the **`CapabilityRowDivergedError`** sentinel, thrown from inside the prelude so a diverged attempt rolls the whole transaction back rather than emitting an event whose payload no longer describes the committed rows.

**The externally documented contract is preserved.** The write is still ONE transaction across the three per-driver tables (`driver_capabilities` / `driver_tools` / `driver_contract_meta`) — atomicity now comes from joining the append's transaction rather than from opening one — and the change-detected `runtime_node.capability_updated` emission (P2-9: a poll emits only when the reconstructed snapshot actually differs; no new event type, `CP-005-5`) is unchanged, as is the `Spec-006 §Runtime Node Lifecycle (runtime_node_lifecycle)` event surface it emits on. Race coverage was extended in the same PR: the writer suite gained deterministic cross-session concurrent-declare arms that park one racer at the daemon signing-key read to interleave the two appends, asserting exactly ONE `capability_declared` when both racers declare the same snapshot, and a loser reclassified to `capability_updated` whose `previousState` is the racer's committed snapshot. **No status implication for this plan:** no new table, no new dependency edge (the Plan-005 → Plan-006 edge is already carried by this plan's §Dependencies row and the [cross-plan-dependencies.md §3 Inter-Plan Dependency Graph](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md#3-inter-plan-dependency-graph)), and no new mandatory plan-body behavior — this entry records a landed, contract-preserving fact.

**Cross-plan touch rationale.** `driver-capabilities-writer.ts` is a Plan-005-owned file (the §2 map's `packages/runtime-daemon/src/provider/drivers/` row names it among the single-owner Plan-005 `provider/` siblings). The touch is sanctioned by the standing `CP-005-5` / `CP-006-5` relationship — Plan-006 owns the `runtime_node.capability_*` event contract this writer emits on — together with Plan-006's ownership of the T3.1 append seam: once that seam is the sole durable append path, adopting it is not optional for an emitter, and the adoption edit necessarily lands in the emitter's own file. Recorded here rather than left implicit because the restructure reaches the file's internals, not only its import list. This is a one-time adoption, not Plan-006 claiming ongoing edit rights on `driver-capabilities-writer.ts` — the file's behavior stays Plan-005's to own.

**Refs:** [Plan-006](./006-session-event-taxonomy-and-audit-log.md) (T3.1 append seam + `EventLogService`; `CP-006-5`, which closes `CP-005-5`), [Plan-003](./003-runtime-node-attach.md) (§Notes — the `SessionEventLog` seam record carrying the same async-transactional re-point), [cross-plan-dependencies.md §2 Package Path Ownership Map](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md#2-package-path-ownership-map).

I-005-11 and I-005-12 verify canonically here and for the first time: each names a single enforcer this PR authors — `provider/usage-delta-accountant.ts` as the single metering path both normalizers emit through, and `provider/thread-frame-router.ts` as the single routing decision both normalizers consult — and the assertions each invariant's Test clause enumerates (AC18's cumulative-sequence / interleaved-attribution / fresh-first-reading / replay-seeded / resume-prior-emitted-sum / compaction-no-re-base / late-frame / floor-hit / cross-check-mismatch / nested-cache / failed-identity set, and AC19's foreign-thread-suppression / connection-scoped-pass / unknown-family-quarantine / absent-identity-quarantine / quarantine-shed / pending-hold-release / hold-timeout-shed / child-spend-attribution / compaction-child-run-scope / child-request-carve-through / surviving-lifecycle-pair set) land with them. I-005-2 (T3.12's flag declaration, T3.15's per-leg gating), I-005-3 (T3.13's MCP idempotency floor), I-005-4 (T3.15's unsupported-implies-degraded legs) and I-005-5 (T3.14's resume-failure surfacing `recovery-needed`) re-verify at the driver-enrichment layer on the established multi-layer convention this plan's PR #159 / #369 / #372 / #375 entries all invoke; their SDK-layer re-verification sites stay at T4.6 (I-005-2, I-005-4) and T4.7 (I-005-5) exactly as those entries projected, while I-005-3's canonical site is neither — it is the T1.3 ingress-default contract test already recorded in this manifest's PR #157 entry.
      I-005-10 is recorded on an explicitly PARTIAL basis rather than silently or by omission, because the shipment demonstrates part of it with a test the invariant itself names: of the four tests I-005-10's Test clause enumerates, only "the binding row's version equals the spawned process's own report under launcher drift (T3.23)" lands here — the three probe-table / zero-billed-turn assertions are T3.24's and auto-update suppression is T3.25's, both PR-D. The conjuncts verified at this shipment are therefore admission-by-version-alone (floor compare, fail-closed refusal before any capability probe and before any billed turn) and one-reading identity (the version compared, the version recorded on the binding, and the version of the process that runs the session are the same reading, asserted against a launcher naming a different build); per-capability probe resolution with its detection source, and the disabled auto-updater that keeps the reading true for the session's life, are NOT verified here. The alternative — omitting it on the PR #369 discriminator, that a shipment structurally cannot demonstrate the invariant — does not apply: this one can, and does, for two of its conjuncts.
      Shipped surface: the T3.11 normalizer-enrichment band — `driver-diagnostics.ts` as the off-timeline default-branch surface both normalizers route an unmapped or `typePending` kind to (never a `session_events` envelope and never a silent drop), the bounded reorder buffer with its paired overflow diagnostic and counter, and the two modules the 2026-08-28 NS-91 amendment specified: `usage-delta-accountant.ts` (one base register per provider thread and axis advanced in stream order, each interval attributed to the turn the metered frame itself names, two-armed base establishment with a provider-native resume basing at the daemon's own prior-emitted sum, no re-base on compaction, and a floor-at-zero plus diagnostic on a falsified cumulative declaration) and `thread-frame-router.ts` (family-scoped classification ahead of any projection decision, registration ahead of the refusal rule, a bounded pending-registration hold distinct from the bounded quarantine, and both usage counters and child-raised interactive requests carved out ahead of suppression) — plus the provider-neutral `terminal-emission-gate.ts` hoist; T3.12's capability declaration, CLI-version floor and `CapabilityRefreshScheduler` cadence with change-detected emission; T3.13's MCP idempotency floor and server-status census; T3.14's lifecycle / intervention / resume enrichment; T3.15's R8 parity legs with `callback-tool-host.ts`; and T3.23's `version-gate.ts` — resolve, spawn, in-band read (Claude `get_binary_version`; the Codex `initialize` `userAgent` extraction with its refuse-rather-than-shell-out rule), floor compare, and the same reading persisted at the T2.2 binding write seam.
      Withheld tasks, and why (the PR's own Review Notes, promoted from residuals to the shipped-set decision): T3.12 is withheld because its in-row C-8 duty (`ProviderModel.effortLevels` + `listModels` current-catalog carriage) is an open contracts gap this PR did not land — the cadence scheduler, floor gate, and refresh legs it also owns did land and are exercised by the shipped tests; T3.15 is withheld because leg 3's dispatch-path contract (the driver invokes the daemon-injected dispatcher and no invocation is left unanswered) is not yet true end-to-end — `CallbackToolHost` is unit-verified on both halves but the composition-root injection into both drivers' callback seam is unwired, and every degradation is fail-closed meanwhile (a seam-absent host answers `denied` plus a diagnostic). Codex callback-tool registration is structurally unreachable at the pin (`dynamicTools` sits behind `experimentalApi: false`) and is withheld at spawn rather than attempted; the Codex-leg `frameRouterFor` / `usageAccountantFor` get-or-create asymmetry against the now-non-creating Claude accessors is accepted as-is with closing it named for the T3.15 completion slice. Both rows complete in the Phase-3 remainder alongside T3.20/T3.22/T3.24/T3.25/T3.26.
      Review: lead-orchestrated rather than run through the /plan-execution pipeline, with skill-compatible artifacts maintained. A consolidated 21-finding fold (`47f2c2a9`) wired the router, accountant and reorder buffer into both driver session paths; its most serious self-found defect was the Codex usage reader consuming `params.usage.total` where the pin publishes `params.tokenUsage.total`, so nothing would have metered against a real Codex — fixed with a negative control. A focused post-gate review of the new Claude inbound-frame seam (`c2665f42`) folded 4 ACTIONABLE + 2 POLISH (+16 tests, each with a mutation negative control), and a round-3 user review plus a focused independent review adjudicated three externally-reported P1s — two refuted at head with line evidence (the router and accountant ARE wired into both production lifecycles; the scheduler's stale-poll overwrite is generation-guarded and test-pinned) and one confirmed and fixed (the refresh scheduler had zero production callers, closed by an additive-optional, exception-contained `NodeRegistry` lifecycle observer and the new `provider/node-provider-plane.ts` binder) — plus 1 ACTIONABLE + 4 POLISH on the Codex rewind rebind (+17 tests). Codex-bot was rate-limited across both external rounds, so the rewind-rebind code was reviewed by a focused independent pass instead; that is recorded here rather than left to read as a clean Codex round.
      The same PR carried an operator-directed provider re-pin — codex-cli `0.149.1` -> `0.150.1` and Claude Code `2.1.245` -> `2.1.251`. The census claims that measured it (ServerNotification full-list set difference 75 -> 79 with the default and `--experimental` unions re-verified identical, and the `thread/realtime/*` family eight -> eleven by addition) are NOT restated here as this entry's own claims: their primary-source homes are the dated ADR-018 §Decision Log row this PR added and the regenerated provider-wire references (`docs/reference/provider-wire/codex.md` / `claude.md`), which carry the regeneration provenance (`codex app-server generate-json-schema` at the pinned binary) a reviewer reproduces the counts from. The ratified floors are unchanged, so admission stays floor-compare only. The round-3 doc rider registered the previously-unrecorded `packages/runtime-daemon/src/node/` ownership row in cross-plan-dependencies.md §2 (Plan-003 Phase 2 CREATE, with its two sanctioned in-place extenders). No census moves: no wire method, error code, event type, table, or column is minted, error-contracts.md is untouched, and the realtime-family recount is a reference-pin erratum that moves none of the tracked censuses.
