```yaml
    spec_coverage:
      [
        "Spec-005 §Required Behavior (prose delivery, frame origin, runtime tripwire)",
        "Spec-005 §Canonical Transcript Export And Replay (the projection-never-a-store rule and the ordered five-step pipeline; the four normative memo properties)",
        "Spec-005 §Interfaces And Contracts (the two new driver operations `exportTranscript` / `replayTranscript`)",
        "Spec-005 §Parity Capability Mechanism Grades (the provider-bound text-neutrality row)",
        "Spec-005 §Fallback Behavior (a memo-settled operation reports `degraded` and carries the declared-loss list)",
        "Spec-005 §Pitfalls To Avoid (no invisible sentinel; no mutation upstream of the driver; no re-minted tool-call ids; no strip-after-repair; no cached render)",
      ]
    notes: |
      Phase-3 tasks T3.18, T3.19 and T3.21 shipped as one contracts+daemon PR — the PR-C slice of the phase's four-PR partition (PR-A: T3.1-T3.10 as PR #375; PR-B: T3.11-T3.15 + T3.23 as PR #378). Nineteen of the phase's twenty-four declared tasks are merged; T3.20, T3.22, T3.24, T3.25 and T3.26 remain, so the phase stays partially_shipped under declared-subset-of-shipped and NO Gate-5 entry re-resolves — the two { plan: 005, phase: 3, status: merged } entries repo-wide, Plan-005 Phase 3B and Plan-005 Phase 5, stay held. T3.20 additionally holds on an external leg: CP-005-13 (registered 2026-08-30 by the machine-authored-prose amendment, §6 node NS-94, reciprocal to Plan-006's CP-006-15) dispatches it only after Plan-006 Phase 3B merges, because a post-replay assertion over content-free turns cannot distinguish an accepted replay from a silently dropped one. T3.19 is deliberately not held by that obligation — its fold and export are correct over a body-less log, which is the state they were authored against.
      verifies_invariant = the T3.18 / T3.19 / T3.21 audit-marker union {I-005-7, I-005-8, I-005-9}. I-005-7 verifies canonically and in full: its Enforced-by clause names T3.18 alone — the shared outbound frame boundary, the tripwire classification, and the encapsulated child-stdin handle that makes bypassing the frame writer unrepresentable rather than merely discouraged — and every test that clause enumerates ships here. I-005-8 and I-005-9 are each recorded on an explicitly PARTIAL basis, with the split stated rather than implied. I-005-8 is enforced by T3.19 and T3.20; T3.19's half lands (the projection-never-a-store property, the ordered pipeline whose step order is asserted by a deliberately reordered pipeline failing the pairing assertion, and the stable bidirectional tool-call id map), while T3.20's post-replay assertion — the conjunct holding that a provider's success return is not evidence a replay worked — is PR-D's and is not verified here. I-005-9 is enforced by T3.21 and T3.22; T3.21's half lands (the memo floor's `degraded` settlement, the always-present `conversation_history_summarized` declared loss, and the tight-budget property that a memo never splits a tool exchange), while T3.22's permanent-refusal classification — a structurally invalid history called exactly once rather than retried — is PR-D's. Both are recorded rather than omitted on the PR #369 discriminator: that entry omitted I-005-4 because a contracts-and-migration shipment structurally could not demonstrate it, whereas each of these lands a test the invariant's own Test clause names.
      Shipped surface: T3.18's `drivers/outbound-frame.ts` — the closed daemon-local origin union (deliberately not hoisted onto the wire-facing contract, since a client-chosen origin is the one input that must never be client-chosen), the coarse command-shaped-first-word predicate, the transport-only single-leading-newline neutralization that never mutates persisted, evented, replayed or participant-visible text, the neutralize-on-absent-or-unrecognized-origin default, and the correlation-minting tripwire whose trip fails the run with `driver.text_neutralization_failed` on the run's ordinary `run.failed` terminal (`failureCategory: 'provider failure'`, `recoveryCondition: 'recovery-needed'`, and a `providerFailureDetail` in the fixed `<code> origin=<...>` form) and disposes the run's provider binding rather than recording a zero-turn success as a completed turn; the additive-optional closed-literal `refusalCode` on `DriverInterventionResult` / `DriverInterventionResultSchema` as a Phase-3 EXTEND of the T1.6 contract, best-effort by construction with the run terminal as the guarantee. T3.19's `transcript/canonical-transcript.ts` + `transcript/transform-pipeline.ts` fold and ordered five-step pipeline, the `exportTranscript` / `replayTranscript` operation shapes, the `DeclaredLossKind` closed union, and the `transcript_replay` capability flag with migration `0012-transcript-capability-backfill.ts` landing its `supported = 0` row per cached `driver_name` in the same ordinal that widens the union — the row set needing the backfill because the writer's snapshot reader enforces exact flag cardinality, while the CHECK needs no second widening because migration 0011 already admits all fourteen values. T3.21's `transcript/memo-projection.ts` floor: a budget as a fraction of the target's context window, whole-exchange eviction behind a protected set (the newest exchange plus the newest N tool-bearing exchanges wherever they sit, replacing the originally-authored recency window that silently dropped older tool exchanges), a once-only key DERIVED from the projection and the target and carried visibly on the memo turn rather than held in a durable claim, and reconcile-before-every-send so an ack-loss delivers exactly once and an unreadable target sends nothing.
      Review ran five rounds, every finding folded and every thread replied to and resolved: round 1 (eight codex findings, 5 P1 / 3 P2), a nine-finding round-2 fold, a four-finding codex round 3 (including steer delivery classified at the transport — `unsent` / `refused` / `indeterminate`, fail-closed to indeterminate — and participant text routed through the encrypted content port after the fold's clear-payload read was found to drop every real participant turn silently), a three-finding round 4, and a rounds-5-terminal fold in which the remaining findings were adjudicated at the orchestrator and fixed rather than deferred.
      The terminal round's largest change is the outbound-frame attribution redesign (`837a8b6e`): frames now declare a role at registration (`turn-opening` | `turn-joining`); items and envelope evidence credit only the turn-opening frame, and a turn-joining steer is consumed on the provider's answer to its own request (`recordRequestAnswered`) — receipt evidence that the provider took the frame, deliberately NOT proof the model read its text, with the accepted residual stated as a conjunction in the tripwire's class doc (wire text survives compose in command-effective shape AND the request is answered AND an interception layer drops the text, on a request path not known to parse client commands). The reviewer's remedy was a disjunction — serialize provider-bound frames on a turn, or require per-frame evidence — and the second branch was taken because serialization would hold steers to turn boundaries, a product change to the steering surface rather than a tripwire fix. Six steer regression pins inverted from trip to pass in that commit, stated plainly rather than absorbed: the previous oldest-unevidenced heuristic caught a swallowed steer only when item timing happened to expose it (the same round documents its false PASS) and false-tripped delivered steers under delayed-item timing, so the inversion trades a luck-dependent catch that came bundled with false trips for a deterministic boundary — an unanswered steer (timeout, dead connection) trips at settlement, a zero-turn terminal still trips the turn-opening frame, and an unrecognized envelope still trips every frame, answered or not.
      One escalated design decision is recorded rather than buried: T3.19's `TranscriptContentSource` port is declared and deliberately unimplemented, because Spec-006's assistant / tool payload shapes are metadata-shaped and Spec-022's PII map confirms no durable home for assistant prose, reasoning bodies or tool bodies — so the fold takes content through a port rather than minting the second record ADR-029 forbids. It was answered before this PR merged, by the 2026-08-30 machine-authored-prose amendment (§6 node NS-94, PR #383), which registers CP-005-13 and routes T3.20's consumption through Plan-006's `HydratedSessionEvent` projection. Two in-PR flake fixes are test-harness-only and touch no production file: per-spawn `--user-data-dir` isolation plus SIGTERM-then-SIGKILL (and win32 `taskkill /t`) shim termination for the desktop launch smoke test, and a 45s file-scoped budget for the turn-snapshot OID-determinism case whose root cause was subprocess budget under load, not determinism. One defect was flagged and NOT fixed, owner named: `turn-snapshot-service.ts` `#normalizeEmbeddedRepositories` wraps `rev-parse --verify HEAD` in a bare catch that turns an infrastructure failure into a durable embedded-repo skip claim the restore leg trusts.
      Census: no move. Migration 0012 widens no CHECK, rebuilds no table and adds no column — it inserts one `supported = 0` `transcript_replay` row per already-cached `driver_name` — so the local SQLite census stays 58; the `transcript_replay` flag had already taken the Spec-005 matrix from thirteen to fourteen at NS-84, so this shipment lands the code for a doc census that had already moved; `driver.text_neutralization_failed` was registered reserved at NS-76 and neither this PR nor PR #378 touches error-contracts.md, so no error-code row is added; and no wire method, event type, table or column is minted. `DriverInterventionResult` takes one additive-optional closed-literal member with `.strict()` retained, which changes no shipped field's type and makes no merged manifest entry mean something different.
```

- 2026-08-28 — **Usage-delta accounting and child-thread frame routing (§6 node NS-91).** Status flipped `approved → review` and restored `approved` in the same diff, jointly with Spec-005; both audit-relevant §Preconditions boxes were Re-opened and Delivered in-diff. **Classification: FLIP** per the runbook [§Status Flip Rule](../operations/plan-implementation-readiness-audit-runbook.md) rows 3 and 4 together — the swap mints invariants **I-005-11** and **I-005-12** and changes mandated driver behavior in the plan body. **Legs.** (1) Provider usage counters are cumulative for the provider session on both pinned surfaces and both fold cache-read tokens inside their input figure; T3.11 gains the owning leg (`provider/usage-delta-accountant.ts`), emitting per-axis deltas against one register per provider thread and axis advanced in stream order with named-turn attribution, no re-base at a compaction, fresh-zero versus resume-at-prior-emitted-sum base establishment, and a floored-plus-diagnostic response to a falsified cumulative declaration. Left unowned this would have overstated session spend by conversation length — measured 22× on a long thread — directly on the `Spec-016 §Session Cost Receipt` committed-spend fold. (2) Both providers multiplex child-thread traffic over the session's connection; T3.11 gains the routing leg (`provider/thread-frame-router.ts`) — fail-closed thread-identity routing, bounded quarantine for an absent or unrecognized identity, and a usage carve-out applied ahead of suppression — and **T3.14 records the split explicitly**: its terminal-emission boundary consumes the routing decision and does not own it. **No CP minted here.** Both rules constrain only what this plan emits over already-registered `usage_telemetry` members and already-registered `subagent.*` kinds; Spec-016's receipt fold already sums per-run rows additively and re-prices nothing at read time. The cross-document registration the PR #377 round-1 fold added lives with its owners rather than as a CP on this plan: Spec-006's §Usage Telemetry re-describes `usage.token_count` as a per-turn figure whose scope totals fold by summation (flip-and-restored `approved` in the same swap, description-only), and Plan-016's CP-016-9 registers the budget accountant's sum-fold consumption of those rows on a recorded no-flip. **No census moves** (counted, not assumed): no table, wire method, error code, or event type is added; the two new modules are `provider/`-level daemon siblings, not `provider/drivers/` members, so the ownership map's thirteen-file enumeration is unmoved; and the §Campaign B10 Amendment Scope quantifiers (eighteen corpus-item enrichments, ten CLI-currency duties, seven R8 parity legs) are unmoved — the two new legs carry no P-, C-, or R8-series id and are scoped to this amendment, not to the B10 delta. **Known limit, recorded:** the cache-read / cache-write split lives on the driver diagnostic band because `usage_telemetry` registers no per-cache-axis member; minting one would mint a member ahead of its reader, which §Capability discovery rejects for capability flags on reasoning that applies unchanged here. **Round-1 fold (2026-08-28, PR #377 Codex review — eight accepted findings, folded as one redesign rather than eight patches).** The delta base is re-specified as one register per provider thread and axis advanced in stream order with named-turn attribution (the per-turn-keyed base it replaces double-counted interleaved turns); base establishment splits fresh-zero — replay-seeded included, the first reading metering in full — from resume-at-prior-emitted-sum (the unconditional zero-meter first reading dropped a fresh session's first billed turn); the wire-declared per-turn figure becomes a corroborating cross-check; the containment subtraction is gated on the breakdown's own arithmetic identity; the routing rule is family-scoped against the pinned census with a parent-linked registration path, a bounded pending-registration hold distinct from the quarantine, and a mandatory child-request carve-through per `Spec-016 §Provider-Native Subagents`; and the evidence is re-graded honestly — the vendor's generated schema (codex-cli `0.150.1`, at-pin regeneration a fixture-time obligation) carries the Verified shape claims, the reference-implementation audit re-graded Derived for the runtime behaviors a schema cannot show. T3.11's two legs, I-005-11 / I-005-12, AC18 / AC19, and the Phase-3 acceptance mapping are rewritten to match; no invariant name, task id, census, or marker line moves.
- 2026-08-27 — **Phase-3 transcript-quartet errata adjudication (§6 node NS-89).** Status flipped `approved → review` and restored `approved` in the same diff, Spec-005 untouched; the audit-relevant §Preconditions box Re-opened and Delivered in-diff. **Classification:** PR #374 began erratum-class — four Files-field corrections aligning T3.20/T3.22 with content the corpus already carried — and its review folds crossed the flip line by moving task obligations, so the growth takes the ceremony while the baseline stays erratum. **The growth, from nine accepted Codex findings (three each in rounds 1, 3, and 4) plus three self-initiated hardenings — the AC12 re-home, the memo floor's caller-side suppression clause (keeping the new Consumes edges acyclic), and the positional read's counting-unit narrowing:** T3.22's Provides owns both arms of the permanent-vs-transient distinction — the transient arm retries only **definitely-unsent** failures, and an applied-but-unacknowledged outcome is reconciled **positionally** — the target's readback turn count against the daemon's acknowledged count, never content matching, since ordinary turns carry no identity marker and participant text may repeat — with an honest arm where readback is unavailable: the turn fails visibly rather than risking duplicate spend or a suppressed request; The amendment is [Plan-006](./006-session-event-taxonomy-and-audit-log.md)'s: its new Phase 3B creates `session_events.content_payload`, the durable encrypted home for assistant- and tool-generated prose, sealed under a session-scoped key — a stored, re-wrappable per-session DEK. This plan is the consumer and the reason the column exists — [ADR-029](../decisions/029-canonical-transcript-is-authoritative.md) makes the canonical transcript authoritative for a provider session's content while `Spec-005 §Canonical Transcript Export And Replay` scopes that content to what the Spec-006 taxonomy carries, and the taxonomy carried no assistant or tool body at all. **Delivered in-diff:** **CP-005-13** (⇄ CP-006-15, consumer-side, authoring nothing on Plan-006's side) and a **task-scoped dispatch hold on T3.20**, which waits on Plan-006 Phase 3B merging. The hold's scope is the judgment: **T3.19 is deliberately not held** — its fold and export are correct over a body-less log, the state they were authored against, and holding the phase would block work this column does not block — while T3.20 is, because its post-replay assertion is `Spec-005`'s only admissible evidence a replay worked and over content-free turns that assertion cannot separate a provider that accepted every frame from one that silently dropped them all. **NS-92's residuals (1) and (2) are explicitly not discharged here** and are recorded as still owed by the swap that registers the fold's emission, which is in flight as PR #379 at authoring time; taking either would land a new requirement on an open PR mid-review and flip Spec-005 to author a clause whose producer is being written elsewhere. **No census moves:** no wire method, event type, error code, Cedar action, table, or column; the capability matrix stays **seventeen**, the `ProviderDriver` operation surface **eighteen**, the client-facing `driver.*` set **nine**, invariants **thirteen**, SQLite **58**, and Postgres **26**. One count moves and is re-derived by counting the section's `### CP-005-N` blocks: cross-plan obligations twelve → **thirteen**, mirrored into the §Done Checklist row.
- 2026-08-26 — **Canonical transcript export and replay (§6 node NS-84).** Status flipped `approved → review` and restored `approved` in the same diff, jointly with Spec-005; both audit-relevant §Preconditions boxes re-opened and re-checked in-diff. [ADR-029](../decisions/029-canonical-transcript-is-authoritative.md) lands `accepted` in the same swap (ADR census 27 → 28). Adds Phase-3 tasks **T3.19–T3.22**, invariants **I-005-8** / **I-005-9**, and **CP-005-10**; one capability flag (`transcript_replay`) and two driver operations join the contract — re-derived by direct count against `Spec-005 §Interfaces And Contracts` and `Spec-005 §Per-Driver Capability Matrix`, the operation axis reads **sixteen** and the flag axis **fourteen**, with the canonical `ProviderDriver` mirror in [api-payload-contracts.md](../architecture/contracts/api-payload-contracts.md) extended in the same swap (a spec that requires an operation its own canonical mirror does not declare is the mirror gap this plan's §Count-claim re-derivation rule now names on both axes rather than only the flag one). Every new task rides Phase 3's own already-met gates, so no phase gate moves and no born-unchecked box is minted. One **correction** rides the swap: the matrix's Codex `model_mutation` cell said `false` while this spec's own §Provider Parameter Vocabularies recorded a per-turn `model` override — the pinned wire reference resolves the contradiction in the vocabulary section's favour and the cell is now `true`. The `transcript_replay` Claude cell is deliberately **probe-valued** rather than a static `true`: no stable seeding contract is published for that provider, and asserting one would be a fail-open claim about a surface the vendor calls internal.

- 2026-08-18 — **Provider-account seam + typed usage-limit signal amendment, with its restoring targeted readiness-audit delta taken in one swap: Status flipped `approved → review` and restored `approved` in this same PR, jointly with Spec-005; both §Preconditions boxes re-opened and re-checked here.** The amendment closes two seams the account plane needs and this plan's §Non-Goals had previously excluded by drawing "provider commercial or billing concerns" wide enough to cover facts that are not commercial at all. **Leg 1 — the typed provider usage-limit signal (T3.16, I-005-6, CP-005-8).** The design question was whether provider exhaustion is a `RecoveryCondition`. It is not, and conflating them would be a real defect rather than a taxonomy quibble: a recovery condition asks a human to reconcile, while a usage limit resolves on its own at a known boundary and asks the runtime to wait — routing the second through the first would page an operator for something that fixes itself, and would leave the run parked after the boundary passed. It is therefore a **sibling axis**: a closed-set cause plus an optional reset boundary carrying its **own** provenance, so a consumer can distinguish a boundary the provider stated from one the runtime derived and never presents a guess as a fact. Recognition is **typed-only**, and the invariant is enforced by a seeded discriminating control that feeds each normalizer prose and an exit code a text-matching implementation would classify as a limit and requires no signal — the assertion fails against exactly the implementation the rule forbids. The two provider legs are deliberately **asymmetric in trust rather than in contract**: the Codex leg normalizes published account rate-limit shapes, while the Claude leg normalizes a retry notification that carries no documented boundary field, so its boundary is marked runtime-derived and the leg is recorded at Derived trust. No capability flag is added — the obligation is uniform across drivers in the `probeAuth` pattern, and a driver with nothing to report emits nothing rather than declaring a false capability, so the V1 matrix is restated **unwidened**. **Leg 2 — provider-account identity at spawn (T3.17, CP-005-9).** Additive-optional on the session-creation and resume parameter shapes, opaque to the driver, persisted through the existing `runtime_bindings.spawn_config` carrier so resume rebinds to the account the run was admitted against rather than re-resolving the current default — without which a default changed mid-session would silently move an in-flight run's billing. The identity is server-resolved and server-stamped, so a client-supplied value is an input to resolution and never the recorded outcome. Gate walk: **Gate 1** — Spec-005 flipped and restored jointly, its growth being exactly the §Fallback Behavior sibling-axis bullet, the §Interfaces And Contracts account-seam bullet, and the §Non-Goals narrowing. **Gate 2** — both new tasks carry the full marker set; no shipped task is edited and no merged PR's manifest entry changes meaning. **Gate 3** — I-005-6 is grounded in a named Spec-005 clause rather than in a sibling plan, and CP-005-8 / CP-005-9 are each bidirectionally surfaced at their Plan-029 counterparts. **Gate 4** — every `Spec coverage:` marker uses the unbackticked spec-plus-heading form (a `Spec-NNN` label followed by the § heading text) byte-compared against the real headings, and T3.17's `none` arm return-cites Plan-029's invariants rather than restating them; the corpus cite screen returned `cite anomalies: none` across 29/29 plans, having first been shown able to fail on three real defects in the sibling Plan-029 earlier in the same session. **Gate 5** — the new work is homed in a **Phase 3B** supplement gated on Phase 3, following the campaign-B10 catch-up precedent, so the shipped Phases 1–2 stay closed and truthful; the supplement carries no carrier box, because its only external dependency is Plan-029's resolver, which consumes this seam rather than producing it. The set-quantifier claim in §Done Checklist moved 7 → 9 obligations in the same diff. **Line-cite drift swept in the same diff.** Inserting the amendment blockquote at the head of Spec-005 shifted every line below it by two to four, so all nineteen inbound `Spec-005:NNN` cites were content-compared against their pre-edit targets rather than assumed intact — the corpus cite gate is a truncation floor and would have passed every one of them silently, each stale number still landing on plausible neighbouring prose. Six **live** cites were repaired by converting them to §-anchor form anchored on the citing text's described content, never on whatever now sits at the stale number: two in [Local SQLite Schema](../architecture/schemas/local-sqlite-schema.md), both of which had pointed into the recovery-consequences region and now cite `Spec-005 §Recovery Consequences`; and four in [API Payload Contracts](../architecture/contracts/api-payload-contracts.md) — two that had pointed into the fallback-behavior region, now citing `Spec-005 §Fallback Behavior`, and two that had pointed into the tool-metadata idempotency region, now citing `Spec-005 §idempotency_class`. The `spec_coverage` cites inside this plan's own §Shipment Manifest are deliberately **left untouched**: they are immutable provenance recording what each merged PR cited at merge time, not live pointers, and the corpus rule against retroactively editing them is stated explicitly at Plan-002's PR #105 entry and Plan-010's PR #309 entry. A reader resolving those numbers must read them against the spec as of that PR's merge commit, which is what a shipment record means. **Audit outcome: pass.**

- 2026-08-25 — **Provider-bound text neutrality amendment, with its restoring targeted readiness-audit delta taken in one swap: Status flipped `approved → review` and restored `approved` in this same PR, jointly with Spec-005; both §Preconditions boxes re-opened and re-checked here.** The amendment closes a delivery hazard neither doc had ever named: a provider CLI whose programmatic input surface also parses client-side commands consumes a message whose first word is command-shaped, and the participant's words never reach the model. Verified first-party 2026-08-25 rather than inherited — Claude Code `2.1.245` answers such a message with a synthetic client-side reply and a **zero-turn success**: no model call, no billed spend, and no error, which is the worst shape a failure can take, because every layer above reads it as a completed turn. The same probes confirmed a single leading newline restores an ordinary model turn and that codex-cli `0.149.1` `exec` passes the identical text through verbatim. **Three rules, not one transform.** The transform alone would have been a guess about a parser this repo neither owns nor pins, so it is paired with a closed **frame-origin** discriminator whose absent/unrecognized arm neutralizes — deliberately not a capability flag, because an undeclared flag resolves through `Spec-005 §Required Behavior`'s undeclared-is-unsupported rule to "not neutralized", which is the fail-open polarity — and with a **runtime tripwire** that fails the turn when a provider dispatches a frame as a command anyway. **Design choices worth recording.** Coverage is structural: the child process's writable handle is encapsulated by the frame writer, so a later provider-bound path cannot bypass neutralization by forgetting to call it, where an audited call-site list decays on its first addition. The origin union stays **daemon-local** rather than hoisted to `packages/contracts/src/provider-driver.ts`, because a wire-facing origin would imply a caller supplies it — the one input that must never be client-chosen. Invisible and zero-width sentinels are **prohibited**, not merely unpreferred: that encoding is a live exfiltration channel that hardened pipelines strip, so a neutralizer built on it fails open exactly where hardening is present. And neutralizing anywhere upstream of the driver is prohibited, because text mutated before persistence is text the audit log no longer matches and a rewind no longer reproduces. The tripwire's **landing** is stated outright rather than left to a reader to derive, and it is **one** landing for both provider-bound paths: the daemon emits the run's ordinary `run.failed` terminal carrying `failureCategory: 'provider failure'`, `recoveryCondition: 'recovery-needed'`, and a `providerFailureDetail` in one fixed daemon-composed form — the code, one space byte, then `origin=` and one of `participant_text` / `system_narration` / `unknown` — pinned to that spelling because a detail no consumer can parse would make _machine-readable_ a claim the text does not cash, and because an origin the union does not name must be recorded as the literal `unknown` rather than echoed into the audit log. That is CP-005-5's already-resolved handoff shape, so the trip mints no driver event, no result variant, and no persisted column — and no state transition either: `starting -> failed` and `running -> failed` are both already declared ([run-state-machine.md §Complete Transition Table](../domain/run-state-machine.md#complete-transition-table)), and those are precisely the two states a provider-bound frame can be in flight in. Neither path raises a JSON-RPC error: `startRun` is daemon-internal with no wire method behind it, and an intervention answers on its own result branch, settling `degraded` on the existing zero-effect arm (dispatch occurred, so pre-dispatch `rejected` is definitionally unavailable per [queue-and-intervention-model.md §Driver Result To Lifecycle Mapping](../domain/queue-and-intervention-model.md#driver-result-to-lifecycle-mapping)) with no `fallbackAction` — no fallback exists, since re-sending the same text meets the same parser — and no cause of its own. That last absence has a sharper reason than the shipped envelope's shape: routing a cause onto the caller's response would need Plan-004's intervention-result mapper to consume a driver-side signal it has no channel for, which is cross-plan work this amendment neither owns nor needs, the cause resting durably on the run's own terminal and readable live or on replay. Two consequences are deliberate: the record says _undelivered_ rather than _no effect_, and an intervention-path trip **fails the run**, because a provider that dispatched the frame as a command may have run an unrecognized client-side action against the session state that run depends on (both this landing and that polarity were corrected at the PR's Codex rounds; see the round-1 and round-2 adjudications below). The domain doc's `degraded` trigger cell was extended by one clause in this same diff so the two surfaces cannot drift. The third origin arm was audited on the same standard as the second rather than left silent: `system_narration` carries exactly one V1 producer as of 2026-08-26 — the memo projection, a daemon-composed turn on the text-input channel that declares this origin and is neutralized like any other daemon prose (the audit read `nil` before that amendment and is re-derived, not restated) — while the goal composition Spec-005 grades `emulated` for Claude reaches the provider through system-prompt composition and `respondToRequest` delivers a `requestId`-correlated structured reply, so neither is a producer rather than a turn on the text-input channel — the arm is kept distinct from `participant_text`, which it matches mechanically, so a tripped frame's record says whose words failed to arrive. Gate walk: **Gate 1** — Spec-005 flipped and restored jointly, its growth being exactly the three §Required Behavior rules, the §Parity Capability Mechanism Grades row (both legs `emulated` — the Codex cell recording that its only verbatim-delivery evidence is `codex exec`, a different frontend from the `turn/start` transport the driver drives, so a confirming probe of that transport re-grades it `native` by ordinary amendment), the two §Pitfalls To Avoid prohibitions, and one appended §Acceptance Criteria bullet (AC8). **Gate 2** — T3.18 carries the full marker set; no shipped task is edited and no merged PR's manifest entry changes meaning. Three path-drift errata were repaired in the same diff (T3.16's two `src/drivers/` spellings and T3.17's `src/providers/` spelling named directories that do not exist; the canonical topology is `provider/drivers/…` and `provider/runtime-binding-store.ts`, as the §2 ownership row and the shipped tree both record); the two never-shipped spellings are now registry-guarded in [`canonical-paths.json`](../../tools/docs-corpus/canonical-paths.json), so a third recurrence is denied at pre-commit rather than found by a reader. The pre-push ripple sweep surfaced one further erratum this task's own mint activated: [Plan-029](./029-provider-accounts-and-credential-homes.md) CP-029-2 resolved the typed usage-limit signal to "Plan-005 Phase 3B, task T3.18", a number that resolved to nothing before this amendment and would have resolved to the wrong task after it — the signal is **T3.16**, as this plan's reciprocal CP-005-8 states — repaired here as an erratum-class cite fix carrying no Status flip. **Gate 3** — I-005-7 is grounded in a named Spec-005 clause rather than in a sibling plan, and the task mints **no** cross-plan obligation: the Spec-017 escape relationship is recorded as reviewer-checkable task prose on the NS-73 precedent, since a phase-level gate would hold Phase-3 tasks that have no such dependency. **Gate 4** — every `Spec coverage:` marker uses the unbackticked spec-plus-heading form byte-compared against the real headings, and the two stale `error-contracts.md` line-range cites in §API And Transport Changes were **converted to the `§Driver` anchor rather than renumbered**, with that bullet's enumeration completed in the same touch (it claimed three codes while naming two — `driver.timeout` had been omitted since authoring) and extended to the seventh. **Gate 5** — T3.18 is homed in the **already-gated Phase 3**, not in a new phase and not in Phase 3B: Phase 3B gates on `{ plan: 005, phase: 3, status: merged }`, which would push the neutralization behind the very driver code it must cover. Phase 3's own three gates are met (PRs #157 / #159 / #247), so T3.18 dispatches with Phase 3 and no gate moves. **Census.** One move: `driver.text_neutralization_failed` registers reserved in [error-contracts.md §Driver](../architecture/contracts/error-contracts.md#driver) (409 — the `driver.cli_version_unparseable` blocked-until-repair family; 502/503 would falsely blame provider availability), taking that namespace to seven codes. **One wire member is minted** and nothing else — no interface operation, no method string, no table, and no column. The driver-result **status** vocabulary is restated unwidened at `applied | degraded` while the result envelope takes that one additive-optional member — the closed-literal `DriverInterventionResult.refusalCode`, authored by T3.18 as a Phase-3 EXTEND of the Plan-005-owned `packages/contracts/src/provider-driver.ts` and consumed by Plan-004 T2.6 under the existing CP-004-1 (no new obligation, no shipped field's type changed, `.strict()` retained since strictness rejects only **unknown** keys), the tripwire's refusal landing on the run's own `run.failed` terminal rather than as a third driver result, and that landing was checked against the shipped contract rather than assumed: `packages/contracts/src/provider-driver.ts` fixes `DriverInterventionResultSchema` `.strict()` at exactly `{ status, fallbackAction? }`, and [API Payload Contracts](../architecture/contracts/api-payload-contracts.md) already carries `failureCategory` / `recoveryCondition` / `providerFailureDetail` on the `run.failed` payload — so the refusal reuses surfaces that exist. That check also surfaced one pre-existing erratum this amendment deliberately does **not** repair, recorded here rather than left as a silent dependency: [Spec-006 §Run Lifecycle](../specs/006-session-event-taxonomy-and-audit-log.md#run-lifecycle-run_lifecycle)'s prose payload enumeration for the nine run-state events omits `providerFailureDetail`, which the API-payload mirror has carried since the CP-005-5 / Plan-006 Phase-3 ratification that mirror itself cites. The field is ratified and typed, so this rule rests on it safely; restating it in the owning spec's enumeration is a Plan-006-lane repair, not a Spec-005 one. The SQLite / Postgres / plan / spec / ADR censuses are therefore all unmoved, and the README plan-status census reads unchanged because both flips restore in this same diff. The set-quantifier claims that did move were re-derived in this diff: §Done Checklist's invariant count 5 → 7 (already stale at 5 before this amendment, I-005-6 having landed 2026-08-18) with the missing **Phase 3B** row added, its acceptance-criteria row extended to AC8, and the [cross-plan-dependencies.md §2](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md#2-package-path-ownership-map) `provider/drivers/` row's Phase-3 file count 12 → 13 for `outbound-frame.ts`, which is also appended to this plan's own §Target Areas Phase-3 file list. Three further enumerations of sets this amendment moved were swept in the same diff rather than left to disagree with the checklist rows: the §Test And Verification Plan Phase-3 bullet, which stated AC1 + AC2 + AC3 as the phase's whole verified set and now attributes AC8 to T3.18's own suite; §Rollout Order step 3, which enumerated the Phase-3 landing set without T3.18; and that same step's missing Phase 3B successor, added as a lettered sub-step so steps 4 and 5 keep the ordinals other surfaces cite. Inbound `Spec-005:NNN` cites were re-swept before this spec edit landed, and the sweep found the manifest-immutability rule had been applied one field too wide at the 2026-08-18 entry above. The rule is **field-scoped**, as its own two cited precedents state: Plan-002's PR #105 entry freezes the `spec_coverage` array, and Plan-010's PR #309 entry draws the line explicitly — the manifest entry's `notes:` clause is _discharged in place_ while its `spec_coverage` mirror stays at the shipment-time enumeration. Seventeen of the nineteen references sit in `spec_coverage` arrays across fourteen cite lines and stay untouched as immutable merge-time provenance; the two that sat in `notes:` prose were **drifted and are repaired here** by conversion to `Spec-005 §Acceptance Criteria`, anchored on the AC2 and AC3 content each sentence describes rather than renumbered — this amendment's own §Pitfalls insertion having pushed both criteria further down `Spec-005 §Acceptance Criteria`, leaving the first cite pointing at a blank line and the second at a heading. Both survived every deterministic gate for documented reasons rather than a hook defect: the `Spec-NNN:LL` label form is not floor-checked, and a citer inside a fenced block is exempt from the volatile-cite deny. Outside this plan, the sweep found no live inbound line cite into Spec-005 in `docs/specs/`, `docs/plans/`, `docs/architecture/`, `docs/domain/`, `docs/operations/`, `packages/`, `apps/`, `tools/`, or `.claude/`. The `docs/superpowers/` campaign tree does retain label-less continuation pins into Spec-005 (`:55`, `:78-84`, `:101`, `:106-114`, `:116-128`, `:166`) on checked-complete B3 steps; those are historical authoring records in an exempt citer namespace, left as-is, and named here rather than denied so the next author sweeps the form instead of trusting an absolute. **Round-1 adjudication (this PR, Codex review) — five contract corrections folded into the text above rather than deferred.** (1) The intervention landing was **wrong as first written** and is redesigned: it said the caller receives the wire error _alongside_ the `degraded` settlement, which `packages/contracts/src/jsonrpc.ts` makes unrepresentable — `JsonRpcResponseEnvelope` is a union of the result and error branches precisely so emitting both fails to typecheck — and the `driver.capability_unsupported` precedent it cited is the opposite of a pairing, its cause riding the intervention response and explicitly never the error channel. Round 1's fix followed that precedent — the code riding the `degraded` response as its machine-readable cause on the intervention path, and a wire error only on `startRun`, where no settlement exists to carry one — and **round 2 superseded it below**: the shipped `.strict()` result envelope cannot carry a cause and `startRun` has no wire method behind it, so the landing moved onto the run's own terminal for both paths. What survives from this round is the finding that drove it: a caller cannot receive a settlement and an error together, so the cause was never going to ride an error branch beside the intervention response. (2) The **command-shaped predicate** was named but never defined, which would have let two drivers classify the same message differently; it is now one shared byte-level rule — first non-whitespace byte is `/` — chosen deliberately coarse, since neutralizing prose costs one invisible byte while missing a command costs the whole message. The earlier path-like carve-out is **withdrawn** for that reason, and the discriminating controls invert with it. (3) `Every driver must neutralize` **over-quantified** against this plan's own byte-preservation test for the graded-`native` leg: the frame boundary and the tripwire are universal, the transform is grade-scoped, and the grade is a claim the tripwire keeps honest rather than one that is trusted. (4) The tripwire was wired into the Claude normalizer alone — leaving the leg whose provider _might_ start command-dispatching with no detector for exactly the change the rule anticipates; both normalizers now carry it, with a paired Codex fixture. (5) The Codex `native` grade rested on `codex exec`, a **different frontend** from the app-server `turn/start` transport this driver uses; the grade is held **provisional** and T3.18's currency step probes the driver's real path before any byte-preservation is relied on. Two further round-1 points were answered without a contract change: the registered error now names both neutralized prose origins and carries the origin in its detail (the `system_narration` arm was kept distinct precisely so a tripped frame records whose words failed to arrive), and the composer-escape follow-up is now stated in `Spec-017` as **authored by no task yet** rather than left to read as scheduled work. **Round-2 adjudication (this PR) — six corrections, two of them redesigns rather than patches.** Round 1's fixes (3) and (5) were individually right and jointly fail-open: grade-scoping made `Spec-005 §Parity Capability Mechanism Grades` the behavioral input, and (5) then left that row reading `native` for a leg whose grade the same round had just declared unverified for the transport the driver drives — so the contract instructed the Codex leg to skip the transform on evidence it had itself discounted, and this plan's byte-preservation test would have locked that assumption into code. Patching the rule with a _provisional does not license pass-through_ override was **rejected** as a third pass on one axis; the structural fix is that **a behavioral row states the grade governing today**, so the Codex cell is graded **`emulated`** with its `codex exec` basis and its unprobed `turn/start` transport recorded in the cell, and confirming verbatim delivery re-grades it `native` by ordinary amendment — a success branch the provisional wording never named, which left nothing authorized to clear the marker even after a clean probe. The test follows the same correction: the frame writer takes the grade as an input and both arms run from the test's own declarations, which is checkable, where _did a human run the probe_ is not. And `Spec-017`'s standing refusal on provider-bound composer paths was still justified by a hazard **this amendment closes** — an escaped `//etc/hosts …` arrives at the composer as `/etc/hosts …`, which the predicate above neutralizes — so its stated reason is corrected to the true one: the escape is composer work no task yet authors, and shipping it ahead of the neutralization would re-open the hazard only for as long as the two are out of step. A reason a reader can disprove would have left the gate looking unliftable. **(3) The refusal's landing was redesigned, not patched a third time.** Three rounds had now moved the same sentence — a wire error paired with the settlement, then the cause riding the intervention response — and round 2 established that neither surface can carry it. `packages/contracts/src/provider-driver.ts` ships `DriverInterventionResultSchema` `.strict()` at exactly `{ status, fallbackAction? }`, so the intervention response was read as having nowhere to put a cause without re-opening a merged Phase-1 task, which this amendment's own Gate 2 forbids — **round 3 below reverses that reading**, Phase 3B being this plan's own precedent for additive-optional widening of a merged contract surface; and `startRun` is a daemon-internal lifecycle operation with no registered JSON-RPC method behind it, so _a wire error on the `startRun` path_ named a response branch that does not exist. A third patch on one axis is the signal to redesign, so the landing moved off the driver's return value entirely. The tripwire fires in the normalizer — P1-1's sole terminal-emission boundary, already in T3.18's file list — so the daemon knows the frame tripped without the driver's envelope telling it, and the refusal lands as the run's ordinary `run.failed` terminal. One landing serves both paths, no shipped contract is widened, no Plan-004 file is touched, and the registered code stays registered as a machine-readable cause on a non-error surface — the convention `InterventionResponseBase.rejectionReason` already sets by carrying `driver.capability_unsupported`. **(4) A tripped intervention now fails the run, reversing round 1.** Round 1 recorded that the run is _not_ failed by an intervention-path trip, because that would destroy work the misfire never touched. That reasoning treated the misfire as inert, which is exactly what it is not: the provider _dispatched_ the frame as its own command, so it may have executed a client-side action against the session state the run depends on, and a context clear or a model switch would leave every later turn running against a session the daemon still believes it understands. Continuing was the fail-open branch. `recovery-needed` is this corpus's existing name for _a human reconciles first_, and the terminal run state is what forecloses further dispatch on the binding — so the quarantine this needs costs no `runtime_bindings` column, which this plan had already ruled does not belong there. **(5) The tripwire's detector was inverted.** It asked the driver to recognize _the provider's command-dispatch signature_ — an open set the pinned provider-wire references document nowhere, so a detector enumerating it would have been stale on arrival and stale silently, which is the failure this rule exists to end. It now asks for positive typed evidence that the correlated frame produced a model turn — a closed set the normalizer already parses for its ordinary work — and an unrecognized response envelope trips rather than passes. T3.18's currency step pins the per-provider discriminants, but the polarity is what keeps an unpinned shape loud, so correctness never rests on the pin. **(6) Two definitional residuals were closed in the same pass.** _Whitespace_ in the command-shaped predicate is now enumerated as exactly the six ASCII bytes, because a byte-level rule may not appeal to a Unicode property without the decoding it forbids; the non-ASCII residual is named and left to the tripwire rather than guessed at, trading a named residual for nothing. And I-005-7's discriminating controls still required an already-newline-led message to pass through unchanged — under the round-1 predicate that message's first non-whitespace byte is the `/` after the newline, so it neutralizes; the control had been left behind by the withdrawal of the path-like carve-out and is re-pinned to the predicate as settled. The provider-reference re-pin those probes imply is deliberately **not** in this diff: `docs/reference/provider-wire/` is owned by a concurrent tooling lane, and two vehicles editing the same pin would collide. **Round-3 adjudication (this PR, final round) — the cause gets a typed carrier, and the binding gets quarantined.** Round 2 had concluded that no cause could reach an intervention caller, on the reading that widening the shipped `.strict()` result envelope would re-open a merged Phase-1 task. That reading was **too strict, and this plan's own Phase 3B is the counterexample**: 3B exists precisely to make additive-optional widenings of contract surfaces whose code has merged, on the ground that no shipped field changes type and no shipped call site must change. `.strict()` is no obstacle either — it rejects **unknown** keys, and a declared optional key is not unknown. So `DriverInterventionResult` takes one additive-optional `refusalCode`, declared as a **closed literal union** rather than a string so an envelope that parses untrusted provider output gains no free-form surface and needs no `wireFreeFormString` bound; Plan-004 **T2.6** consumes it under the already-registered **CP-004-1**, whose row widens by one clause on the B13 one-clause precedent — Plan-004 takes that mirror on a **recorded no-flip**, minting no obligation, task, or invariant of its own. The cause is now recorded twice on purpose: a one-shot intervention caller should not have to subscribe to a run's event stream to learn why its text was not delivered, and the run terminal is what a later reader replays. Three further dispositions land with it. The **run-start path's** landing is restated as what it always structurally was — an **asynchronous run outcome** on the existing `run.failed` run-lifecycle kind (registered nowhere new; [Spec-006 §Run Lifecycle](../specs/006-session-event-taxonomy-and-audit-log.md#run-lifecycle-run_lifecycle) already carries it) — with the reason made explicit rather than left to inference: a run admitted from the queue or started by orchestration has **no caller waiting**, so a synchronous refusal would have had nobody to refuse to. The **binding is quarantined**, closing a hole failing the run alone left open: the run ends but the provider process holding possibly-mutated client-side state stays alive, and the next run admitted against that session binds straight back to it — so the trip disposes the live binding — the driver closes the provider session and the process leaves the live provider-process registry — and returning to work is a fresh spawn, which is what _operator restarts_ means mechanically. Disposal is a process fact rather than a row state, which is what keeps T2.1's prohibition on `runtime_bindings` recovery columns intact while minting nothing: T2.2 already rules the store owns no liveness column and the CALLER owns the liveness intersection, so a disposed binding is excluded from delivery by shipped machinery, the refusal takes no attach-time arm (that arm would need the marker being refused), and a process that no longer exists cannot be re-bound by a caller that forgets to read a flag. And the **zero-turn synthetic shape** is now named normatively — a client-composed reply item, no model-turn attribution for the correlated frame, no token usage accounted to it — with the recorded transcript checked in as T3.18's **conformance fixture**. That is deliberately the rule's _instance_, not its definition: the positive-turn-evidence burden and its fail-closed polarity are unchanged, so a frame settling in any other evidence-free shape trips just the same, and the fixture proves the detector present without rebuilding the open enumeration round 2 refused. Closes BL-148, archived in this same PR. **Audit outcome: pass.**
